# CalcBJ math recheck — 6/7 November 2026

Version checked: v0.1.3 (`main` = `cd7ff40`), all four implementations: the C# engine and Windows app (`src/`), the Rust
engine (`web/engine`), the WebAssembly layer (`web/engine-wasm`) and the web/desktop/Android app (`web/app`).
Nothing in the repository was changed; every test program lives in a scratch folder.

## Update 7 November: the three majors are fixed (not yet committed)

- **M1:** basic strategy is now worked out with exact card removal. Every cell listed below now gives the exact and
  published play. Per-count indices get the exact-removal correction carried over from the full shoe. Against the exact
  reference on the same count model:
  - 115 of 120 hard and soft index bins agree (82 before);
  - 26 of 36 pair-split indices agree (in 6 decks, 10 of 12, against 4 before; for example 7,7 v 8 now splits at +3,
    where it was +8).
- **M2:** preset ramps map each count row by its typical value. Wong Halves now bets 2/4/…/12 at TC +1…+6, in every
  rounding mode.
- **M3:** 16 v T with surrender is now "surrender, else stand". The 1D H17 DAS LS game shows a +0.0039% player edge.
- **Checks:**
  - All suites pass: fixtures regenerated and C# and Rust bit-identical, simulation against analysis in 11 games, and
    UiTest.
  - New regression tests in `web/engine/tests/basic.rs`, the WASM tests and EngineTest `ca`.
- **Speed:** the exact work makes a rule change on the web Game page take 0.14–0.34 s (before 0.08–0.33 s). Repeat
  refreshes are now instant, because recent basic strategies are cached.

## Update 9 November: the minors and nits are fixed too (not yet committed)

Of the 24 minor findings and 13 nits below, 35 are fixed in every implementation they concern. Two stay as they are, on purpose
(the second half of m24 and n11), and a new test found two leftovers of the same size as n11 (n14, n15). The findings list below
is left as it was written, so this table says what happens now.

**Left as they are:**

- **m24, second half (decision depth).** The index generator still spreads decisions evenly over the cards seen up to the nominal
  penetration. The Monte-Carlo test of a depth model was mixed (better in one game, worse in the other), and a proper model needs
  the number of spots as an input of the strategy.
- **n11.** A pair that is neither split nor surrendered on its own follows the total's surrender flag.
- **n14 (new).** With three decks and surrender, 16 v 9 is surrendered where hitting is better by 0.00015 per hand (0.00007% of
  expectation): the cell's average still counts the pair of eights, which is split.
- **n15 (new).** With two decks and DAS, soft 17 v 2 is hit where doubling is better in total by up to 0.00006% of expectation:
  the hands made by splitting sixes use the cell too.

  n14 and n15 are everything the new "best" test found in 600 rule sets (1–8 decks × H17/S17 × DAS × no/late/early surrender ×
  2 or 4 split hands × the three hole-card rules, and 24 games with the other rules): about a million changed decisions tried, 77
  of them gain, all of these two kinds.

All engine changes of this round together move 16 of 121 index lines in 1D H17, 12 of 151 in 1D S17 LS, 7 of 107 in 2D and 1–3
in six and eight decks (Hi-Lo, full index set, −10 to +10). Against v0.1.3 it is 60, 89, 48 and 19–26.

**Monte-Carlo score per 100 rounds on the two one-deck test lines** (higher is better; the "best possible" strategy knows the
exact expectation at each decision):

| Game | v0.1.3 | After the majors | Now | Best possible |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1D H17 noDAS, 3 spots | 0.2521 | 0.2646 | 0.2691 | 0.2865 |
| 1D S17 DAS LS, heads-up | 0.1026 | 0.1046 | 0.1061 | 0.1116 |

**Two things that changed with the fixes:**

- **The betting correlation in surrender games is lower than the reviewer's figure** (KO, 1D S17 DAS LS: 0.968 where the review
  expected 0.973; Hi-Lo, 6D H17 DAS LS: 0.961, was shown as 0.963). The reviewer held the old strategy fixed. The strategy now
  stands on 16 v T with three or more cards, and a hand that stands loses nothing when a 5 leaves the shoe, so the 5 counts for more
  (+0.74% against +0.69% in 1D H17 LS). Without surrender the figures agree with the reviewer's within 0.0004 (Griffin's game:
  Hi-Lo 0.9696 against 0.9697, KO 0.9795 against 0.9797).
- **Speed.** The exact effects of removal take 0.4–0.75 s per set of rules. The Windows app works them out on all cores (0.07–0.13 s
  on 16); the web app in a worker, so the Count page opens at once and the betting correlation follows about a second later. The
  web Game page's "one rule different" list is worked out in a worker too and fills in row by row, so a rule change no longer
  holds the page (on the page's own thread the list would now take 0.4–0.7 s per rule change; it took 0.1–0.3 s in v0.1.3).

**Checks on the final code:**

- Fixtures regenerated; C# and Rust bit-identical: 19 engine tests and 11 WASM tests pass; `cargo fmt` clean; clippy shows the one
  warning v0.1.3 already had.
- EngineTest `ca`, `best`, `idx`, `regress`, `misc`, `sim` and `offtop` pass. `offtop`: 11 games × 2·10⁹ rounds, simulation
  against analysis, all within 1.9 SE and 0.0049%.
- `svelte-check`: 122 files, 0 errors, 0 warnings. UiTest passes.
- Web app logic in Node with mock workers: simulation runs (stop, pause, failures, hung worker; tallies equal the native engine),
  the background clock, app state and scenario files, sweeps.
- Web app tried in the browser, development and production build: every changed page, no console errors.

## Bottom line

- **The core math is right.** Dealer probabilities are exact, and the off-the-top EV of the strategy the engine plays is exact; it
  matches Wizard of Odds within 0.0014% for 1–8 decks. The simulator matches independent simulators. Every risk formula is right:
  EV, SD, DI, SCORE = DI², N0, ROR, bankroll for a target ROR, Kelly unit, certainty equivalent, trip ruin and loss probability. C#,
  Rust and WebAssembly give bit-identical results.
- **Three real errors in the strategy and the betting presets (major):**
  1. A shortcut in the analysis makes a few 1–2 deck basic-strategy cells wrong and moves about half of the 1–2 deck indices by
     one count.
  2. In surrender games, 3+-card hard 16 v T is hit where stand is better.
  3. The preset bet ramps bet one count late for Wong Halves, Silver Fox, Zen and other counts whose units differ from Hi-Lo's.

  The money effect of the first two is small: at most 0.016% per round, in 1 deck. The third makes some counts look up to 30% worse
  than they are.
- 24 minor problems and 13 nits. Two of the minors are small modelling gaps in 1–2 deck index generation; most of the rest are
  input handling, labels and stale-state display in the apps (listed below).

## How it was checked

- **My own runs (section "What I ran myself"):**
  - Rebuilt the C# test tools from the current sources and regenerated the Rust test fixtures.
  - Ran every existing suite: cargo test, EngineTest ca/idx/regress/misc/sim/offtop, svelte-check and UiTest.
  - Re-ran one borderline simulation with new seeds.
  - Read and re-derived the risk formulas and the core of the analysis.
- **12 math areas, 16 independent area reviews** (5 areas were reviewed twice), **674 individual checks**:
  - Each reviewer worked out what the math should be first, then compared the code (C#, Rust, WebAssembly, TypeScript).
  - They wrote independent programs: an exact-fraction dealer calculator, two separate combinatorial analyses, exact split
    solvers, separate simulators, a Python Evaluate, and Monte-Carlo index checks. Their numbers were compared with the engine's.
  - Every check is listed in "Every check, area by area" below, passes included.
- **Verification:** 11 adversarial verifier runs re-checked the main findings and tried to refute them.
- **Completeness critic:** searched the code for math no check had covered; its gaps are listed under "Open items".
- **One decisive follow-up test** of the critic's main gap: whether indices are built at the right shoe depths.

## Findings

Severity: **major** = noticeably wrong numbers or plays in some settings; **minor** = small, edge-case or display/input problem;
**nit** = cosmetic. "Verified" means independent verifiers re-checked it; "reviewer evidence" means the reviewer showed it with a
program, and I checked the code myself where noted.

### Major

**M1. 1–2 deck basic strategy and indices: the analysis keeps the draw odds fixed after the first three cards.** Verified (2 verifiers).
- `CAEngine.Evaluate` (`src/Engine/Combinatorial.cs` FillDP/Dbl/SplitEV, `web/engine/src/ca.rs`) works out hit, double and split
  values with the player's draw odds and the dealer's distribution frozen after the upcard and the first two cards.
- In peek games it also ignores, for the player's draws, that the dealer has no blackjack.
- Exact card removal gives different plays in these cells:
  - 2D S17: 11 v A (engine hit, exact double)
  - 1D S17: A,2 v 4 and A,8 v 6 (double is right)
  - 1D S17 LS: 16 v A (surrender is right)
  - 2D H17: A,7 v 2
  - 8D S17: A,2 v 5 (near tie)
  - Pairs: 6,6 v 7 and 7,7 v 8 in 2D DAS; 9,9 v A in 1D H17; 2,2 v 3 in 1D noDAS (split is right; the split EV also never
    removes a split hand's own second card)
- The published 1D/2D charts agree with the exact plays.
- About half of the common Hi-Lo indices are one count off in 1–2 decks: 11 of 24 checked in 1D S17, 10 of 24 in 2D S17, 3 of 24
  in 6D H17.
- **Money effect:** at most about 0.003% per round. 6–8 decks are essentially unaffected.
- The documentation overstates the accuracy: the guide says "optimal", the Game page "perfect basic strategy", and the code
  comment "up to 0.04%" (it is about 0.05%, and the sign can be negative).
- Fix: compute the two-card cell values with exact removal (the recursion ExactOffTop already has, taking the best play at each
  node). As a first step, condition the draw odds on "no dealer blackjack" for A and T upcards.

**M2. Preset bet ramps bet one count row late for counts whose scale factor is not 1.** Verified (numeric re-simulation).
- `AppState.ApplyPreset` and `RampForRules` (`src/App/AppState.cs`), and the same code in `web/engine-wasm/src/app.rs`, map each
  count row by its lower edge, `TcEquivalent(v)/Factor`, and then round down.
- **Wong Halves** (factor 1.049) gets 1/2/4/6/8/10 units at TC +1..+6 instead of 2/4/6/8/10/12. **Silver Fox** is affected the
  same way. Zen, Omega II, Hi-Opt I/II and Uston APC lag on 2–4 rows. Hi-Lo, Canfield, RPC, KO, Red Seven and Uston SS are not
  affected.
- Example (6D H17 DAS LS, 3 spots, $25 unit, default 1–12 preset):

  | Count | Win rate now | With the fix |
  |---|---|---|
  | Wong Halves | $29.9/h | $43.7/h |
  | Silver Fox | $29.9/h | $41.6/h |
  | Zen | $36.5/h | $40.1/h |

  Halves therefore shows about 24% below Hi-Lo instead of about 11% above it.
- Both apps are affected, and the preset is re-applied whenever the count changes.
- Fix: map each row by its typical value (`Representative`), or by the nearest Hi-Lo row under the count's own rounding. The
  representative alone does not fix "round to nearest".

**M3. Surrender games: 3+-card hard 16 v T is hit where stand is better.** Verified (independent recomputation and a 5-billion-round
simulation, 6 SE).
- Pass 1 of `Evaluate` (`src/Engine/Combinatorial.cs` lines 381–401, `ca.rs`) sets the stand bit of a cell from the two-card
  hands.
- In LS/ES games every two-card hard 16 v T is surrendered, so the bit is used only by 3+-card and split hands, which do better
  standing.
- **Exact gain from "surrender, else stand":**

  | Decks | Gain |
  |---|---|
  | 1 | +0.0160% (ENHC +0.0201%) |
  | 2 | +0.0067% |
  | 4 | +0.0028% |
  | 6 | +0.0015% |
  | 8 | +0.0009% |

- **Visible effect:** in 1D H17 DAS LS the Game page shows "house edge 0.012%", where the corrected strategy has a +0.004% player
  edge (the sign changes). Default 6D H17 DAS LS: 0.530% → 0.528%.
- Every other surrender cell was flipped and checked: only 16 v T gains.
- Fix: choose the fallback from the hands that use it, at the full shoe and in the per-count tables, then regenerate the fixtures.

### Decision depth of index generation (the critic's main gap)

**Real, but minor; tested with a Monte-Carlo.**

`BinMoments` (`src/Engine/Strategy.cs` lines 133–181, `strategy.rs`) assumes decisions are spread evenly over 3…round(N·pen)
seen cards. The simulator, however, caps the cut card, finishes the last round past it and lets the player's seat decide last.
Rebuilding the indices at the depths the simulator really deals changes 43–60 of about 120–150 one-deck index lines; in 2 decks
23 lines change and in 6 decks 3.

A Monte-Carlo with exact EVs at the simulator's real decision points settled which list is right:

| Game | Rebuilt list right | Current list right | Switching to the rebuilt list (per 100 hands, engine simulator end to end) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1D H17 noDAS, 3 spots | 48 of 60 changed lines | 10 | +0.046 ± 0.003 (+7% of what all index plays are worth) |
| 1D S17 DAS LS, heads-up | 9 of 31 | 19 | −0.011 ± 0.002 (−1.5%) |

So this is a real modelling gap with a small money effect whose sign depends on the game. It is worth fixing with a depth model
taken from the real deal, not with the simple reweighting alone.

**Side finding (an extension of M3):** a cell's decision is built from two-card hands only, but 3+-card and split hands use the
same flags. Counting every hand that consults the cell, the one-deck 16 v T crossing is about 0, against +2/+3 in the tables (16 v
9: +4 against +6/+7). That is worth about +0.002 to +0.0045 per 100 hands for 16 v T alone.

### Nits

- n1. "truncateed" in the true-count note (both apps).
- n2. The good/bad trip tiles use z = 1.2816, while the percentile table uses PhiInv(0.9) = 1.28155 ($1 differences).
- n3. TC "round to nearest" with exact deck estimation rounds some exact .5 ties toward zero (floating point; 0–58 of 2·10⁷
  rounds).
- n4. ROR is shown with 1–4 decimals depending on the view. A tiny ROR shows as 0.0%, and "—" and "∞" are mixed in the bankroll
  table.
- n5. The Risk page's ROR chart keeps a stale caption when the ramp stops betting.
- n6. The max-bet sweep uses a stale simulation without a warning.
- n7. Phone app: speed and run time are too high after the app goes to the background (results unaffected).
- n8. `format.ts` rounding differs from the Windows app in tie cases (money(1.005) etc.): "0.000%" vs "+0.000%", "-0".
- n9. Typed tags are rounded twice in the web app (0.125 → 0.15; Windows 0.1).
- n10. The spread ignores hands at the minimum row ("1–24" for 2×1 to 2×12 units).
- n11. A pair cell cannot stop the total's surrender flag (EV < 0.0001%).
- n12. DescribeIndices text for ranges starting above 0 with deviations at both ends; the chart mark for a bounded index range
  reads as open-ended.
- n13. `num.rs` rounds exact 16th-digit ties half-to-even, unlike .NET (no realistic input reaches it).

## Open items (not checked or not settled)

- Pair-split indices per count were not compared with an independent calculation (the off-the-top split EVs were).
- A heads-up player who spreads to 2–3 hands is evaluated from a one-spot simulation, with an assumed correlation of 0.37 (measured
  0.372) and constant rounds per hour. This was not simulated with the hands actually changing.
- The per-count tables near TC −1..0 likely have the same 16 v T fallback issue as M3; the fix should cover them too. *(Covered: the fix of
  7 November shifts the stand decision at every count, and since 9 November the cell decision counts the hands of three or more
  cards directly.)*
- The risk formulas use the normal approximation. Against the real skewed outcome distribution the critic estimates about a 0.1%
  change in the exponent (a shown 5% ROR is about 4.9–5.0%). Not simulated.
- The count restart after an emergency mid-round reshuffle was not checked for unbalanced counts.
- Small web page formulas (Charts, Sim and Results helpers) were compared with C# by reading, not by running.

8. ✔ Re-ran the borderline game (4D S17 DAS RSA HSA OBO, −2.4 SE) with two new seeds: +2.3 SE and −0.8 SE. Pooled −0.0008% ±
   0.0015%, no bias. The peek and ENHC twins are within 1.5 SE.
9. ✔ EngineTest `misc`:
   - ENHC + LS simulation against analysis: 0.6 SE.
   - KO bins and indices are plausible.
   - The optimiser runs: SCORE 33.7 for 1–12, wong-out, half-Kelly with ROR 5.18%.
   - Two hands: variance ratio 2.73, i.e. correlation ≈ 0.37.
   - Trip ruin 0.4632 against lifetime 0.4634.

    spot-checked the I18/Fab 4 plays against the published values, all within one count (the floored-TC convention). The indices
    review compared all 22: 21 match when the crossover is rounded.
11. ✔ `svelte-check`: 121 files, 0 errors, 0 warnings.
12. ✔ UiTest: all checks pass (manual runs, caching, KO/Red Seven, overrides, ramp presets, optimiser, compare, save/load, sweeps,
    odd rules).
13. ✔ Read `Analysis.cs` in full and re-derived it:
    - Per-round EV = Σ f·h·u·μ, and E[W²] = Σ f·u²(h·E[x²] + h(h−1)·E[xy]).
    - Var = E[W²] − EV², the mixture over counts.
    - DI = 1000·EV/SD, SCORE = 10⁶·EV²/Var = DI², N0 = Var/EV².
    - ROR = exp(−2·EV·B/Var) and its inverse for "bankroll for ROR".
    - CE = EV − Var/(2B); Kelly unit = B·EV/E[W²].
    - Trip ruin = the Brownian first-passage formula, with the second term in log space. ProbLoss = Φ(−μ√n/σ).
    - The optimiser's Lagrangian scan maximises EV/√E[W²], which is equivalent to maximising SCORE.

    All correct.
14. ✔ Read `CAEngine.Evaluate` and re-derived it:
    - Insurance EV = 1.5·P(T) − 0.5, taken when P(T) > 1/3.
    - Blackjack paid only when the dealer has none.
    - Peek: the dealer distribution is conditioned on no blackjack, and every action loses only the original bet to one.
    - ENHC: doubles and splits are lost too. OBO is the same as peek.
    - LS = −P(BJ) − 0.5·(1 − P(BJ)), ES = −0.5.
    - The pass-1/2/3 flag logic.

    The fixed draw odds are as documented (they are M1). The pair cell not being able to stop the total's surrender flag is n11.
15. ✔ Read `BinMoments`, `TcEquivalent`, `ShiftedShoe` and the I18/Fab 4 cell lists:
    - The seen-card tag sum uses the finite-population variance k·σ²·(N−k)/(N−1).
    - KO's pivot +4 corresponds to TC +4 at every depth.
    - The composition shift equals the least-squares slope on the tag sum.
    - The I18 and Fab 4 cell lists match the published plays.
16. ✔ Confirmed directly in the code: M2 (both apps), m7, m15, m16, m17, m18, m20 (cache key and the unvalidated `AppState.Load`),
    n1, n2, n3, n10.

## Every check, area by area

16 area reviews of 12 areas, 674 individual checks, as each reviewer reported them. ✔ pass · ✘ fail · ⚠ concern · · not checked. Each line: what was checked — how — the evidence. "fail" and "concern" lines are the evidence behind the findings above (some were later corrected by verifiers).

### 1. Dealer probabilities — review 1 of 2 (independent)

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: Dealer probabilities (DealerDag.cs / dealer.rs, every use of the dealer distribution in Combinatorial.cs / ca.rs including per-deal removal, peek conditioning, ENHC/OBO and the ExactOffTop raw-dealer cache, plus the simulator dealer loop in Simulator.cs / sim.rs)_

I checked the dealer-probability code in both engines (C# and Rust) against exact first-principles calculations and simulations. With real card counts the dealer outcome probabilities are exact. Over 480 shoes (1 to 8 decks, depleted, tiny and random shoes, S17 and H17, every upcard), the engine matches an independent exact-fraction calculator to within 2.2e-15. The no-blackjack conditioning matches to within 8.7e-16, and the C# and Rust results are bit-identical. The peek, ENHC and OBO handling in the exact off-the-top EV matches independent calculations that deal the hole card first, to 12 decimals. Both simulators' dealer loops agree with exact fixed-strategy EVs, all within 2 SE once a 2.4 SE run was re-run (two more runs, pooled +0.9 SE). I found two minor defects. (1) On the fractional card counts used when generating indices, the dealer probabilities add up to more than 1, by up to 0.37% in 1-deck games. This changes no index at the default ranges, but shifts some displayed per-count EVs by up to 0.12% (up to 0.9% when the index range is widened). (2) In the Windows (C#) app only, a hand-edited scenario file with 16 or more decks makes cached dealer results from different games collide, and house edges come out badly wrong.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- Fractional compositions have no single 'exact' dealer distribution. Renormalising per draw is the minimal consistent fix, but whoever owns index generation may prefer another treatment of ranks with fewer than one expected card (e.g. clamping in Regress). Either way, 1-2 deck per-bin fixtures will change slightly.
- OBO semantics: the CA (peek-equivalent: busts pay -bet*(1-P(BJ)), then -pBJ once) and the simulator (dealer BJ gives -20 regardless of earlier busts) both refund a busted double or split hand when the no-hole-card dealer later turns up a blackjack under ENHC_OBO. They agree with each other, but many real OBO tables collect busted hands at once. Basic-strategy doubles cannot bust, so only split hands such as 8,8 v T/A matter; my rough estimate of the effect is about 0.01% or less, not computed. Which interpretation is intended is a rules question.
- Not my area, noticed in passing: in 1-deck Hi-Lo games BinMoments gives alternating unseen-card counts for even and odd TC bins (about 26 vs 41-45 cards, from the half-deck estimate). This makes some per-bin decisions alternate, e.g. 3,3 v 5 split/no-split by bin between TC -12 and -6. The index-generation reviewer may want to confirm this is intended.
- The Rust engine crate itself (used by tests, bench and native builds) does not validate decks; only the WASM wrapper does. No current app path passes decks > 8 to the Rust engine.

### 1. Dealer probabilities — review 2 of 2 (independent)

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: Dealer probabilities (key: dealer) - DealerDag.cs / dealer.rs, every dealer-distribution producer/consumer in Combinatorial.cs / ca.rs (per-deal distributions, peek conditioning, ENHC/OBO, ExactOffTop raw dealer cache), simulator dealer loop (Simulator.cs / sim.rs)_

The exact dealer math is correct in both engines. An ordered-sequence brute force with exact fractions matches DealerDag (C#) and dealer_outcomes (Rust) to <=2.2e-15 on 340 cases, and C# and Rust are bit-identical. The cases cover 1/2/6/8 decks, depleted, zero-rank, ace-heavy, small-card, tiny and random shoes, S17/H17 and all upcards, with and without the no-BJ condition. The infinite-deck and single-deck bust tables are reproduced. The DAG equals the independently enumerated reachable multisets, the 4-bit and 5-bit keys never overflow, and ExactOffTop's peek/ENHC treatment is exact (independently reproduced to 13 digits). The simulator's dealer loop agrees with the exact stopping rule on all 124,994 sequences. Two major defects are in how dealer probabilities are used. First, CA Evaluate conditions the dealer on "no blackjack" in peek/OBO games but not the player's own draw odds. That makes 1-deck basic strategy exact-suboptimal at 16 v A (S17 LS) and 9,9 v A (H17), and shifts 7-11 Hi-Lo indices vs A/T by a step in 1-2 deck games. Second, DealerDag.Evaluate is not normalised for the count model's fractional compositions: probabilities sum to up to 1.0059 in single deck, some single-deck indices move by a step, and per-count cell EVs move by up to 0.16% (Hi-Lo) or 1.16% (KO). Two minor issues remain. Evaluate's fixed dealer distribution and draw odds leave a few further 1-2 deck basic-strategy cells exact-suboptimal (e.g. 2D S17 11 v A shown as H). The dealer-cache key's game field breaks for decks >= 16, which a hand-edited .bjev can reach in the Windows app.

1. ✔ **DealerDag.cs read line by line: outcome codes 0..4=17..21, 5=bust, 6=BJ; BJ only for k==1 with A up/T hole or T up/A hole; ace counted as 11 only when h+10<=21; H17 hit-soft-17 test (best==17 && ace && h==7); hole card always drawn (state k=0 draws); terminal states get no children** — code reading (src/Engine/DealerDag.cs:44-79). Logic matches blackjack dealer rules; confirmed numerically by the brute-force items below
2. ✔ **Rust dealer.rs is a line-by-line port of DealerDag.cs (build, evaluate thresholds 1e-12, cache per H17, up-1 indexing)** — code reading + executable comparison (rsdump crate vs DealerDump.exe on the same 340+30 cases). 0 bit differences between C# and Rust over 340 integer cases, 10 fractional/near-infinite cases and 20 infinite cases (cs_out.txt vs rs_out.txt, cs_frac/rs_frac, cs_inf/rs_inf)

'exact dealer probabilities with card removal' for index analysis is only true for integer shoes; with fractional count-model compositions they sum above 1 (covered by the fractional-composition finding)

Open questions the reviewer left:
- The count model plugs the expected (fractional) composition into the nonlinear CA. Against an exact mixture of integer shoes with the same mean, stand EVs differ by 0.3-0.5% in single deck whether or not the dealer distribution is normalised. That plug-in error may matter more for single-deck index accuracy than the normalisation defect itself; it belongs to the index-generation reviewer.
- The flip scans rely on ExactOffTop being exact. I verified that independently only for hit-once/surrender paths (peek and ENHC, to 1e-16), not for multi-hit or split paths.
- The remaining exact-suboptimal split cells in 2D (6,6 v 7, 7,7 v 8 with DAS) come from SplitEV's fixed-odds approximation, not the dealer distribution. Hand them to the CA/split reviewer.
- OBO semantics: both the CA (peek-equivalent) and the simulator charge exactly one unit when the dealer has blackjack, even if a split hand had already busted. They agree with each other; whether casinos settle busted doubled/split hands that way under OBO is a rules question, not a dealer-probability one.
- The published single-deck and infinite-deck tables used for comparison were quoted from memory; my computed values match them to all digits I remembered.

### 2. Combinatorial analysis: stand, hit, double, surrender, insurance, payouts — review 1 of 2 (independent)

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: ca-core: combinatorial analysis (stand, hit, double, surrender, insurance, blackjack payouts, hole-card rules, flag passes, PlayerEV) in src/Engine/Combinatorial.cs, Types.cs, web/engine/src/ca.rs, types.rs and the per-cell EV consumers_

I wrote my own total-dependent CA in Rust. It computes the dealer by ordered recursion over draw sequences, not the engine's DAG. It has two models: (a) the engine's documented model, with draw odds fixed after the first three cards; (b) exact removal, where every player card comes out of the shoe for later draws and for the dealer, and the dealer's no-blackjack information is used exactly. A separate Python program deals the hole card explicitly and agrees with (b) to 12 decimals. Model (a) reproduces the C# and Rust engines to within 7.7e-15 on every cell EV, all 540 flags, PlayerEV and InsuranceEV, across 26 rule sets and 4 custom shoes. C# and Rust are identical to the last bit. My exact evaluation of the engine's own flags matches ExactOffTop to within 3e-16. Stand, surrender, insurance, blackjack payouts, the hole-card rules (peek, ENHC, OBO), the double rules and the three flag passes are all correct. The one material problem is the fixed-draw-odds approximation, which is implemented exactly as documented. Its stated effect is only "PlayerEV up to 0.04% high in one deck", but it also changes decisions: 6 basic-strategy plays that matter in common 1D, 2D and 8D S17/H17 games are wrong (two more A,A double cells are moot because aces are split), and Hi-Lo indices move by one count bin for 25–45% of the checked plays in 1D and 2D (12% in 6D). There is also a minor display issue: the chart's 'EV per unit' values for dealer A and T include the hands lost to a dealer blackjack.

1. ✔ **C# vs Rust parity, full shoe, 26 rule sets (1/2/4/6/8D, S17/H17, peek/ENHC/OBO, no/LS/ES, AnyTwo/D9-11/D10-11, 3:2/6:5/1:1/2:1, insurance on/off, splits 1/2/4)** — Executable: CaDump.cs (compiled with engine sources) vs Rust crate calcbj-engine in scratch crate cacheck. No pair is ever surrendered only through the total cell's flag

Hi-Lo BC 0.966 vs 0.969 (1D), 0.967 vs 0.970 (6D)
35. ✔ **Strategy chart tooltip EVs (C# EvText '+0.000;-0.000', Svelte toFixed(3) with sign, NaN/null skipped)** — Code reading. StrategyChart.cs:97-110, StrategyChart.svelte:92-104; formats agree
36. ✘ **Meaning of the tooltip EVs for upcards A and T in peek games** — Engine dump plus conversion to EV after the peek. 6D H17 11 v A shows hit -0.237, double -0.230, surrender -0.655. After the peek these are +0.107, +0.117 and -0.500. 20 v A stand shows +0.114 vs +0.600 after the peek (minor finding)
37. ⚠ **Upcard rank absent from the shoe (pu = 0, or a fractional count < 1)** — Custom shoes through C#/Rust. Npop = N-1 although no card was removed: 1D without aces gives InsuranceEV 0.0106 and Insure=true, and the default flags for that upcard come from an inconsistent card count. PlayerEV unaffected (weight 0). App inputs (full shoe, full minus one card, count model on a full shoe) never reach this path

Open questions the reviewer left:
- Evaluate with a shoe where the upcard's rank is absent or below 1 (pu = 0 or fractional) subtracts a card that is not there (Npop = N-1). InsuranceEV, Insure and that upcard's default flags then come from an inconsistent count (1D without aces: InsuranceEV +0.0106, Insure=true). Current app inputs never reach this path; should it be guarded for future callers?
- The simulator stores the blackjack payout in 1/20 units (bj20 = Round(BlackjackPays*20)) while the CA uses the exact value. The UI only offers multiples of 0.05, but a scenario or .bjev file with e.g. 1.333 would make CA (1.333) and simulation (1.35) disagree. Is the payout validated on load? (simulator/scenario area)
- The Count page EORs come from Evaluate's approximate PlayerEV, re-optimised per composition. 1D EOR(5) is 0.681% vs 0.628% exact for the same strategies, and the Hi-Lo betting correlation moves by about 0.003. Left to the count-quality reviewer to decide whether that matters for the displayed BC/PE.
- Late surrender under ENHC/OBO is modelled as losing the whole bet to a dealer blackjack that arrives later (documented in Types.cs). Casinos differ; a product decision, not a math error.

### 2. Combinatorial analysis: stand, hit, double, surrender, insurance, payouts — review 2 of 2 (independent)

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: ca-core: Combinatorial analysis (stand, hit, double, surrender, insurance, blackjack payouts), C# Combinatorial.cs / Types.cs, Rust ca.rs / types.rs / dealer.rs, WASM chart/houseEdge consumers, Svelte StrategyChart/GamePage_

I compared C# Evaluate and Rust evaluate_with against two independent reimplementations. One is a Rust reference with an ordered-recursion dealer, run on 38 rule sets and 9 depleted shoes. The other uses exact fractions in Python. I also ran exact card-removal analyses in two formulations, and targeted shoe simulations of 9.6 billion rounds in total. The engine computes its documented model correctly: cell EVs agree to 8e-15 and to 9e-16 with exact fractions. C# and Rust give bit-identical results, and stand, insurance, surrender, payout, hole-card rules and the three passes are all right. ExactOffTop's non-split paths agree with my independent exact EV of the same strategy to 3e-16. The problem is the model itself. The player's draw odds stay fixed after the first three cards, and in peek games they ignore that the hole card has been shown not to make a blackjack. In 1-2 decks this moves decision boundaries by 0.5-1.2 true count (TC). As a result, 5-10 of 16 checked Hi-Lo index plays are off by one, as the simulations confirm. A few well-known 1D/2D basic-strategy cells come out wrong (EV cost ≤0.003%), and the per-cell EVs shown in the chart are off by up to 0.02 in 1D. 6-8 decks are essentially unaffected (shifts ≤0.3 TC). There are also small issues in documentation, pair-cell semantics and payout rounding.

1. ✔ **Line-by-line comparison of C# Evaluate (FillStand, FillDP, Dbl, peek conditioning, surrender, passes 1-3, insurance) with Rust evaluate_with** — code reading. Combinatorial.cs:101-159,252-448 vs ca.rs:226-288,338-604: same operations in the same order. The Rust dealer() helper folds the C# peek/ENHC branch together; the arithmetic is the same.
2. ✔ **Constructor and FullShoe: dealer DAGs chosen by H17, mass buffer size, rules cloned, 4D/16D shoe** — code reading. Combinatorial.cs:81-99, ca.rs:168-173,192-224, DealerDag.cs:25-94 (BFS multiset DAG, natural only on the first draw, hits soft 17 under H17). EngineTest dag counts: dag1=1575 … dag10=208.
3. ✔ **C# vs Rust bit-identity, full shoe, 4 rule sets (6D H17 LS SP4; 6D S17 noSurr; 8D S17 ENHC ES SP3; 1D S17 OBO LS SP4)** — independent dumps: C# G17Dump.exe (G17) and Rust enginedump (round-trip {:?}), compared with py/cmp_g17.py. 0 of 2700 CellEV values differ, PlayerEV and ExactOffTop bit-identical in all 4 rule sets (run2/g17_cs.txt vs g17_rust.txt).
4. ✔ **C# vs Rust on rule paths and shoes outside the fixture tests (38 rule sets incl. 1D ENHC/OBO/ES, 3D/5D/7D, 1:1, 2:1, 7:5, D9-11 noIns, plus 10 depleted/fractional shoes)** — CaDump.exe ('R' format) vs calcbj-engine in the cacheck crate. Flags, NaN pattern, Insure identical everywhere. Largest |cellEV| difference 1.11e-16, only in 6D sets: this comes from .NET Framework's non-round-tripping 'R' format, since the G17 dump shows 0 difference.
5. ✔ **Independent Rust reference of the documented model vs the engine's CellEV array (all cells, all 5 options)** — independent reimplementation (cacheck/main.rs model_vals + reference): ordered-recursion dealer, integer 'ways/pairs' weights, own passes 1-3; run2/model_run.log. 38 rule sets: largest |CellEV diff| 7.66e-15, flag differences 0, PlayerEV diff ≤4.2e-16. 9 integer depleted shoes: ≤6.55e-15, flags identical (run2/shoes.log).
6. ✔ **Exact-fraction check of the documented model, 1D S17 LS: hard 16 v T, 11 v A, 12 v 3, 16 v A, soft 19 v 6, soft 13 v 4 (S/H/D/R)** — independent Python with fractions.Fraction (py/frac_model.py) vs engine CellEV. Largest |diff| 8.88e-16. Example 16 v T: S -0.574139667360230, H -0.546859144221088, D -1.012085635380952, R -0.540816326530612, identical to the engine to the 16th digit.

Python explicit-hole-card check agrees (1D 16 v A S -0.759322114940). FillStand: 'below' = bust - BJ - P(17..21); t>d wins, t<d loses, equal pushes.

44. ⚠ **Documentation and guide claims about the CA** — code/doc reading vs measurements above. Combinatorial.cs:661-663 / ca.rs:819-821 'up to 0.04%' (measured 0.047%). README.md:43-44 'generated decision at every count but one'. GuidePage.svelte:46-48 / GuidePage.cs:29-31 'optimal hitting, doubling'.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- At a count, Evaluate weights the two-card hands of a cell by the full-shoe composition ('deals are weighted by the shoe'), not by their probability at that count (e.g. 6,6 vs T,2 inside hard 12 at high counts). I did not quantify this; it may explain 13 v 2 (1D S17/H17) and 12 v 4 (1D H17), where the simulation disagrees with both the engine and the exact-removal analysis. This belongs to the index-generation reviewer.
- Hard cells average over pairs that are normally split (8,8 in hard 16, 6,6 in hard 12, …) and leave out multi-card hands. This is part of the accepted total-dependent design, but it affects indices such as 16 v T; I did not quantify it.
- My targeted simulator plays the 'hit' option after the first card with a fixed basic-style policy, not the optimal one. I estimate that biases the measured margins by ≤0.001 (e.g. 14 v T at +3 hits a later 16 instead of standing), which changes none of the sign calls listed.
- Published 1D/2D basic-strategy charts (2-deck: double 11 v A; 1-deck S17: double A,2 v 4 and A,8 v 6) are quoted from memory; I checked these decisions against my own exact calculations only, not against the published sources.
- This scratch folder already contained a Rust reference crate and logs from an earlier run of this same review (cacheck, holecheck.py, scan_out.txt). I reviewed that code and re-ran its analyses from the current sources; the results reproduced. The scan_out.txt flip list and EV-gain figures come from that earlier run; I re-confirmed the flips on the 38-rule-set reference run.

### 3. Pair splitting

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: Pair splitting EV (Evaluate split approximation and ExactOffTop SplitExact), area key: splits_

I wrote my own exact pair-split solver in Python. It removes every drawn card from the shoe for all split hands and plays them in the simulator's order using the engine's flags. Fraction arithmetic and an explicit hole-card-first enumeration back it up. It agrees with the previous attempt's Rust solver to 1.5e-15 on 30 cell runs, so I used that Rust solver, re-run, for complete rule sets. With split to 2 hands, SplitExact is exact to 4.6e-15 on all 600 pair cells I forced (6 rule sets covering 1 and 2 decks, peek/ENHC/OBO, DAS, HSA, double on 9-11 or 10-11 only, late and early surrender); I also prove it must be exact there. With split to 3 or 4 hands, the level approximation moves the 1-deck off-the-top EV by only +2.1e-6 to +4.7e-6, well inside the documented 0.002%. C# and Rust are bit-identical on 40 rule sets, and the C# and Rust simulators play splits the same way SplitExact assumes. The one real split defect sits in Evaluate's split EV, which sets the strategy flags and the indices. It never removes a split hand's own second card, so single-cell errors reach 2.5-3.6% in 1 deck. Because of that, 2,2 v 3 is not split in 1-deck no-DAS games, although splitting is better by 1.0-1.3%. That pair is not in the confirmed-major list, and the money effect is tiny (0.0004%). The only other issue is a nit: hand counts of 5 to 8 are accepted from files and the WASM API, and then the simulator and the analysis use different limits.

1. ✔ **Reused previous attempts' work** — Read the v2 scripts and outputs (splitx2.rs exact/level/fixed solver, SplitProbe.cs, ParityCs.cs/parity.rs). Re-ran everything reported here into v3/; v2 numbers were only reused after re-running them.. Recompiled SplitProbe.exe and ParityCs.exe from the current src/Engine (repo at cd7ff40, unchanged). cargo build --release of v2 splitx/parity was a no-op (up to date). Re-ran splitx2 into v3/out/rx_*.txt (basic-split cells) and ra_*.txt (all 100 pair cells).
2. ✔ **Evaluate ResplitDP structure (number of hands, resplit probability)** — Derived from first principles and checked the recursion in Combinatorial.cs:173-196 by hand. SP2: ResplitDP(2,2,2)=2*[(1-pp)x+pp*y]=2*sum_r p_r v_r, which is correct. SP3: the expected number of hands from the DP is 2+2pp-pp^2, which equals 2 + P(resplit) = 2 + 1-(1-pp)^2, also correct. A pair card at the maximum number of hands is played as the total (y = SplitHandValue(a,a)). Aces without RSA use maxHands=2.
3. ✔ **Evaluate SplitHandValue rules (split aces, DAS, double restrictions, 21 not blackjack)** — Code reading: C# Combinatorial.cs:161-171 and Rust ca.rs:290-303. Without HSA, split aces stand on one card at sev[best] (A+T = sev[21], paid as 21). With HSA they get max(stand, hit) and can never be doubled (a!=1). Doubling requires R.DAS && R.CanDoubleTotal(h,soft). Surrender is never offered on a split hand.
4. ✔ **SplitExact / SplitHand / SplitDP / Play / Draw implement the documented level model** — Code reading: C# Combinatorial.cs:474-656 and Rust ca.rs:608-815. The v2 independent re-implementation ('level') was re-run against the engine.. Hand values at level j use 2+j pair cards plus the hand's own cards out of the shoe. splitP/X/Y are filled per level, and y is only needed at the maximum. A missing pair card disables deeper levels. The re-implementation matches SplitExact to <=1.7e-16 on every one of the 100 forced pair cells in 14 rule sets (ra_*/rx_* merges).
5. ✔ **Theory: for SP2 the level model is exactly full card removal** — Proof via optional stopping: f(C)=E[payoff vs a dealer drawing from the top of C] is a martingale under exchangeable sampling, and the other hand's draws form a stopping time. Without resplits, each hand's EV with the other hand's cards left in the shoe equals its EV under full removal. Resplitting breaks this, because a finished hand must have drawn a non-pair card, which conditions the shoe seen by later hands.
6. ✔ **Independent exact brute force (Python, from scratch): two formulations agree** — split_exact.py carries the finished hands as a multiset; split_vec.py carries an 8-vector by linearity (W_t for bust, <=16, 17..21, plus future payoff). Both use full card removal, the simulator's play order and the engine's flags (v3/py).. 7 SP2 cells (1D H17 noDAS) agree to <=1e-17. The SP4 cell 9,9 v 9 agrees exactly (-0.099043463717626054 in both forms).
7. ✔ **Exact fractions vs floats** — split_exact.py --frac (Python Fractions), 1D H17 noDAS SP2. Fraction vs float: A,A v 6 0.75592747854287845, A,A v T 0.14550648486157935, 9,9 v 9 -0.11242439647223289, all within 5.6e-17 of the float values. The engine's SplitExact differs from the exact fractions by +4.4e-16, +2.8e-16 and -8.3e-17.
8. ✔ **Peek handling (lose pBJ once, dealer BJ = 0 in Terminal, bust x(1-pb)) checked against a hole-card-first enumeration** — The --explicit mode conditions on each hole card: BJ costs -1, otherwise the hand is played against the known hole card with that card removed. Explicit minus hole-card-last: 8,8 v T 0.0, 8,8 v A 0.0, A,A v T -5.6e-17 (exactly 0 in Fractions) for 1D H17 noDAS SP2; A,A v A -5.6e-17 (1D H17 DAS SP4 RSA); A,A v A -2.8e-17 (1D S17 noDAS SP4 RSA).

No flips in the other 6 sets.
21. ✔ **Simulator plays splits as SplitExact assumes (C#)** — Code reading: Simulator.cs:504-575 and 617-705. The current hand redraws at once on a pair card and the new hand is queued. Resplit condition: a==b && nh<maxHands && (a!=1 || !split || rsa) plus the pair flag. Split aces without HSA get one card (A+A=12, A+T=21, paid 1:1). Doubling needs !split || (das && a!=1) plus CanDouble. No surrender after a split. Peek: the round ends before any split on dealer BJ. ENHC: -20 x sum of bets. OBO: -20. The lead's offtop suite (established) confirms this in aggregate.
22. ✔ **Rust simulator split conditions** — Code reading: sim.rs:367, 569, 590, 635, 646. Same conditions as C#: max_hands clamp(1,8), resplit loop with rsa, one card on split aces unless hsa, double rule (!split || (das && a != 1)), and the surrender check skips split pairs.
23. ✔ **C#/Rust parity of the split paths** — Re-ran ParityCs.exe (recompiled) and parity.exe on 40 rule sets (1-8 decks, US/ENHC/OBO, DAS, SP1-4, RSA, HSA, D9/D10, none/late/early surrender): bit patterns of ExactOffTop (basic, all pairs forced to split, no split), Evaluate PlayerEV, and the sum of CellEV P. All 34 rule sets with splitting are bit-identical, including the forced run that calls SplitExact on all 100 cells. In the 6 no-split sets only the probe's own NaN placeholder differs (C# 0xfff8... vs Rust 0x7ff8...).
24. ✔ **Rust ca.rs split code vs C# line by line** — Code reading: ca.rs:290-332 and 608-815 against Combinatorial.cs:161-196 and 487-656. Same formulas, memo keys, loop bounds and peek adjustment (dmax mirrors Math.Max).
25. ✔ **splitMemo, handMemo and dealer cache keys** — Code reading plus the numeric corroboration above. The split key ((cards*16+up)*16+rank)*4+level fixes the hand total (sum(cards)-(1+level)*rank), the ace flag and the shoe. cards uses 5 bits per rank (<2^50), and keys stay below 2^60. The dealer key ((cards*16+up)<<5)+xGame, with xGame<=17, covers deck count and H17. Both memos are cleared per ExactOffTop call. No collisions showed up: SE equals exact to 4.6e-15 over 600 forced SP2 cells, and level equals SE in the SP4 sets.
26. ✔ **Code comments' accuracy claims** — Compared with the measurements above. '0.002% or less' for leaving the other hands' cards in: measured <=0.00047%, and exactly 0 for SP2. 'Fixed draw odds up to 0.02% too low with one deck': measured Evaluate split bias -0.019% to -0.021% in the 1D DAS SP3/SP4 sets.
27. ⚠ **Maximum split hands consistent between analysis and simulator** — Code reading and grep. Analysis caps at 4 hands (Combinatorial.cs:184,606; ca.rs:319,762); the simulators cap at 8 (Simulator.cs:282,331; sim.rs:367). WASM accepts 1..8 (engine-wasm lib.rs:113). The .bjev loader does not clamp (scenario.ts:314). Both UIs only offer 1-4 (GamePage.svelte:131-136; GamePage.cs:105,130).
28. ⚠ **UI display of split EVs** — Code reading of StrategyChart.svelte:92-104 and StrategyChart.cs:97-110. The 'EV per unit ... split' tooltip shows Evaluate's CellEV P. Example, 1D H17 noDAS 2,2 v 3: shows split -0.095 and hit -0.078, while the exact values are split -0.070 and hit -0.080. This belongs to finding 1.
29. · **2-deck SP4 SplitExact effect** — Rust exact over the split cells of 2D H17 DAS SP4. The run had not finished within the time budget and I stopped it. 2D SP2 is covered (max 4.6e-15).
30. · **Python exact for 2,2 v 7 SP4** — split_vec.py. Still running after more than 20 minutes; stopped. The value used is the Rust exact (0.0138643), whose solver agrees with Python to 1.5e-15 on 30 other cell runs, including 3,3 v 2 SP4.
31. · **Pair-split index accuracy under the count model** — None (time budget). Not computed. Evaluate's split error affects the count-model path the same way (it belongs to the confirmed major); see the open questions.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- Published charts: I believe single-deck no-DAS charts (S17 and H17) split 2,2 v 3, but I am quoting from memory. Please check against a reference before citing it.
- Pair-split indices in 1-2 decks: Evaluate's split EV is about 2.5% too low at 2,2 v 3 (1D) at count 0. If the split margin moves about 1% per true count, the generated 2,2 v 3 index would be off by more than one count. I did not compute any count-model split EVs.
- 2-deck SP4: the off-the-top effect of SplitExact's level approximation was not measured (run stopped); it should be smaller than the 1-deck +4.6e-6.
- OBO modelling: the analysis and the simulator both refund busted and doubled split bets when the dealer has blackjack (only the original bet is lost), which is the standard 'same EV as peek' reading. Casinos that collect busted bets immediately would differ slightly; that is a rules question, not something I tested.

### 4. Off-the-top EV and house edge by rule set (Game page)

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: offtop_

I checked off-the-top EV and house edge (ExactOffTop / exact_off_top, the WASM "houseEdge" op, the Game page and its rule-effect rows) four ways: a separate exact evaluator on 170 rule sets, a separate simulator plus the engine's simulator on 9 games, exact fractions, and Wizard of Odds tables fetched live. The EV of the strategy the engine chooses is correct. It matches the separate evaluator to 2.5e-14 percentage points, C# and Rust agree bit for bit on 256 games, it is within 0.0014% of WoO's house edges for 1-8 decks, and every simulation lands within 1.65 SE. The main defect (major) is in the strategy itself: in every late or early surrender game, hard 16 v T is "surrender, else hit" where "else stand" is better. The fallback is picked from the two-card hands, which are all surrendered, so hands of 3+ cards hit. This overstates the house edge by 0.016-0.020% with 1 deck, about 0.007-0.009% with 2 decks and about 0.001-0.002% with 6-8 decks. In 1D H17 DAS LS it turns a +0.004% player edge into a 0.012% house edge. Smaller items: the "beatable without counting" text ignores the cut-card effect (minor), and the dealer-cache key breaks with 16+ decks, which only a malformed scenario file can reach (nit).

1. ✔ **ExactOffTop formulas from first principles: peek games draw the hole card last, leave dealer blackjacks out of the outcomes and subtract pBJ once; ENHC takes all bets (terminal subtracts bet*d[6], a bust loses the bet); OBO is equivalent to peek; late surrender = -pBJ-0.5(1-pBJ), early = -0.5; natural pays (1-pBJ)*payout; insurance adds shoe[1]/N*InsuranceEV** — Code reading of Combinatorial.cs:466-731 and ca.rs:608-899 against an exchangeability argument: swapping the hole card to after the player's stopping sequence is a bijection on deal orders, so a strategy that does not look at the hole card gets the same expectation. The derivation holds. The bust term -bet*(1-pb) and Terminal leaving out d[6] in peek games are consistent with that. The Rust port mirrors C# line for line (raw_dealer/terminal/bust/play/draw/split_hand/split_dp/split_exact).
2. ✔ **Engine exact EV vs a separately written exact evaluator (deals the hole card FIRST, its own dealer recursion) on 170 rule sets: 1/2/4/6/8 decks x 34 sets (S17/H17, DAS/noDAS, LS/ES, RSA, RSA+HSA, SP2/SP3/SP4/no split, 6:5, 2:1, ENHC, OBO, ENHC+LS/ES, D9-11, D10-11)** — Independent re-implementation (indep.exe check "" 0, rebuilt from its current sources; output v3/grid_check.txt). Max |engine - independent| = 2.5e-14 percentage points over 170 games, and 1.9e-14 with the same strategy minus splits. The run also asserts that insurance is never taken off the top (no failures).
3. ✔ **C# HouseEdgeOffTop vs Rust house_edge_off_top** — Recompiled cs/GridCs.cs with the current src/Engine sources (csc /optimize+) and compared with indep print, bit for bit (v3/cs_print.txt vs v3/rust_print.txt). 256 games, 256 bit-identical, max |diff| 0.
4. ✔ **WASM op "houseEdge" and "rulesInfo" through the JSON interface** — indep.exe wasm (calls calcbj_wasm with JSON requests). houseEdge 256/256 bit-identical with house_edge_off_top; rulesInfo consistent 768/768; decks 0 and 9 give the error 'the number of decks must be 1 to 8'; maxSplitHands 8 is treated as 4 (-0.005296783540774827, same as default).
5. ✔ **House edge vs published Wizard of Odds table 'Total dependent vs composition dependent basic strategy house edge' (no surrender, no RSA), 1-8 decks x S17/H17 x DAS/noDAS** — Fetched wizardofodds.com/games/blackjack/composition-dependent-benefit/ (not from memory) and ran indep.exe woo (v3/woo.txt). 32 games: max |engine SP4 - WoO TD| = 0.0014% (e.g. 6D S17 DAS 0.4057 vs 0.4062; 8D S17 DAS 0.4324 vs 0.4329; 1D S17 noDAS -0.0015 vs -0.0026; 2D S17 DAS 0.1937 vs 0.1923). With SP3 the gap grows to +0.0075%, so WoO uses split-to-4. In 1-2 decks the engine's house edge is 0.0005-0.0014% higher, which is the direction of the already-confirmed 1-2 deck Evaluate cell flips.
6. ✔ **Deck-count effects monotonic, and 1D S17 noDAS near break-even** — Engine numbers from v3/woo.txt. House edge rises strictly from 1 to 8 decks for all four combinations (H17 noDAS 0.1886, 0.5365, 0.6503, 0.7064, 0.7401, 0.7625, 0.7786, 0.7906). 1D S17 noDAS SP4 player EV +0.0015% (WoO -0.0026% house edge).
7. ✔ **Rule effects (8D S17 DAS SP4 base) vs WoO rule-variations page** — Fetched wizardofodds.com/games/blackjack/rule-variations/; engine deltas from v3/grid_check.txt. Engine vs WoO: H17 -0.2139 vs -0.22; noDAS -0.1416 vs -0.14; LS +0.0759 vs +0.07 (T) +0.00 (A); ES +0.6288 vs +0.24+0.39; RSA +0.0709 vs +0.08; SP3 -0.0080 vs -0.01; no split -0.5705 vs -0.57; D9-11 -0.0941 vs -0.09; D10-11 -0.1878 vs -0.18; ENHC -0.1122 vs -0.11; 6:5 -1.3580 vs -1.39; 2:1 +2.2633 vs +2.27. Only outlier is SP2 -0.0553 vs WoO 'may not resplit -0.10' (see next items).
8. ✔ **Split-to-2 effect (WoO's -0.10 outlier) settled by simulation** — Separate shuffle-every-round simulator (indep/src/mysim.rs, not engine code), 3e9 rounds each on 8 threads, for 8D S17 DAS noSurr SP2 and SP4. SP2: sim -0.4857% +- 0.0021% vs engine -0.4877% (+0.94 SE). SP4: sim -0.4294% +- 0.0021% vs engine -0.4324% (+1.40 SE). Simulated effect -0.0563% +- 0.0030% vs engine -0.0553%. WoO's -0.10% is about 14 SE away.
9. ✔ **Rule effects vs an independent infinite-deck calculator (Python, written separately, no card removal)** — python -I py/infdeck.py (output v3_infdeck.txt). Infinite deck S17 DAS SP4 = -0.5117%. Effects: SP2 -0.0587, SP3 -0.0090, RSA +0.0770, H17 -0.2194, noDAS -0.1408, LS +0.0855, ES +0.6334, ENHC -0.1135, D9-11 -0.0890, D10-11 -0.1754, 6:5 -1.3529, no split -0.5750. All within the expected finite-deck shifts of the 8D engine values.
10. ✔ **Blackjack payout effects are exact** — Exact fractions: effect = (pays - 3/2) * P(player BJ) * (1 - P(dealer BJ | player BJ)), compared with engine grid differences for 1/2/4/6/8 decks x S17/H17 x 6:5/2:1. 20 effects agree to <= 7e-7 percentage points, which is the grid's printed rounding. E.g. 1D 6:5 -1.394773 both; 8D 2:1 +2.263306 vs exact +2.263307; 6D 6:5 -1.359690 both.
11. ✔ **Insurance is never taken off the top, and the insurance EV formula** — Exact fractions plus code reading (Combinatorial.cs:301-306, 729). P(T | A up) = 16D/(52D-1) is at most 0.31373 (1 deck) < 1/3 for D=1..8, so Insure is false. InsuranceEV = 1.5*pT - 0.5 per unit of main bet with a half-unit insurance bet is correct, and averaging it over the player's cards is exact by exchangeability.
12. ✔ **Independent simulation 1D H17 DAS ES SP4 (not covered by EngineTest offtop)** — mysim.rs, 3e9 rounds, 8 threads, seed 6106001. Sim 0.6509% +- 0.0021% vs ExactOffTop 0.6531% (-1.07 SE).
13. ✔ **Independent simulation 6D S17 noDAS noSurr SP3 D9-11** — mysim.rs, 3e9 rounds. Sim -0.6420% +- 0.0020% vs -0.6417% (-0.15 SE).
14. ✔ **Independent simulation 8D S17 DAS LS SP4 ENHC** — mysim.rs, 3e9 rounds. Sim -0.4602% +- 0.0020% vs -0.4622% (+0.99 SE).
15. ✔ **Independent simulation 2D H17 DAS SP4 with blackjack paying 2:1** — mysim.rs, 3e9 rounds. Sim 1.8983% +- 0.0022% vs 1.8973% (+0.47 SE).
16. ✔ **Independent simulation 1D S17 DAS SP4 RSA HSA (split-ace paths)** — mysim.rs, 3e9 rounds. Sim 0.3001% +- 0.0021% vs 0.2996% (+0.21 SE).
17. ✔ **Split approximation (other split hands' cards left in) vs exact splits in the simulator's order** — indep.exe exactpar "1D S17 DAS noSurr SP4 RSA HSA" 8 (exact recursion with all earlier cards removed; 181 s). Exact 0.2992564% vs engine 0.2996271%: engine 0.00037% too high, inside the accepted <= 0.002%.
18. ✔ **The strategy ExactOffTop evaluates is the one the simulator plays (flags and insurance)** — The engine's own simulator (calcbj_engine::simulate, BasicOnly, flat bet, 8 threads) reshuffling every round; code reading of Strategy.cs:240-243 / strategy.rs:199 against HouseEdgeOffTop. 1D H17 DAS ES SP4: +0.6537% +- 0.0025% vs +0.6531%. 8D S17 DAS LS ENHC: -0.4645% +- 0.0025% vs -0.4622% (-0.92 SE). 1D S17 DAS SP4: +0.1438% +- 0.0037% vs +0.1431%. Build uses the same Evaluate(FullShoe) + ExactOffTop, so the Sim/Results 'basic strategy EV' equals the Game page value.
19. ✘ **Hit/stand fallback of surrender cells (hard 16 v T)** — indep.exe flip16 on 73 LS/ES games (engine and independent evaluator) plus the separate simulator with and without the flip (5e9 rounds each). Turning on the stand bit of 16 v T raises the exact EV by +0.016007% (1D LS), +0.020103% (1D LS ENHC), +0.006724% (2D LS), +0.002783% (4D), +0.001512% (6D), +0.000884% (8D); both evaluators give the same numbers. Sims: 0.1582 +- 0.0016% vs 0.1720 +- 0.0016% (exact 0.1587 / 0.1747). See finding 1.
20. ✔ **Sign conventions and percent vs fraction on the Game, Compare and Results pages, C# vs Svelte** — Code reading: GamePage.cs:174-226, GamePage.svelte:22-60 and 173-212, AppState.cs:413, state.svelte.ts:429, ComparePage (.cs:35, .svelte:21), ResultsPage (.cs:242-243, .svelte:314-317), Theme.Pct/SignedPct vs format.ts pct/signedPct. Big number is -EV when EV <= 0 ('house edge', with the signed player expectation underneath), '+EV' otherwise. Effect rows show the variant's player EV and the delta e2-ev with the same +-0.00005 (0.005%) colour threshold. Scenario HouseEdge = -PlayerEV in both apps. The Compare page shows the house edge and ranks by -houseEdge in both. Everything is in fractions, x100 only for display, 3 decimals.
21. ✔ **Rule-effect rows: same list, labels and order in C# and Svelte** — Code reading GamePage.cs:194-214 vs GamePage.svelte:27-59; Rust effects run (v3/effects.txt, 6D H17 noDAS SP4 base -0.763%). Identical row construction. Example deltas: S17 +0.215, 1D +0.574, DAS +0.145, LS +0.088, ES +0.715, D9-11 -0.098, D10-11 -0.181, SP2 -0.033, SP3 -0.005, RSA +0.068, HSA +0.177, 6:5 -1.360, 7:5 -0.453, ENHC -0.110, OBO 0.000. RSA/HSA effects are identical across DAS/LS bases, as expected.
22. ⚠ **Display rounding C# vs TS** — Code reading Theme.cs:256-267 vs format.ts:34-42. Both round to 3 decimals. The only difference is that C# SignedPct prints '+0.000%' for 0 < x < 0.0005% where TS prints '0.000%' (cosmetic). No Game page row with such a delta was found, so it is not reported.
23. ✘ **Game page claim 'Player edge ... this game is beatable without counting' for EV > 0** — Engine simulator (BasicOnly, flat bet) at 75% penetration vs penetration 0, 1 spot. 1D S17 noDAS SP4: off the top +0.0015%, with the cut card -0.1261% +- 0.0036% (1e9 rounds). 1D S17 DAS SP4: +0.1431% off the top vs +0.0169% +- 0.0037%; at penetration 0 the sim gives +0.1438%. See finding 2.
24. ✘ **Dealer-cache and memo keys** — Code reading (bit widths) plus recompiled cs/CacheKey.cs (v3/CacheKey.exe). Hand memo keys fit: 50+4 bits (split: +4+2 = 60). Sharing the dealer cache across peek/ENHC/surrender/payout variants is valid: the raw distribution depends only on decks, H17, upcard and removed cards. But xGame = 2*decks + H17 overflows its 5 bits from 16 decks on: 17 decks fresh -0.393318% vs -0.161916% after a 1-deck game on the same thread. Finding 3 (nit).
25. ✔ **D9-11 / D10-11 doubling rules** — Code reading Types.cs:46-54, types.rs:104-110, the separate simulator and the infinite-deck script. Hard 9-11 / 10-11 only, no soft doubles, the same in C#, Rust and both independent checkers. Effects -0.0941% / -0.1878% (8D).
26. ✔ **OBO vs peek and ENHC semantics** — Code reading plus grid. OBO delta is exactly 0.000000% (peek-equivalent, as the types.rs:46 doc says). ENHC -0.1122% (8D) vs WoO -0.11 and infinite deck -0.1135. ENHC + LS loses the whole bet to a dealer blackjack. Whether OBO should lose busted bets (OBBO) is an open question.
27. ✔ **Guide claim 'House edges match published figures (6D S17 DAS 0.41%, H17 0.62%)'** — Engine values vs the WoO table. Engine 0.4057% / 0.6180% vs WoO 0.4062% / 0.6184%.
28. · **Index tables in LS/ES games (per-count 16 v T stand bit chosen the same way)** — -. Index-generation area. Same mechanism as finding 1, listed under open questions.
29. · **Stand bits of the other surrender and double cells** — -. An earlier attempt's search over all surrender cells' stand bits found only 16 v T worth changing; I re-ran only the 16 v T flip within the budget.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- WoO's rule-variations page lists 'Player may not resplit -0.10%' (8D S17 DAS base). The engine gives -0.0553%, my simulations -0.0563% +- 0.0030% and the independent infinite-deck calculation -0.0587%, so I treat WoO's figure as a different definition or an older calculation, not an engine defect.
- OBO is modelled as exactly peek-equivalent, so busted split and double bets are also refunded on a dealer blackjack (Game page delta 0.000%). WoO lists the common 'OBBO' variant, where busted bets are lost, at -0.03%. If the OBO option is meant for casinos that collect busted bets at once, the shown EV is about 0.03% optimistic. Which rule is intended?
- The per-count tables use the same pass-1 logic, so in LS/ES games the 16 v T stand bit per count bin is chosen from surrendered two-card hands. The 16 v T stand index played on 3+-card hands may be too high there. Not checked; index area.
- An earlier attempt's search over every surrender cell's stand bit found only 16 v T worth changing; within the budget I re-ran only the 16 v T flip.
- C# SignedPct prints '+0.000%' for 0 < x < 0.0005% where the TS signedPct prints '0.000%'. Cosmetic, and no concrete Game page row with such a delta was found.

### 5. Count model and index generation

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: Count model, bin moments and index generation (area key: indices)_

I checked the count model's regression, BinMoments, TcEquivalent, Representative and ShiftedShoe, plus Build (I18/Fab 4 masks, Merge), ApplyOverride, DescribeIndices and the WASM strategy/chart ops, in C#, Rust and the apps. Most checks ran code: exact hypergeometric computations, a recompiled C# check program, identical C#/Rust index dumps for 17 games, and new independent Monte-Carlo runs that compute exact EVs on simulated shoes (6D S17, 6D H17, 1D/2D H17, KO 6D, insurance for 13 count setups). The count model itself is accurate. The regression slope equals the exact least-squares slope to 1e-16, BinMoments matches the exact moments to <=0.003 TC in 6D, and exact play on the model's composition reproduces the Monte-Carlo EV differences per bin within ~0.0004. All 8 tested 6D S17 index plays (including the 5 required: 16vT, 15vT, 12v3, 10vT, insurance) equal the Monte-Carlo index. 21 of the 22 6D S17 I18+Fab 4 values match the published ones when the crossover is rounded; the +1 differences under the default floored TC are the convention. One new minor model flaw: deals are weighted by full-shoe hand frequencies, not their frequencies at the count, which shifts some 1-2 deck indices by one. Two nits in DescribeIndices. The other 1-2 deck and 6D H17 index differences come from the already-confirmed CA shortcuts.

1. ✔ **Regression formula q_r = n p_r (1 + (t_r - m) x), x = (s/n - m)/V, derived from first principles for sampling n of M cards without replacement** — Derivation: indicator covariance Cov(I_i,I_j) = f(δij - 1/M), f = n(M-n)/(M(M-1)); Cov(Q_r,S) = f M p_r (t̄_r - m), Var(S) = f M V_pop; best linear predictor of Q_r on S; compared with Combinatorial.cs:211-241 and ca.rs:53. The finite-population factor cancels and the best linear predictor is exactly n p_r (1 + (t̄_r - m)(S/n - m)/V), with V = Σ p_r[(t̄_r - m)^2 + within-rank tag variance]. The code implements this: mean and V come from the population left after the deal (upcard and both player cards removed), x is clamped to ±0.9/maxDev, and q is capped at popl[r].
2. ✔ **Exact least-squares slope vs model slope (Hi-Lo 1D, Hi-Lo 2D, Red Seven 1D, synthetic red/black 7s and 9s), population with T,T,6 removed** — Exact multivariate-hypergeometric DP over the tag sum (regress_exact.py, re-run → run3/regress_exact.txt). max |slope exact - model| = 5.55e-17 (1D cases) and 1.11e-16 (2D). E[S] and Var[S] match n*m and n*fpc*var exactly (e.g. Hi-Lo 1D Var[S] 9.433569 vs 9.433569).
3. ✔ **Linear model vs exact conditional expectation E[X_r | S = s] (non-linearity)** — Same exact DP, s within ±6..8 of centre. max |E[X|S] - model| = 0.0030 cards (Hi-Lo 1D), 0.0040 (Red Seven 1D), 0.0012 (Hi-Lo 2D), 0.0036 (synthetic) over 50 (rank, s) points each.
4. ✔ **TagVar red/black term** — Code reading (Types.cs:150-163: Tags = black tag, RankTag = average) + the exact slope check above with red tags + EngineTest regress output from the lead. h = (red - black)/2 is the deviation of each colour from the rank average, so h^2 is the within-rank variance for a 50/50 colour mix. Including it makes the Red Seven slope exact (5.55e-17). EngineTest regress: Red Seven 2.2/2.6 SE with the term vs 42.5/19.4 SE without.
5. ✔ **BinMoments seen-sum distribution: mean k*mu, variance k*var1*(N-k)/(N-1)** — Code reading (Strategy.cs:133-181, strategy.rs:323-380) against the hypergeometric variance. The formula is the correct finite-population variance. Scaled tags include red/black codes. Total tags = mu*N. The unseen mean tag is (total - s)/scale/n.
6. ✔ **BinMoments normal approximation vs exact hypergeometric moments (same uniform-depth assumption, simulator binning)** — binmoments_exact.py (exact sums over low/high/neutral counts) vs engine bin_moments via moments.exe; re-run → run3/binmoments_exact.txt. Largest mean-tag difference in TC units: 0.0030 (6D Hi-Lo), 0.0021 (2D Hi-Lo), 0.0748 (1D Hi-Lo, bin 7 at 0.04% frequency; <=0.026 in other bins), 0.020 (KO 6D), 0.049 (KO 2D), 0.115 (KO 1D). Largest unseen-card difference: 0.86 cards.
7. ✔ **Uniform-depth assumption vs actual decision moments in dealt shoes** — MOM lines of the independent shoe MC (mc.exe 6D S17, seed 777, 1 decision per round) vs exact uniform-depth moments. Bin 0: unseen 225.40 (sim) vs 225.48 (exact) vs 225.53 (engine); mean tag -0.006824 vs -0.00699 vs -0.00696. Bin 1: 194.57 vs 196.77; -0.025815 vs -0.02580. Bin 2: 165.41 vs 167.62; -0.044433 vs -0.04443. So the mean tag is within about 0.009 TC and unseen cards within about 2 cards.
8. ✔ **Hole card in the bin (n-1 cards remaining) consistent with the simulator** — Code reading: Strategy.cs:168 / strategy.rs:370 vs Simulator.Bin (cr = nCards - pos after the hole card is dealt). Both use cards remaining after the hole card. The model's Unseen includes the hole card (n). The MC uses the same convention (nr-4).
9. ⚠ **Depth range: k = 3..round(N*pen), and penetration 0** — Code reading vs Rules.CutCard (Types.cs:70-79). Pen 0 gives k = 3 only, which matches a reshuffle every round. BinMoments ignores the cut-card reserve (1D 1 spot: cut 38 vs last 39; 1D 3 spots: cut 35 vs 39) and the first decision at k = 2*spots+1. Expected to matter only for 1D with several spots; not quantified.
10. ✔ **MidDepthDecks = D(2-pen)/2** — Rust misc_checks re-run (run3/misc_checks.txt) + hand formula. 6D at 75% = 3.75. 28 ok, 0 failures.
11. ✔ **Representative per rounding mode** — Derivation (bin intervals) + misc_checks. Floor [v,v+1) gives v+0.5. Truncate gives v±0.5 and 0 for (-1,1). Round (away from zero) gives v. Running count with scale s holds values v..v+(s-1)/s, mean v+(s-1)/(2s); scale 2 gives 3.25 for bin 3 (ok).
12. ✔ **TcEquivalent for balanced and unbalanced counts** — Hand formula (rc - IRC - I*(D - dMid))/dMid vs misc_checks. Hi-Lo is the identity (-3, 0, 2.5, 7). KO 6D (IRC -20): RC -20 → -2.4, -11 → 0, +4 → +4 (pivot), +10 → 5.6. Red Seven 6D: RC 0 → 2.0 (= 7.5/3.75).
13. ✔ **ShiftedShoe formula** — Derivation from the regression with balanced-equivalent TC + misc check + grep for callers. f_r = 1 - T(t_r - m)/K with K = 52*V per deck is the regression at mean tag m - T/52. Hi-Lo +4 gives tens = 16/52*1.1 = 0.3385 and the tag sum -15 = -TC*decks. It is unused: no callers in C#, Rust, WASM or TS.
14. ✔ **Unreached-bin fallback (NaN moments): Unseen = 52*dMid, UnseenTags = total - (rep*dMid - IRC)** — Code reading (Strategy.cs:283-290, strategy.rs build). Consistent with TC = RC/decks at mid depth. Only extreme bins use it (for -10..10 every Hi-Lo bin is reached).
15. ✔ **C# vs Rust index tables on 17 scenarios** — C# StratChecks dump (recompiled from current sources) vs Rust dump.exe; md5 of all DescribeIndices lines. Identical for 6s, 6h, 2h, 2hn, 1h, 1s, 6s_ko, 2h_ko, 1h_ko, 6s_r7, 2h_r7, 1h_r7, 6s_ss, 6s_halves, 6s_round, 6s_trunc, 6s_exact (108-179 index lines each). Basic EV identical (e.g. 6D S17 -0.33304%).
16. ✔ **I18 cell masks vs the published Illustrious 18** — Code reading Strategy.cs:75-94 / strategy.rs:91-109 against Schlesinger's list (from memory). 17 cells + insurance: 16vT S, 15vT S, TTv5 P, TTv6 P, 10vT D, 12v3 S, 12v2 S, 11vA D, 9v2 D, 10vA D, 9v7 D, 16v9 S, 13v2 S, 12v4 S, 12v5 S, 12v6 S, 13v3 S.
17. ✔ **Fab 4 cell masks, and Fab 4 only with surrender** — Code reading + StratChecks. 14vT, 15vT, 15v9, 15vA surrender. 6D S17 without surrender: I18Fab4 equals I18 (1667 cell-bins deviate in both modes; no surrender plays listed).
18. ✔ **Merge semantics: I18 tables equal basic outside the masked flags and the full-index flags inside, at all 201 bins** — StratChecks (C#) on 6D S17 LS, 6D H17 LS, 6D S17 no surrender, 2D H17 LS × {I18, I18Fab4}. 0 mismatches in all 8 runs. Insurance equals the full table. The described plays are exactly the 18 (22 with Fab 4).
19. ✔ **ApplyOverride semantics (AtOrAbove, negative plays, pairs, soft, insurance, fractional index, no-insurance game)** — StratChecks synthetic overrides. Hit 12v2 at <= +2: hit at +2, stand at +3. No split 8,8vT at >= 8: split at +7, not at +8. Insure at >= 2.5: from +3. Double 9v2 at >= 0.7: from +1. Soft 18v2 hit at <= -3. Applies at bins -100 and +100. Ignored when the game has no insurance. Note: a Pair 'Stand' override sets a bit the simulator never reads for pairs (no effect, not listed).
20. ✔ **DescribeIndices text and irregular flag** — StratChecks synthetic patterns on 16vT. 'Stand at +1 or more', 'always', 'from -2 to +4 [irregular]', 'at -5 or less, and at +6 or more [irregular]', gap at +3 flagged. Edge cases with a reversed range or lo > 0 are reported as nits.
21. ✔ **WASM 'strategy' and 'chart' ops** — Code reading web/engine-wasm/src/lib.rs:224-250. strategy calls describe_indices(pt, index_min, index_max) as the C# app does. chart uses flags_at(v) and result_at(v), or basic flags when at = null. The same reversed-range caveat applies (nit F2).
22. ✔ **StrategyConfig.Key / IndexOverride.Key cover everything that changes the table** — Code reading Types.cs. Mode, IndexMin, IndexMax and every override (enabled, kind, total, upcard, play, direction, index). Concatenation without separators can collide only for invalid totals or upcards.
23. ✔ **REQUIRED independent MC, 6D S17 DAS LS Hi-Lo, half-deck estimation, floored: 16vT stand** — mc.exe (own exact dealer/player EVs with removal and no-BJ conditioning on simulated shoes; weighted hypothetical deals per round), seed 777, 400k rounds, 8 threads, 40 s. Bin 0: -0.00037±0.00021 (avg TC 0.352); bin 1: +0.00663±0.00030. MC index +1, engine +1. Crossover 0.40 (MC) vs 0.49 (engine). Without 8,8: bin 0 -0.00068, bin 1 +0.00631, index +1.
24. ✔ **REQUIRED MC: 15vT stand (6D S17)** — Same run. Bin 3: -0.00476±0.00037; bin 4: +0.00225±0.00049. MC +4, engine +4. Crossover 3.94 vs 4.07.
25. ✔ **REQUIRED MC: 12v3 stand (6D S17)** — Same run. Bin 1: -0.00438±0.00030; bin 2: +0.00918±0.00045. MC +2, engine +2. Crossover 1.66 vs 1.53.
26. ✔ **REQUIRED MC: 10vT double (6D S17)** — Same run. Bin 3: -0.00270±0.00029; bin 4: +0.00389±0.00035. MC +4, engine +4. Crossover 3.69 vs 3.75.
27. ✔ **REQUIRED MC: insurance (6D S17)** — Same run (1.5 P(T) - 0.5 for all hands vs ace). Bin 2: -0.00858±0.00041; bin 3: +0.00176±0.00061. MC +3, engine +3. Crossover 3.13 vs 3.01.
28. ✔ **MC: 12v2 stand and 15vA surrender (6D S17), where floored engine differs from published** — Same run. 12v2: bin 3 -0.00204±0.00061, bin 4 +0.00947. MC +4 = engine +4 (published 3). Crossover 3.47 rounds to 3. 15vA surrender vs best of hit/stand: bin 1 -0.00113±0.00025, bin 2 +0.00649. MC +2 = engine +2 (published 1). Crossover 1.48 (engine 1.59).
29. ✔ **Generated 6D S17 I18 + Fab 4 vs published Schlesinger values** — i18_table.py on the Rust dump (engine crossover interpolated against BinMoments' exact TC) → run3/i18_table.txt. Bins shift as expected: Round gives 15vT 5, 11vA 2, insurance 4; Truncate moves negative indices up by one (13v2 0, 12v6 0). Exact decks gives 12v2 3.
31. ✔ **Independent MC, 6D H17 (seeds 4242/4343, 300k rounds): 12v6, 12v3, 16v9, 13v2, 15vA surrender, 10vA** — mc.exe sets h17/h17b vs engine dump run3/cs_6h.txt. 12v6: MC bin -4 -0.0004±0.00096, bin -3 +0.0130, so -3/-4 vs engine stand from -4 (within noise). 12v3: bin 0 -0.0123, bin 1 +0.00114±0.00031, so +1 = engine. 16v9 +5 = engine. 13v2 stand from -1 = engine. 15vA surrender from -1 = engine and the app's H17 row. 10vA +3 = engine and the app's H17 row.

So the true index is -1 (as the app's H17 published row) and the engine says 0. The count model is right; the cause is the confirmed CA no-BJ shortcut, which also reaches 6D H17. 9v7 bin 3: MC -0.00185±0.00066 vs engine +0.00053, so MC +4 vs engine +3; cause not separated.

The engine is off by about 0.002-0.003 from these (CA shortcuts).

36. ✔ **Insurance index vs MC in 13 setups (Hi-Lo 6D/2D/1D, KO 6D/2D/1D, Red Seven 6D/1D, Uston SS 6D, Halves 6D, Hi-Lo Round/Truncate/exact decks 6D)** — mc_ins.exe (800k rounds each; exact 1.5 P(T) - 0.5 per simulated deal, count binned as the simulator does) vs engine per-bin insurance EV. First insure bin equal in 12/13 (6D Hi-Lo 3, KO 6D 2, 1D Hi-Lo 2, KO 1D 3, 2D 3, KO 2D 3, Red Seven 6D 3, Red Seven 1D 0, Uston SS 20, Round 4, Truncate 3, exact 3). Halves 6D bin 3 is a tie (MC -0.00004±0.00039, engine +0.00006). Max per-bin |engine - MC| (bins >= 0.5%): 0.0011 (6D) to 0.013 (KO 1D).
37. ✔ **KO 6D running-count indices vs MC** — COUNT=ko mc.exe ko6 (seed 6161, 300k rounds) vs run3/cs_6s_ko.txt. 16vT: bin -7 -0.00143±0.00052, bin -6 +0.00036±0.00051, so -6/-5 vs engine -5. 15vT: bin 4 -0.00044±0.00054, bin 5 +0.00336, so +5 = engine. 12v3: bin -2 -0.00032±0.00045, so -2/-1 vs engine -2. 10vT +3 = engine. 12v4: hit <= -8 = engine. Insurance +2 = engine.
38. ⚠ **KO published values (Vancura-Fuchs, from memory: insurance +3, key-count plays near -4, pivot +4 for 6D)** — Comparison with engine and MC above. Engine and MC agree with each other (insurance +2, 16vT -5/-6, 15vT +5, 10vT +3). The book's simplified key/pivot matrix differs by 1-2 by design. These are memory values with tolerance, not a defect.
39. ✔ **Red Seven / Uston SS / Halves index generation** — C#=Rust dumps + insurance MC + EngineTest regress (lead). Tables are identical across implementations and insurance indices match the MC. Play indices for these counts were not MC-checked (only insurance).
40. ✔ **Published H17 rows in both apps (11vA -1, 10vA +3, 15vA -1)** — Code reading CountPage.svelte:174-207 / CountPage.cs:402-432 vs 6D H17 MC. MC: 11vA -1, 10vA +3, 15vA surrender -1, all equal to the app rows. Both apps hold identical tables.
41. ✔ **Non-monotone flips** — Engine dumps. For example, 6D S17 '17 v A Surrender from -8 to -6 [irregular]' with 'Hit at -8 or less': hit overtakes surrender at very negative counts, which is physically plausible and correctly flagged.
42. · **Published 1D/2D Hi-Lo tables (Schlesinger/Wong)** — -. No reliable reference available offline. The MC above was used as the reference for 1D/2D instead.
43. · **TTv5/TTv6 split indices** — -. The MC has no split EV. Engine crossovers 4.83/4.46 round to the published 5/4; floored TTv6 is 5.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- The confirmed CA no-BJ shortcut (CONFIRMED major, described as 1-2 deck) also shifts a 6D index. In 6D H17, 11vA double is engine 0 vs MC and exact-on-model -1 (bin -1: engine -0.00243, exact-on-model +0.00038, MC +0.00049±0.00023). The lead may want to widen that finding's scope.
- 6D H17 9v7 double: engine +3, MC +4 (bin 3: MC -0.00185±0.00066 vs engine +0.00053). model_exact has no 9v7 cell, so the cause (CA shortcut vs count model) was not separated; most likely the CA (dealer distribution not updated after the double card).
- Under H17 the 'Add published Hi-Lo I18 + Fab 4' rows keep the S17 values for 12v3 (+2) and 12v6 (-1). The 6D H17 MC gives 12v3 +1 and 12v6 -3/-4, so at TC -2..-3 standing on 12v6 is better by 0.013-0.027. I could not verify from memory whether Schlesinger's H17 table lists different values.
- BinMoments uses round(N*pen) for the last decision and k >= 3. It ignores the Rules.CutCard reserve (1D: cut 38 with 1 spot, 35 with 3 spots, vs 39) and that with several spots the first decision comes at k = 2*spots+1. The effect on 1D multi-spot indices was not quantified.
- Published 1D/2D Hi-Lo index tables were not compared (no reliable reference available offline); the MC was the reference. TT v 5/6 split indices were not MC-verified.

### 6. Count systems, presets, true-count conversion, betting/insurance correlation — review 1 of 2 (independent)

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: Count systems, presets, IRC/true-count conversion and count quality (BC/PE/IC) — area key: counts_

I checked all 13 presets against the published tag sets (tags, red tags, level, balance, IRC, metric) and found no differences. I also re-derived the Hi-Lo conversion helpers (Factor, MetricFor/TcEquivalent, HiLoEquivalent, ramp and index ranges), the simulator's true-count binning and the BC/IC formulas independently. I wrote my own exact CA (no engine code) and used it to recompute effects of removal; its fixed-strategy EV matches the engine's ExactOffTop to ≤1.1e-16 on 8 no-split rule sets. C# and Rust agree exactly on every helper the fixtures don't cover (37,900 checks, BC/IC bit-identical 480/480), and the TS helpers and labels match C#. The one material defect is in the ramp presets: rows are mapped by their lower edge (v/Factor, floored), so for TC counts with Factor ≠ 1 the preset bets come late. Wong Halves (Factor 1.049) and Silver Fox get every bet one TC late: the 1–12 preset gives Halves $29.81/h instead of $43.72/h, which also makes Halves look worse than Hi-Lo ($36.75/h). The other findings are minor or nits: extreme but allowed IRCs crash or break the ramp in both apps; the old-Canfield upgrade keeps a ramp and index range built for the old tags; BC uses re-optimised approximate-CA EoRs (up to 0.006 below the textbook value); the Factor label is wrong for running-count play; exact deck estimation with round-to-nearest misses .5 ties; there is a 'truncateed' typo; typed tags snap differently in the two apps when 3+ decimals are entered; DependsOnDecks ignores an IRC on a balanced TC count.

1. ✔ **Preset tag sets vs published systems (Hi-Lo, Hi-Opt I, Hi-Opt II, Omega II, Zen, Wong Halves, Uston APC, Revere RPC, Canfield Expert, Silver Fox, KO, Red Seven red7=+1/black7=0, Uston SS)** — Typed the published tables from memory into ref_counts.py (exact fractions); check_conv.py compares them with the engine presets dumped by CountCheck.exe (C# Types.cs Presets) ; also read types.rs presets. 13/13 presets identical (tags, red tags, IRC base/per deck, metric). Types.cs:264-278, types.rs:347-369 identical
2. ✔ **Level of each preset** — Independent max|tag| over 20 card codes (Python fractions) vs C# Level() and Rust level(). Hi-Lo 1, Hi-Opt I 1, Hi-Opt II 2, Omega II 2, Zen 2, Halves 1.5, APC 3, RPC 2, Canfield 1, Silver Fox 1, KO 1, Red Seven 1, Uston SS 3; all equal
3. ✔ **Balanced/unbalanced and imbalance per deck** — Independent Σ tag×cards per deck (exact) vs ImbalancePerDeck/IsBalanced (C#, Rust, TS text.ts). 10 balanced; KO +4, Red Seven +2, Uston SS +4; 24 count cases × C#/Rust/TS all equal (texttest.mjs 72 checks, 0 diffs)
4. ✔ **IRC conventions (KO 4−4D, Red Seven −2D, Uston SS −2D)** — Irc(d) for d=1,2,4,6,8 from cs_out.json vs independent formula; published conventions from memory. KO 0/−4/−12/−20/−28; Red Seven −2/−4/−8/−12/−16; Uston SS same as Red Seven

KO: RC +4 ↔ balanced TC +4 for every deck count and penetration; Red Seven: RC 0 ↔ TC +2 everywhere; Uston SS: RC +2D ↔ TC +4 (no deck-independent pivot with −2/deck, consistent with the stated convention)
6. ✔ **TagOfCode / RankTag (red/black codes, average tag)** — Code reading + independent 20-code construction in check_conv.py vs C#; fixture parity. code = rankIndex*2+red, 2 cards per colour per deck (8 for tens) in TagOfCode, ImbalancePerDeck, Factor, the simulator's shoe build (Simulator.cs:356-361) and corr weights

Halves rows +1..+6 get 1,2,4,6,8,10 instead of 2,4,6,8,10,12; WR $29.81 vs $43.72/h, SCORE 16.99 vs 22.82 (major finding)
