40. ✘ **True-count note text** — Node rendering of both templates. 'then truncateed to a whole number' for Truncate (C# CountPage.cs:457, CountPage.svelte:88) — nit
41. ✘ **Canfield Expert upgrade of old saved counts** — UpgradeCheck.exe: AppState with 0.1.0 tags → Save .bjev in scratch → Load. Tags fixed, but ramp −1..11 (2u at +4 … 12u at +9) and index −9..13 kept; fresh Canfield ramp is −3..8 (2u at +1, 12u at +6), index −10..10 — minor finding
42. ✔ **Key() uniqueness for snapped tags** — Reasoning: '0.###' formatting vs grid spacing. Distinct grid values differ by ≥1/60 > 0.001
43. ⚠ **Simulator RC clamp ±4000 scaled units** — Code reading BuildBins (Simulator.cs:234-235). Only ±66.7 tag units at scale 60; see open questions
44. ✔ **Metric notes (RC; unbalanced TC uses IRC)** — Reading vs simulator MetricValue (rc includes IRC). Consistent

Open questions the reviewer left:
- Uston SS IRC: the code uses −2 × decks (as the task states). With +4/deck imbalance this gives no deck-independent pivot (RC +2D ↔ balanced TC +4); I could not confirm the published IRC from a source.
- Factor is a pure SD ratio. Matching on advantage instead would also scale by BC_count/BC_HiLo (Hi-Opt I 0.908/0.963 ≈ −6%). This only affects preset ramps and the Hi-Lo-equivalent ranges; it is a design choice I did not rate.
- BuildBins clamps the scaled running count to ±4000 (Simulator.cs:234-235). At scale 60 (custom counts mixing thirds and 0.05) that is only ±66.7 tag units, which a level-3 8-deck custom count can exceed at mid-shoe (TC ≈ 16). Rounds beyond it go into lower bins. Not simulated; it is in the simulator area.
- In both apps, changing the count (or the decks for RC/unbalanced counts) replaces a custom ramp with the 1–12 preset (ApplyPreset(8) → 3), while the decks sweep carries custom ramps over by Hi-Lo equivalent. Is that intended?
- Two published BCs I quoted from memory (Red Seven 0.98, RPC 0.99) do not match the BC formula with Griffin's EoRs (0.972, 0.984). Most likely my recollection or a different EoR set; the engine agrees with the formula.

### 6. Count systems, presets, true-count conversion, betting/insurance correlation — review 2 of 2 (independent)

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: counts_

I covered all 13 count presets, the IRC and pivot conventions, the true-count conversion (Scale, Snap, EstimateDecks, MetricValue), Factor, HiLoEquivalent, MetricFor, the ramp and index ranges, DependsOnDecks, RampForRules, the presets' bet units, and BC/IC with their effects of removal. I checked these in C#, Rust, WASM and TS. All 13 presets match the published tags, imbalance, level and IRC (KO 4-4D with pivot +4, Red Seven -2D with pivot 0, Uston SS -2D, all running count). C# and Rust gave identical values in all 107,290 compared numbers, and the WASM JSON path matches the direct calls. The BC/IC formulas are right: I recomputed them to 1e-15, they reproduce the published single-deck values with Griffin's effects of removal, and an exact combinatorial analysis I wrote myself confirms the engine's effects of removal (BC within 0.0012 for 6D H17 DAS LS, at most 0.0076 in 1D). No critical or major problems. I found 2 minor issues: DependsOnDecks and the range resets ignore an IRC on balanced true-count systems, and the label '1 Hi-Lo TC = X of this count' is in true-count units even for running-count systems. I also found 3 nits: exact-deck rounding at .5 ties, the web app rounding typed tags and IRCs differently from Windows, and the 'truncateed' typo.

1. ✔ **Hi-Lo preset definition** — Compared the C# and Rust preset with the Wikipedia card-counting table (fetched during review). Tags A..T are -1,+1,+1,+1,+1,+1,0,0,0,-1 (Types.cs:264, types.rs:347). True count, IRC 0, balanced, level 1, Factor 1.000000 (sqrt(40/40)).
2. ✔ **Hi-Opt I preset definition** — Compared with Wikipedia table. Tags 0,0,+1,+1,+1,+1,0,0,0,-1. Balanced, level 1. Factor 0.894427 = sqrt(32/40), which I recomputed.
3. ✔ **Hi-Opt II preset definition** — Compared with Wikipedia table. Tags 0,+1,+1,+2,+2,+1,+1,0,0,-2. Balanced, level 2. Factor 1.673320 = sqrt(112/40).
4. ✔ **Omega II preset definition** — Compared with Wikipedia table. Tags 0,+1,+1,+2,+2,+2,+1,0,-1,-2. Balanced, level 2. Factor 1.788854 = sqrt(128/40).
5. ✔ **Zen Count preset definition** — Compared with Wikipedia table. Tags -1,+1,+1,+2,+2,+2,+1,0,0,-2. Balanced, level 2. Factor 1.788854.
6. ✔ **Wong Halves preset definition** — Compared with Wikipedia table. Tags -1,+.5,+1,+1,+1.5,+1,+.5,0,-.5,-1. Scale 2, balanced. Level shows 1.5 (the 'level 3' in the literature refers to the doubled tags). Factor 1.048809 = sqrt(44/40).
7. ✔ **Uston APC preset definition** — Compared with the published table (from memory; search result confirms BC/PE/IC .91/.69/.90). Tags 0,+1,+2,+2,+3,+2,+2,+1,-1,-3 (no ace side count). Balanced, level 3. Factor 2.529822 = sqrt(256/40).
8. ✔ **Revere RPC preset definition** — Compared with the published table (from memory; search result gives BC/PE/IC .99/.55/.78). Tags -2,+1,+2,+2,+2,+2,+1,0,0,-2. Balanced, level 2. Factor 1.949359 = sqrt(152/40).
9. ✔ **Canfield Expert preset definition (and the 0.1.1 fix)** — Compared with the published table (memory) and ran UpgradeSaved directly (UpgradeTest.exe). Tags 0,0,+1,+1,+1,+1,+1,0,-1,-1, balanced, Factor 1.000. UpgradeSaved turns the old tags (2=+1) into the preset key. It leaves 'Custom', red-tag and current counts alone. TS upgradeSavedCount (state.svelte.ts:141) uses the same conditions (code reading).
10. ✔ **Silver Fox preset definition** — Compared with the published table (search result: BC/PE/IC .96/.53/.69). Tags -1,+1 (2-7),0,-1,-1. Balanced (24-24), level 1. Factor 1.095445 = sqrt(48/40).
11. ✔ **KO preset: tags, imbalance, IRC, metric** — Compared with Vancura/Fuchs (memory) and a web source giving IRC 4-4D. Tags -1,+1 x6 (2-7),0,0,-1. Imbalance +4/deck. IRC 4-4D gives 0/-4/-20/-28 for 1/2/6/8 decks. Running count. Factor 1.045135.
12. ✔ **Red Seven preset: red 7 +1, black 7 0, IRC** — Compared with Snyder's LVA article (fetched): -2 per deck, pivot 0, +2 per deck. Black tags have 7=0 and red tags have 7=+1 (Types.cs:275-276). Imbalance +2, IRC -2D (-12 at 6D), running count, level 1, Factor 1.023756.
13. ✔ **Uston SS preset: tags, imbalance, IRC** — Compared with secondary web sources (start at -2 per deck, running count used directly). Tags -2,+2,+2,+2,+3,+2,+1,0,-1,-2. Imbalance +4 (36-32). IRC -2D, running count, level 3. Factor 2.166174.
14. ✔ **Pivot points implied by the IRC conventions** — Read TcEquivalent from the C# run, 8 rule sets. KO RC +4 gives balanced-equivalent TC 4.0000 on all 8 rule sets (pivot +4). Red Seven RC 0 gives 2.0000 on all. Uston SS RC 0 gives 0.0 to 2.0 (no pivot design, consistent with running-count use).
15. ✔ **ImbalancePerDeck / IsBalanced / Level for all 25 tested counts** — Independent exact-fraction recomputation (corr_check.py) and TS text.ts run in Node. 0 differences against C#. TS imbalancePerDeck/isBalanced matched C# bit-for-bit on 25 counts, including thirds and red tags.
16. ✔ **Factor formula (SD of tags per card relative to Hi-Lo)** — Derivation, plus independent recomputation over 25 counts. Factor = sqrt(sum over 52 cards of (t-m)^2 / 40), with m = imbalance/52 and red/black spread included. Matches the app to 3.3e-16. Red Seven 1.0238, KO 1.0451.
17. ✔ **Balanced-equivalent TC for unbalanced counts (TcEquivalent)** — Derivation from the tag sum of seen cards, then code reading of C# and Rust. TC_eq = (RC - IRC - k(D - dr))/dr is minus the deviation of the unseen tag sum per deck. Evaluated at dMid = D(2-pen)/2, which is the arithmetic mean of decks remaining over the dealt rounds (Strategy.cs:105-108, strategy.rs:119-121).
18. ✔ **MetricFor is the inverse of HiLoEquivalent** — Algebra, plus numbers from the C# run. MetricFor(h)*dMid gives rc = h*F*dMid + IRC + k(D - dMid), and TcEquivalent/F returns h exactly. Examples: KO 6D MetricFor(0) = -11.000 gives HiLoEq(-11) = 0; Hi-Lo MetricFor(h) = h.
19. ✔ **RampRangeFor values** — Hand calculation compared with the C# and Rust outputs. Hi-Lo [-3,8]. KO 6D: -22.76 -> -23 and 20.35 -> 21, giving [-23,21]. Red Seven 1D [-4,4] (-3.17 and 3.87). Uston SS 8D/85% [-33,78], 111 rows, matching the 'about 110' comment. Zen [-6,15].
20. ✔ **IndexRangeFor values** — Hand calculation compared with the C# and Rust outputs. Hi-Lo [-10,10], Hi-Opt I [-9,9], APC [-26,26], KO 6D [-51,29] (covers about ±4.5 SD of the RC at mid-shoe), Uston SS 8D [-100,98] (clamped to BinMin).
21. ✘ **DependsOnDecks and the range-reset conditions** — Code reading, plus MetricFor ranges for a Hi-Lo TC count with IRC (BalIrcTC, BalIrcPerDeckTC). Returns false for balanced TC counts with IRC != 0, yet their ranges depend on decks: 6D ramp [2,14] / index [-5,16] versus 1D [29,40] / [22,42]. See finding 1.
22. ✔ **Ramp presets (RampPresets.UnitsAt vs PRESET_UNITS) and ApplyPreset** — C#/Rust parity run, all 9 presets x 25 counts x 8 rules. Identical tables. Hi-Lo default ramp units are [1,1,1,1,2,4,6,8,10,12,12,12] (matches the wasm unit test). The custom preset falls back to 1-12 in both apps.
23. ✔ **RampForRules (preset and custom ramps across rule sets)** — C#/Rust parity run from 6D to 7 other rule sets, via direct calls and the WASM rampForRules op. 0 differences in min/max/units/hands, including the nearest-Hi-Lo-equivalent mapping of a custom ramp with 1-3 hands.
24. ✔ **C# vs Rust parity of all count helpers** — Compiled the C# app sources with a harness (CountsCheck.exe) and a Rust crate including app.rs (#[path]) on 25 counts x 8 rule sets. 107,290 values bit-identical: scale, imbalance, level, balanced, tagText, key, factor, IRC, metricFor (9 points), ramp and index ranges, HiLoEquivalent (all rows +/-5), 9 preset ramps, 2 RampForRules, BC, IC and the EoR arrays.
25. ✔ **WASM JSON interface vs direct app.rs calls** — calcbj_wasm::call/out_ptr with countInfo, defaultRamp (9 presets), rampForRules (2), indexRange. 0 differences over 25 counts x 8 rule sets.
26. ✔ **Rust count fixture tests** — cargo test --test counts (scratch target dir). 3/3 passed: count_systems, presets_match_the_windows_app, true_count_presets_are_balanced.
27. ✔ **Snap (C#) vs snapTag (TS), as pure functions** — Node ran the real format.ts on 40,001 three-decimal inputs in [-20,20], compared with C# Snap. 0 differences. Every snapped value lies on the thirds or 0.05 grid. Both round ties to even.
28. ⚠ **Tag and IRC entry path, Windows vs web** — The same 40,001 inputs through each UI path; a WinForms NumericUpDown test; Node Math.round. 926 of 40,001 three-decimal inputs snap differently (0 of the two-decimal ones). IRC -0.35 gives -0.4 on Windows and -0.3 on the web. See finding 4.
29. ✔ **Scale() including IRC, up to 60** — C#/Rust run on custom counts. Halves 2, Thirds 3, Mixed60 (0.35, thirds, IRC 0.1) 60. Loops 1..59 then 60 in both languages; the Whole() tolerance is 1e-9 in both.
30. ✔ **Negative-zero tags in Key/TagText** — Snap(-0.001) = -0 in C#, formatted in C# (.NET 4.8) and Rust. Both print '0'. JSON from TS cannot carry -0. Keys stay identical across apps.
31. ⚠ **Simulator true count (MetricValue), exact check** — Exact rational/integer re-implementation (tc_grid_check.py) vs C# over scale {1,2,3,60} x rc [-150,150] x cards 0..416 x every estimation and rounding mode. 6,526,884 cases, 44 mismatches, all with Exact estimation + Round at exact .5 ties (e.g. RC 27 with 216 cards = TC 6.5 lands in bin 6). Floor and Truncate: 0 mismatches. See finding 3.
32. ✔ **EstimateDecks conventions** — Exact recomputation of 4 modes x 417 card counts. 0 mismatches. Quarter/half/full round half away from zero with minimum 1 unit. Exact uses max(cards,6)/52. Rust .round() matches.
33. ✔ **BC/IC formula** — Independent exact-fraction weighted Pearson over 20 card codes (weights 2 and 8 cards per code; insurance +1 / -9/4) on the engine's EoRs. Matches the app to 5.6e-16 on presets and 3.3e-16 on custom counts. Closed form: Hi-Lo IC = 52/sqrt(40x117) = 0.7602.
34. ✔ **Effects of removal: engine vs independent exact CA** — My own C# finite-shoe composition-dependent analysis (MyCA.cs, no engine code), re-optimised and fixed strategy, vs CAEngine EoRs, 6 rule sets with one split. Centred EoR differences at most 0.003%/card at 6D. At 1D: at most 0.015% vs re-optimised and 0.037% vs fixed (ace, H17 DAS LS). BC differences: at most 0.0012 (6D H17 DAS LS), 0.0027 (6D S17), 0.0016 (2D), 0.0024 (8D), 0.0076 (1D H17 DAS LS).
35. ✔ **Self-check of the independent CA** — Fixed-strategy EoRs must sum to about 0 over a deck. Sums +0.0002% (6D/8D), +0.0024% (2D), +0.0075% and +0.011% (1D).
36. ✔ **Engine EoR sum is non-zero** — Compared with my re-optimised EoR sums. Engine +0.99% (1D) and +0.023% (6D), versus my re-optimised +1.34% and +0.045%. This is the strategy re-optimisation effect. A constant shift does not affect a Pearson correlation.
37. ✔ **Engine 1D EoRs vs Griffin's published table** — Compared with Griffin's single-deck EoRs (from memory), centred. Engine centred: A -.611, 2 .384, 3 .445, 4 .559, 5 .716, 6 .437, 7 .287, 8 .017, 9 -.177, T -.514. Griffin: -.61, .38, .44, .55, .69, .46, .28, .00, -.18, -.51. All within 0.03%.
38. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Hi-Lo** — App BC at the 1D Griffin-like rules; my BC with Griffin's EoRs; published value (tolerance ±0.01). App 0.966 (6D default 0.963), Griffin EoRs 0.968, published 0.97. IC 0.760 vs 0.76.
39. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Hi-Opt I** — Same as Hi-Lo. App 0.874 (6D 0.908), Griffin EoRs 0.875, published 0.88. IC 0.850 vs 0.85.
40. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Hi-Opt II** — Same as Hi-Lo. App 0.911 (6D 0.933), Griffin EoRs 0.909, published 0.91. IC 0.909 vs 0.91.
41. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Omega II** — Same as Hi-Lo. App 0.916 (6D 0.944), Griffin EoRs 0.917, published 0.92. IC 0.850 vs 0.85.
42. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Zen** — Same as Hi-Lo. App 0.961 (6D 0.964), Griffin EoRs 0.962, published 0.96. IC 0.850 vs 0.85.
43. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Wong Halves** — Same as Hi-Lo. App 0.992 (6D 0.991), published 0.99. IC 0.725 vs 0.72.
44. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Uston APC** — Same as Hi-Lo. App 0.906 (6D 0.919), Griffin EoRs 0.904, published 0.91. IC 0.901 vs 0.90.
45. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Revere RPC** — Same as Hi-Lo. App 0.982 (6D 0.970), Griffin EoRs 0.984, published 0.99 (within the ±0.01 tolerance). IC 0.780 vs 0.78.
46. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Canfield Expert** — Same as Hi-Lo. App 0.868 (6D 0.893), Griffin EoRs 0.869, published 0.87. IC 0.760 vs 0.76.
47. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Silver Fox** — Same as Hi-Lo. App 0.961 (6D 0.953), published 0.96. IC 0.694 vs 0.69.
48. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: KO** — Same as Hi-Lo; Pearson with the tag mean subtracted. App 0.975 (6D 0.955), Griffin EoRs 0.977, published 0.98. IC 0.783 vs 0.78.
49. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Red Seven (card by card)** — Card-level vs rank-averaged correlation. App, per card: BC 0.970, IC 0.771. Rank-averaged (7 = +0.5) gives Griffin-EoR BC 0.983 and IC 0.780, the published 0.98/0.78. Per card is correct: red and black 7s have the same EoR but different tags.
50. ✔ **BC/IC vs published: Uston SS** — Same as Hi-Lo. App 0.993 (6D 0.992), Griffin EoRs 0.994, published 0.99. IC 0.729 vs 0.73.
51. ✔ **Rule dependence of the BC** — Independent CA at 6D H17 DAS LS vs engine. Default-rule BCs differ from the single-deck tables by up to ±0.03 (KO 0.955, Hi-Opt I 0.908). The independent CA confirms them: KO 0.9560/0.9571 vs engine 0.9562, Hi-Opt I 0.9066/0.9071 vs 0.9075.
52. ✔ **Degenerate and sanity counts** — C#/Rust run. All-zero tags: BC/IC 0 (guarded), Factor 1. Ten count (+1/-2): IC 1.000, as two-valued tags proportional to the insurance EoR must give. Red 9 = 0 variant of Halves raises IC 0.725 -> 0.743.
53. ✔ **Insurance not taken inside the EoR shoes** — Analysis of CAEngine.Evaluate's insurance branch. The largest ten share after an ace upcard and one non-ten removed is 16/50 = 0.32, below 1/3, so EoRs carry no insurance term.
54. ✔ **EoR caching** — Code reading. C# eorCache is keyed on Rules.Key() (CountPage.cs:472-493). Rust uses a thread_local keyed on r.key() (app.rs:122-147).
55. ✘ **Label '1 Hi-Lo TC = X of this count'** — Compared with MetricFor(1) - MetricFor(0) from the C# run. KO 6D label says 1.05; the app's own conversion gives 3.92 RC points. Hi-Lo played by running count: 1 vs 3.75. See finding 2.
56. ⚠ **Count-page notes about TC and RC** — Code reading vs Simulator.MetricValue. Accurate: the RC note says TC settings are ignored (true), and for unbalanced TC counts the IRC is included in the TC (true). The Truncate text reads 'truncateed'. See finding 5.
57. ✔ **Playing efficiency** — grep over C#, Rust, TS and docs. Not computed or shown anywhere, so nothing to verify.
58. ✔ **Running-count lookup clamp at large scale** — Analysis of BuildBins/SimWorker clamps. ±4000 scaled units = ±66.7 count units at scale 60. That is more than 6 SD for realistic level-1.5 counts at 8D. The lookup clamps rc before indexing in both C# and Rust.
59. ✔ **Representative() and card-code layout** — Code reading plus fixtures. RC bins: v + (s-1)/(2s); TC Floor: v + 0.5. The simulator shoe holds 2 black + 2 red per rank per deck (8+8 tens), consistent with the 20-code weights.
60. ✔ **TS count-page flows vs C#** — Code reading. chooseSystem keeps deckEst and rounding. replaceCount names the count after a matching preset key or 'Custom'. setConversion uses the same reset condition as C#. Input limits match: tags ±20, IRC ±200 / ±20 at 1 decimal; WASM validates ±20 and ±10000/±1000.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- Factor converts by equal spread (SD ratio of tags), not equal advantage (SD ratio x BC ratio). At 6D H17 DAS LS the equal-advantage factor would be about 8% larger for Canfield Expert (BC 0.893 vs Hi-Lo 0.963) and about 6% larger for Hi-Opt I. Their default preset ramps therefore raise bets at slightly lower advantage than Hi-Lo's. The code comment says spread is intended, so I did not report it.
- MidDepthDecks is the arithmetic mean of decks remaining (3.75 at 6D/75%). For converting RC points to true counts over the shoe, the harmonic mean (3.25) is arguably better. This only moves default ramps and ranges of running-count and unbalanced counts; no reported result depends on it.
- Red Seven's BC/IC on the count page (0.970/0.771 single deck, 0.957/0.771 at 6D) are lower than the published 0.98/0.78. That is correct by design (card by card), and BCs at the user's rules differ from single-deck tables by up to ±0.03. The page does not say so, which may confuse users who compare with published tables.
- The Uston SS IRC of -2 per deck is confirmed only by secondary web sources (no primary source found). KO's 4-4D comes from one web source plus memory of Vancura/Fuchs. Red Seven's -2D comes from Snyder's LVA article.
- Scenario files edited by hand with tags or IRC off the thirds/0.05/0.1 grid are not snapped on load in either app. Scale() then falls back to 60 and the simulator counts round(x*60)/60, while the index analysis uses the exact tag. I found no way to reach this through the UI.

### 7. Shoe simulator

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: Shoe simulator: shuffling, dealing, counting, play, payouts and tallies (src/Engine/Simulator.cs, web/engine/src/sim.rs, Rules.CutCard/TotalCards, WASM sim_run + TS driver)_

I read Simulator.cs and sim.rs line by line, along with CutCard/TotalCards/Scale, the WASM sim_run/simPrepare and the TS driver. I re-checked the earlier attempts' harness (its sim copy is verbatim identical to the current sim.rs) and re-ran every test I report. The simulator core holds up. An independent Rust simulator (SFC64 generator, full Fisher-Yates, exact integer TCs, own payouts) matches the engine in EV, E[x^2], E[AB], rounds per shoe and TC-bin frequencies on 15 configurations (6.4e8 or 3.2e8 rounds each, all |z| <= 1.8 after reruns). All 32 hand-crafted rounds pay exactly right in both C# and Rust, 200 random C#/Rust parity configurations give identical tallies, and the RNG, shuffle, deck estimate, bin tables, merge and thread handling all pass. There are four findings. (1) Analysis.Bins averages the last two seats even for one-hand rows, although the app says the player sits in the last seat; in 1-2 deck multi-spot index play this understates EV by about 0.007-0.010% per hand and SCORE by about 1-1.6%. (2) TC 'Round' mode rounds exact .5 ties the wrong way because of floating point. (3) A blackjack payout not on the 1/20 grid (only possible from a scenario file) is silently rounded in the simulator. (4) The ±4000 RC table clamp gives wrong bins for exotic custom scale-60 level-3 counts.

1. ✔ **Earlier attempts' harness is still valid** — diff --strip-trailing-cr of the scratch sim_body.rs against web/engine/src/sim.rs minus its 2 header lines; recompiled the C# checks (SimCheck3.exe, Parity3.exe) from the current src/Engine/*.cs. 'copy identical'; csc builds clean; every result below was re-run in this attempt (files r3_*.txt and run3_*.txt)
2. ✔ **xoshiro256** step and output, SplitMix64 seeding** — Code reading against the reference algorithm; C# vs Rust reference dump (5 seeds x 3 workers: state words, 6 raw outputs, 12 NextInt(52)). Simulator.cs:380-397 and sim.rs:267-273,432-443 match the reference; r3_rngref_cs.txt == r3_rngref.txt (15 lines identical)
3. ✔ **Uniform integer method (32-bit halves, high half first, multiply-shift without rejection): size of the bias** — Exact enumeration of outcome probabilities for n = 1..416 (bias mode). Largest relative deviation 9.45e-8 (n=410); total-variation distance at most 2.38e-8 per draw; theoretical bound n/2^32 = 9.7e-8. Negligible.
4. ✔ **Lazy Fisher-Yates correctness and uniformity** — Code reading (j = p + NextInt(n-p), swap, permutation carried over between shoes) plus chi-square on 2 x 2e7 fully dealt one-deck shuffles through the engine's own draw_hidden. First three ranks (999 dof): chi^2 976.2 (z -0.51), second seed 986.9 (z -0.27). Rank at positions 0/1/2/25/26/50/51 (9 dof): all |z| <= 2.4; positions 1 and 2 (z 2.18/2.37) re-run with seed 13579: z 0.43/0.21. Last pair (99 dof): chi^2 110.7 (z 0.83).
5. ⚠ **Per-worker seeds and stream independence** — Code reading (seed + (idx+1)*gamma into SplitMix gives adjacent workers states shifted by one word: 3 of 4 words shared); empirical cross-stream tests. 5 seeds x 4 adjacent pairs x 4e6 outputs: 180 lagged cross-correlations, largest |z| 2.70; NextInt(52) pair chi-square over 20 tests, largest standardised 1.56. No correlation found; a jump() or disjoint SplitMix positions would remove the overlap by design (open question).
6. ✔ **Seeds per run in the apps (so merged runs are independent)** — Code reading: MainForm.cs:380, SweepPage.cs:167, simulation.svelte.ts:69-71,206-208, engine-wasm lib.rs:305-316. C# uses clock ticks; TS uses crypto.getRandomValues for every run; worker i gets worker_seed(seed, i), the same as the C# threads. A worker is only replaced when its run ends, so no stream is replayed into a tally.
7. ✔ **Cut card / penetration with several spots; when a reshuffle happens** — Code reading of CutCard (C# Types.cs:70-79, Rust types.rs:127-141) and PlayRound; rounds per shoe compared with the independent simulator. Same banker's rounding of n*pen, reserve min(n/3, 8+6*spots), minimum 10 in both. Reshuffle only between rounds, when pos (burn included) >= cut. 1D 7 spots at pen 100% gives a cut at 35 of 52. Rounds per shoe engine/indep: 6D75 43.37499/43.37487 (z 0.25); 1D 3 spots 3.59040/3.59043; 6D80 ENHC 31.02203/31.02162 (z 1.18); 8D85 5 spots 21.96315/21.96311; 6D75 4 spots 17.85702/17.85700. Pen 0 gives exactly 1.00000 round per shoe.
8. ✔ **Burn card** — Code reading (DrawHidden at Shuffle, never counted, leaves the deck estimate); bin-frequency match in burn configurations. Simulator.cs:419-426, sim.rs:471-479. TC-bin chi^2 engine vs indep for burn configs: A 59.7/43, Ai 50.2/43, B 32.5/42, Bf 45.4/42, D 41.4/46, E 58.7/45, G 34.7/36. Pooled over all 9 shoe configs: 443.4 over 406 dof (z about 1.3).
9. ✔ **Emergency mid-round reshuffle (discards only, count restart from cards in view, hidden hole card excluded, full reshuffle next round)** — White-box stacked-shoe test through the verbatim Rust copy and C# reflection (SimCheck3 emerg); statistical comparison in a 1D 7-spot pen-100% game. Table cards moved to the front, discards behind them; final RC 3 (want 3; -9 if the old count were kept); next round reshuffled first (C# and Rust). Config F: 1,357,198 (engine) vs 1,358,791 (indep) mid-round reshuffles in 3.2e8 rounds (z about 1); EV A 0.75896% vs 0.75351% (z 0.65).
10. ✔ **Dealing order and counting: player cards, upcard, second cards, peek hole card counted when revealed (also on the dealer-BJ path), ENHC second dealer card, burn** — 32 hand-crafted rounds with the final RC checked in each (Rust copy with forced in-order dealing, and C# via reflection); code reading. r3_hand.txt: 32/32 ok; r3_hand_cs.txt: 32/32 ok (e.g. 'BJ vs dealer BJ' RC -4 want -4; 'A6 v 5 doubles' RC 1 want 1).
11. ✔ **Deck estimation (Exact / quarter / half / full; rounding direction; minimum)** — Exhaustive comparison against exact integer arithmetic; C#/Rust hash. 1668 values, 0 differ (nearest unit with halves up, at least one unit, Exact at least 6 cards); estimate_decks hash 6B859B84FECDC7DB in both C# and Rust.

Floor, Truncate and the RC metric: 0 wrong. See finding 2.

The independent sim's B0 spot A was -2.66 SE (its spot B -0.75): noise in the reference, not the engine.
28. ✔ **Splits to 8 hands with RSA against CA** — Engine CSM runs of 4e8 rounds each against CA ExactOffTop. 1D SP4: 0.0285% ± 0.0058 vs CA 0.0193 (z 1.6); 1D SP8: 0.0281 (z 1.5); 2D SP8: -0.2792 vs -0.2759 (z -0.6).
29. ✔ **Rounds and shoes counting, AddFrom/Reset, Merge, thread-count independence** — C# SimCheck3 threads; Rust threads mode (8 threads vs 1 thread, statistics); code reading of Merge and the TS mergeSim. C# 3 threads and Rust 8 threads, 10,000,003 rounds requested: exactly 10,000,003 rounds and hands tallied. Merge adds every tally (C# and Rust). 8 vs 1 thread: EV A z -0.75, E[x^2] z -0.27, EV B z 0.80, E[AB] z 0.77, rounds per shoe z 0.49, bins 52.4/57. Same seed twice on one thread gives identical tallies.
30. ✔ **C#/Rust parity on paths the fixtures don't cover** — Random-configuration harness: C# Parity3.exe writes configs, flag tables and tallies; the Rust replay uses the real calcbj_engine::simulate. 160 random configs (all scales 1-60, red tags, IRC with halves, RC/TC, 4 estimations, 3 roundings, 1-7 spots, count-dependent tables, every rule feature) + 40 stress configs (1-2 decks, CSM, 7 spots, 8 hands, RSA): 200/200 identical; 45 with mid-round reshuffles. Seeded 2D run hash 2940FF67BD670313 (108,850 shoes) identical in C# and Rust.
31. ✔ **WASM/TS consumer of the tallies** — Code reading: lib.rs sim_run/simPrepare, simulation.svelte.ts reply/assign/build, sim-data.ts mergeSim. sim_run plays, copies the 6 x 201 tallies plus rounds/shoes/emergency to f64, then resets; TS adds them per chunk; mergeSim adds all tallies. No second simulator implementation in TS.
32. ⚠ **Emergency counter semantics** — Code reading (MidRoundReshuffle adds 1 per reshuffle; the no-discards fallback adds 1 per fresh-pack card) vs the label 'N reshuffle fallbacks' (SimPage.cs:127, SimPage.svelte:117). Informational only. In the stress parity set (CSM, no burn, 7 spots, random tables) it reached 239,514 in 4e6 rounds; it never fired in any realistic configuration tested.
33. · **IRC restart after an emergency reshuffle for unbalanced counts (IRC is defined for a full shoe; the reshuffled discards are fewer)** — not run. Edge case of an edge case (needs a mid-round reshuffle with an unbalanced count); not quantified within budget.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- Adjacent simulation workers start from xoshiro states that share 3 of 4 words (shifted by one), because the worker seeds are consecutive SplitMix positions. Tests found no correlation (180 lagged cross-correlations, max |z| 2.70; pair chi-squares fine), but xoshiro's jump() or disjoint SplitMix positions would remove the overlap by design. Is a change wanted?
- Does index generation (BinMoments / per-bin regression) bin true counts with exactly the simulator's deck estimate (cards physically left, hole card and burn out) and the same rounding conventions, including AwayFromZero for Round? This is outside my area but decides whether the simulated bins and the generated indices line up.
- Three-hand ramp rows use 3 x avg(A,B), and the third seat is not tallied. Its overstatement (the third seat sees fewer cards) was not quantified.
- After an emergency mid-round reshuffle, an unbalanced count restarts at the full-shoe IRC plus the table cards, even though the reshuffled shoe is smaller. Should the IRC be scaled to the remaining decks? Not quantified; very rare.
- In the C# app, opening a scenario with an off-list payout shows 3:2, and editing any game rule silently rewrites it to 1.5 (GamePage.cs:101-102,127). This is a UI-state issue next to finding 3; should it be handled in the same fix?

### 8. Bet-ramp statistics, risk formulas and the optimiser

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: Bet-ramp statistics, risk formulas and the optimiser (analysis)_

I checked every formula in Analysis.cs and analysis.rs (bins, rows, Evaluate, ROR, Kelly, CE, N0 and SCORE, the normal-distribution helpers, TripRuin, ProbLoss and the optimiser), plus their WASM, TypeScript and UI consumers, and re-ran all evidence on fresh simulations. An independent exact-fraction Python version of Evaluate agrees with both engines to <= 2.3e-13 relative on every field: 7 new simulations (1, 2, 3 and 7 spots; 6D at 20M and 200M rounds; 2D at 50M) x 18 ramps. C# and Rust are bit-identical except for 1-ulp exp() differences. Testing confirmed four further points: (1) SeEV is calibrated, (2) rounds are effectively independent, so the per-hour SD, ROR and trip-ruin figures are valid, (3) the assumed correlation of 0.37 matches the measured 0.372 and an independent 0.375, and (4) the normal helpers and the trip-ruin formula are accurate. The SCORE optimiser finds the exhaustive optimum in all 42 real-data cases, and Kelly mode is exact whenever the minimum bet is a multiple of the increment. One minor defect: when the minimum bet is not a multiple of the increment, the optimiser never tries the minimum bet with 2 or 3 hands. With a very narrow min/max window it can also bet below the minimum, or sit out although wong-out is off. On real data this costs <= 0.3% of SCORE or <= 1.9% of Kelly growth. There is also a nit on the spread when the minimum row plays several hands.

1. ✔ **Per-round mixture formulas: E[W]=sum f_i h_i u_i mu_i, E[W^2]=sum f_i u_i^2 (h_i E[x^2]_i + h_i(h_i-1) E[xy]_i), Var=E[W^2]-E[W]^2, rows with u<=0 contribute 0** — derivation from first principles + line-by-line reading. Analysis.cs:219-234 and analysis.rs:253-277 implement exactly this (hands<1 treated as 1; Var clamped at 1e-12)
2. ✔ **INDEPENDENT CHECK: Evaluate (all scalar fields, row arrays, BankrollForRor x5, RorForBankroll x4) vs exact-fraction Python re-implementation from raw tallies** — py3/refeval.py + py3/cmp_eval3.py (Fractions, no shared code) on 7 fresh sims made with calcbj-engine (s1/s2/s3/s7 20M rounds, s1big/s2big 200M, s2_2d 2 decks 50M) x 18 ramps (flat 1-3 hands, 1-12, wong, backcount, multi-hand, fractional, never bets, negative EV, -40..40, 2 hands everywhere, 1-24 two hands top, out-of-range ramps, tiny/huge bankroll, hands 0). worst relative difference over all fields, both engines: 2.27e-13 (rorForBankroll); flags valid/usedAssumedCov and rowN identical; e.g. s2big 1-12 preset EV +0.014980 SD 3.3935 SCORE 19.487 N0 51,317 ROR 0.07415
3. ✔ **C#/Rust bit parity of Evaluate and Optimize, including paths the fixtures do not cover (Kelly, wong-out, 2-3 hands, off-grid minimum, increment 0, huge spreads)** — same inputs through cs/AnaCheck.exe (compiled from current src/Engine) and the Rust scratch binary; bitwise comparison. 12,271 values plus 238 optimiser ramps compared; only 2 differ, in the last bit (exp in ROR: 0.06012607698330367 vs ...675). All 34 optimiser cases x 7 sims identical
4. ✔ **BinStat for a heads-up (1-spot) sim: Mean=SA/(20n), M2=QA/(400n), E[xy]=mu^2+0.37 v, MeanVar=v/n** — reading + exact reference. Analysis.cs:161-168, analysis.rs:197-203; rowEv/rowM2 match exact fractions to <=6e-16
5. ✔ **BinStat for 2+ spots: Mean=(SA+SB)/(40n), M2=(QA+QB)/(800n), E[xy]=AB/(400n), MeanVar=(v+c)/(2n)** — derivation Var((x+y)/2)=(2v+2c)/4 per round, then /n; reading. Analysis.cs:151-160, analysis.rs:189-196
6. ✔ **Rows: frequency-weighted aggregation, tails collapse into the end rows, MeanVar = sum f_b^2 MV_b / F^2** — exact reference rowFreq/rowEv/rowM2/rowN; 'beyond bins' ramp 95..104 and 'only rare counts' 30..31 cases. identical rowN; rel err <= 6e-16; out-of-range ramps collapse to flat or never-bets as expected
7. ✔ **Assumed two-hand correlation 0.37 for heads-up data** — measured from engine 2/3/7-spot sims (py3/corr.py, exact fractions) + independent infinite-deck H17 Python simulation (py3/corr_indep.py, 400k rounds, no engine code). engine rho 0.3721 (s2, s3, s2big), 0.3723 (s7), 0.3718 (2D); per-count 0.365-0.378; independent 0.3748 +- 0.0014 (E[xy] 0.4934). Var(2 hands) measured/assumed = 1.0013-1.0016; flat 2 hands SD 1.9032 (1-spot, assumed) vs 1.9042 (2-spot, measured)
8. ✔ **DI = 1000 EV/SD; SCORE = 1e6 EV^2/Var = DI^2 = win/100 rounds at a $10k full-Kelly bankroll** — derivation (Kelly unit k=B EV/Var, 100 k EV = 1e6 EV^2/Var) + exact reference. Analysis.cs:237-238; the engine's Kelly unit uses E[W^2], which differs from Var by EV^2/E[W^2] ~2e-5 (negligible)
9. ✔ **N0 = Var/EV^2 (infinite for EV<=0), HoursN0 = N0/rph, the '84% ahead after N0' text** — derivation: after N0 rounds the mean equals 1 SD, Phi(1)=0.8413; exact reference. Analysis.cs:239-240; negative-EV ramp gives N0=Inf, which serialises as null and becomes Infinity in TS (state.svelte.ts:444)
10. ✔ **EdgePct = EV / sum f h u; AvgBet, AvgBetPlayed, PlayedFrac** — exact reference. e.g. s2big 1-12 wong <=-1 played 0.5411; rel err <= 4e-16
11. ✔ **WinPer100 = 100 EV unit; SdPer100 = sqrt(100 Var) unit; WinPerHour, SdPerHour = sqrt(rph Var) unit; SeWinPerHour** — exact reference + reading. Analysis.cs:242-246; rel err <= 4e-16
12. ✔ **ROR = exp(-2 EV B/Var) with B = bankroll/unit; BankrollForRor = -ln(r) Var/(2EV) unit; RorForBankroll is its exact inverse; EV<=0 gives 1 or Inf** — algebraic inverse + exact reference at r = 0.05/0.135/0.01/0.5/0.001 and B = 1k/5k/20k/250k. rel err <= 2.3e-13; TS bankrollFor and the ROR curve (RiskPage.svelte:40-55) use the same formula; WASM evaluate returns bankrollFor at [0.05,0.135,0.01], matching the C# tiles
13. ✔ **KellyUnit = bankroll EV/E[W^2]** — derivation: maximiser of the second-order log growth k EV/B - k^2 E[W^2]/(2B^2); exact reference. Analysis.cs:250, analysis.rs:296
14. ✔ **Certainty equivalent per hour = (EV - Var/(2 B_units)) rph unit** — derivation: ln(B+c) = E ln(B+W) expanded to 2nd order gives c = EV - Var/(2B); exact reference. Analysis.cs:249; tooltip and glossary text match
15. ⚠ **Spread = MaxBet (units x hands) / MinPositiveUnits** — reading + exact reference. correct when the minimum row plays 1 hand; reports 24 for a 2-hands-everywhere 1..12 ramp whose stake per round is 2..24 (nit finding)
16. ✔ **SeEV calibration including correlated hands in the same round** — 200 independent replications x 2M rounds each, 1 and 2 spots, 6 ramps (Rust reps mode + py3/reps2.py). empirical SD of EV / reported SeEV = 0.995, 0.995, 1.040, 1.011, 1.051, 1.022 (1 spot) and 1.052, 1.052, 0.960, 0.933, 0.966, 0.953 (2 spots); 95% band for a correct SE 0.902-1.098
17. ✔ **SeEV omits the between-bin (frequency) variance term** — exact full-variance formula in refeval.py (SEfull). SEfull/SE = 1.00005-1.00051 on all sims and ramps
18. ✔ **Independent-rounds assumption behind SD/hour, sqrt(h) scaling, ROR and trip figures** — Rust autocorr mode: 20M sequential rounds; variance of block sums vs m Var. ratios for m = 10/100/1000/10000: flat 1.000/0.997/1.008/0.992; 1-12 1.000/0.988/0.997/1.029; backcount 1.000/0.988/0.995/1.018
19. ✔ **Phi/Erfc (Numerical Recipes Chebyshev) accuracy over x in [-40,40] step 0.02 plus extremes** — 40-digit Decimal reference (py3/hpnormal.py) and Python math.erfc (py3/erfc_check.py). max rel err 9.4e-8 on [-5,5], 1.07e-7 on [-37,-5], 3.7e-15 for x>5; underflows to 0 below x=-38.5 (values <1e-307); Phi(+-1e300) = 1/0
20. ✔ **LogPhi tail (3-term asymptotic series for x<=-5)** — via TripRuin against the 40-digit reference; Mills-ratio hand check at x=-5. 8e-4 relative jump at x=-5 (series 0.9648 vs exact 0.96404); TripRuin max rel err 3.6e-4 for P>1e-200 (P=1.28e-8) and 9.9e-5 for P>1e-3
21. ✔ **PhiInv (Acklam, no refinement) for p from 5e-324 to 1-1e-15** — 40-digit reference; also bisection on math.erfc. max abs err 6.8e-8, max rel err 1.8e-9; risk-page percentiles within 2e-9 (z0.95 engine 1.6448536251 vs 1.6448536270); hard-coded 1.2816 vs 1.28155157 (3.8e-5). The math.erfc bisection shows 1e-3 at p=1-1e-14 only because that reference is ill-conditioned near 1
22. ✔ **TripRuin closed form P = Phi((-B-mu n)/(s sqrt n)) + exp(-2 mu B/s^2) Phi((-B+mu n)/(s sqrt n)), any sign of mu** — derivation (reflection principle) + Monte Carlo of Brownian motion with Brownian-bridge crossing correction, 40,000 paths (py3/mc_trip2.py). 7 cases, z = -0.46, +0.53, +0.60, +1.61, +0.61, -0.76, +0.21 (e.g. engine 0.03954 vs MC 0.03999 +- 0.00098). A discrete walk with blackjack-like jumps is within ~1 SE (0.0425 +- 0.0032 vs 0.0395)
23. ✔ **TripRuin edge and overflow cases** — 40-digit reference. n<=0 gives 0; B<=0 gives 1; (1e-9, 7182, 1e9, 1e12): 3.90852e-32 vs 3.90851e-32; (-0.001, 1.3, 1e6, 1e9): 0.50718971 vs 0.50718970; large |mu| n saturates at 1/0 with no overflow (exp argument capped at 700)
24. ✔ **ProbLoss = Phi(-mu n/sqrt(var n)); chart 'chance of losing' = Phi(-mu/sd)** — 40-digit reference; reading ChartsPage.cs:117 and ChartsPage.svelte:42. max abs err 4.0e-8
25. ✔ **C#/Rust parity of the normal helpers** — bitwise comparison on 4,016 x, 1,935 p, 427 trip and 24 loss inputs (C# fed exact bit patterns). Phi: 9 last-bit differences, all < 1e-240; PhiInv 0; trip 4 last-bit; loss 0
26. ✔ **Optimiser theory: SCORE optimum lies on the per-row Lagrangian path with lambda = E[W^2]/EV; lambda grid 10^0.5..10^6.5 (0.05-decade steps, then +-0.05 decades in 0.0025 steps) covers the needed range** — derivation (dS/db_i = 0 gives dE/db_i - dQ/db_i/(2 Q/E) = 0); range estimate lambda >= Var/EV >= 26. Analysis.cs:375-395; e.g. a 1-12 ramp needs lambda ~770, and 10,000-unit maxima need <= ~1e6
27. ✔ **Kelly mode objective and per-row optimum: maximise f(h b mu - b^2(h M2 + h(h-1)C)/(2 lambda)), lambda = bankroll units x Kelly fraction, b* = lambda mu/(M2+(h-1)C)** — derivation (second-order log growth is separable over rows). Analysis.cs:327-344, 378; analysis.rs:408-431, 487
28. ✔ **SCORE optimiser vs exhaustive search on real data** — Rust brute mode (all feasible units x hands x wong-out per row, Pareto pruning shown valid) on s2big, s1big, s2_2d; 14 settings each (max 4..24, increments 0.5/1/2, 1-3 hands, wong out on/off, 6/8/12 rows). 42/42 cases: engine SCORE = exhaustive optimum (ratio 1.000000), e.g. 2D 3 hands 274.0753 = 274.0753
29. ✔ **SCORE optimiser vs exhaustive search, synthetic fuzz with the minimum on the increment grid** — Rust fuzzscore (random rows, settings, 1-2 spots). 4,539 trials: min ratio 0.9936, 1st percentile 0.99998; second run of 5,937: min 0.9989
30. ✔ **Kelly ramp vs exhaustive per-row maximum** — Rust kelly mode (22 cases x 3 real sims) and fuzzkelly (20,000 synthetic). 0 shortfalls whenever the minimum is a multiple of the increment; shortfalls only with off-grid minima (see finding)
31. ✘ **Optimiser respects min/max, wong-out off and max hands** — Rust constraints mode, 20,000 synthetic + 3,000 real-data trials, Kelly and SCORE. 0 violations when a multiple of the increment lies in [min,max]; 46-55% of trials violate when none does (e.g. min 2.5, max 2.75, inc 1 bets 2.0; min 1, max 3, inc 5 sits out with wong-out off). Same output in C# and Rust
32. ✘ **Off-grid minimum with 2-3 hands on real data** — brute and kelly modes with min 1.5/2.5/0.5/0.25/1 and increments 1/2/5 on s2big and s2_2d. Kelly growth -1.45% (min 2.5, inc 1, 2 hands), -1.87%/-1.54% (min 2.5, inc 5), -0.30..-0.34% (2D); SCORE 0.9972, 0.9982, 0.9991 of optimum; all other off-grid settings optimal
33. ✔ **Degenerate data: empty sim, all pushes (zero variance), positive mean with zero variance (1 and 2 spots), a single 1-round bin, ramps that never bet** — hand-made SimData through Evaluate and Optimize in both engines. no NaN, no crash, C# = Rust bit-identical; never-bets is invalid (UI shows dashes); zero variance clamps Var to 1e-12; EV<=0 gives SCORE 0, N0 Inf, ROR 1, KellyUnit 0, BankrollForRor Inf
34. ✔ **Code-level C#/Rust differences on uncovered paths (Math.Round ties-to-even vs round_ties_even, Math.Pow vs powf, Math.Max/Min NaN rules vs dmax/dmin, null guard, BetRamp.Create with max<min)** — line-by-line reading + parity runs above. no behavioural difference seen in 238 optimiser ramps or the edge cases; Rust create() clamps negative sizes where C# would throw (not reachable from the UI)
35. ✔ **BetRamp helpers Row/UnitsAt/HandsAt/Resize/MaxUnits/MaxBet/MinPositiveUnits** — reading C# and Rust; exercised by out-of-range ramp cases. Analysis.cs:41-81 = analysis.rs:49-87
36. ✔ **Consumers: RiskPage (C#, Svelte), Compare snapshot (AppState.cs:420 vs state.svelte.ts:440-452), contribution table share of variance, SimPage flat EV +- SE, spread sweep optimiser settings** — reading. same formulas and fields in C# and TS; trip mu/var in dollars per observed round with n = hours x rph; percentiles exp + z sd
37. ✔ **Tooltip and glossary statements (SCORE, DI, N0, ROR, Kelly, CE, SD x sqrt(h), covariance ~0.48 units^2)** — compared with the derivations and measured values. all formulas correct; glossary covariance ~0.48 vs measured E[xy] 0.492 (6D), 0.4935 (2D), 0.4934 (independent): acceptable as an approximation
38. · **Effect of playing a different number of hands than simulated on the count-bin frequencies and rounds per shoe** — not checked. outside the analysis formulas (simulator design); listed as an open question
39. · **Exact log-utility Kelly/CE vs the second-order approximation** — not checked. needs the full result distribution rather than moments; the second-order form is the standard (Schlesinger) definition

Open questions the reviewer left:
- Bin frequencies come from the simulated number of spots. A ramp that plays 2-3 hands only at high counts in a heads-up simulation (or 1 hand in a 2-spot one) changes the cards used per round, and so the count distribution and rounds per shoe. Not quantified here: it belongs to the simulator, not the analysis formulas.
- Kelly unit and certainty equivalent use the standard second-order log-growth approximation. Not compared with an exact E[ln(1+W/B)], which needs the full per-round result distribution.
- The guide's 'covariance ~0.48 units^2 per pair of hands' is slightly low against the measured 0.492 (6D H17), 0.4935 (2D) and 0.4934 (independent infinite-deck H17). Within the meaning of '~', so not reported.

### 9. Windows app: numbers shown to users — review 1 of 2 (independent)

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: Windows app (C#): math and numbers shown to users (app-csharp)_

I ran the real WinForms pages without showing a window. Each page was given 40M/20M-round simulations covering six bet-ramp scenarios: preset 1–12, back-count, two hands, heads-up with assumed covariance, KO running count, flat-bet negative EV and a ramp that never bets. I dumped every tile, grid, chart and chip value and recomputed them in Python from the raw tallies: 1855 checks with 3 mismatches, all a $1 rounding difference. Risk formulas were confirmed by Monte Carlo, the sweep optimiser by brute force, correlations, house edges and the basic-strategy chart against published values (from memory), and every AppState helper (presets, count scale, ranges, RampForRules) by round-trip and parity checks. The formulas the pages use (EV, SD, edge, SCORE=DI², N0, ROR, bankroll for a target ROR, Kelly unit, CE, trip ruin, loss probability, percentiles) are correct and displayed in the right units. No critical or major defects. Six minor findings: a Game-page deck change replaces a custom ramp; the published Hi-Lo rows are loaded unconverted for other counts; the spread ignores hands at the minimum bet; penetration labels go past the cut-card cap; strategy-chart tooltip EVs include dealer blackjacks; a typographic minus in an override index becomes 0. Five nits are cosmetic.

1. ✔ **Theme.Pct / SignedPct: sign, negative zero, tiny values, NaN/inf** — Executable: Fmt.exe compiled with the app sources. On .NET 4.8: (-0.001).ToString("F2")="0.00", so no "-0.00%" ever appears. Pct(-4e-6,2)="0.00%", Pct(-4e-6,4)="-0.0004%". SignedPct(1e-9,3)="+0.000%" (cosmetic). NaN or inf shows "—".
2. ✔ **Theme.Money / Money0 rounding and sign** — Executable: Fmt.exe. Money(-0.004)="$0.00"; Money(0.005)="$0.01"; Money(999.995)="$1,000"; Money(1234.5)="$1,235"; Money0(-0.4)="$0"; Money0(-999.996)="-$1,000"; NaN and -inf show "—".
3. ✔ **Theme.F with NaN and ±infinity** — Executable (Fmt.exe) plus a review of every F() call site. F(NaN)="—"; F(+inf)=F(-inf)="∞". No call site can pass -inf: N0, HoursN0 and Kelly are guarded by EV>0, and BankrollForRor only returns +inf.
4. ✔ **Theme.Count (K/M/B) thresholds** — Executable: 21 values. 999,950→"1M"; 999,950,000→"1B"; 1,234,567,890→"1.23B"; 10,050,000,000→"10.05B"; 99,950→"100K"; 1,049→"1K".
5. ✔ **Theme.CountLabel for ramp end rows** — Executable. "≤ -3", "0", "+5", "≥ +8"; a single-row ramp shows "0".
6. ⚠ **Theme.TryParse (ramp units/hands, tags, override index)** — Executable plus a review of all callers. "25,000"→25, "$1,000"→1, "1,5"→1.5, "25,000.50"→25000.5. No field parsed with TryParse ever shows thousands grouping (the bankroll and unit fields are NumericUpDown and parse "25,000" correctly), so the web's earlier "25,000" bug has no C# twin. But "−1" (U+2212) and "–1" fail to parse, which leads to finding 6.
7. ✔ **Culture independence of displayed numbers** — Code reading: Program.Main. CurrentCulture, CurrentUICulture and DefaultThreadCurrentCulture are all InvariantCulture, so worker threads format the same way.
8. ✔ **RampPresets.UnitsAt shapes vs preset names** — Executable: 8 presets × 17 Hi-Lo TCs. Flat: all 1. 1–4: 1,1,2,3,4. 1–8: 1,1,2,4,6,8. 1–12: 1,2,4,6,8,10,12. 1–16: up to 16. 1–24: 1,4,8,…,24. Preset 6 bets 0 for TC ≤ −1 and 1 at TC 0. Preset 7 bets 0 up to TC 0, then 2..12 from +1, so the spread tile shows 1–6. The floor uses +1e-9: 0.999999→1 unit, 1→2 units.
9. ✔ **Rust preset_units / apply_preset / ramp_for_rules parity with C#** — Code reading: web/engine-wasm/src/app.rs vs AppState.cs. Same table, same floor(x+1e-9), same clamp to 0..6, same wong rule for preset 6, and a custom preset also becomes 3 there. reset_ramp_range and index_range are identical (MAX_RAMP_ROWS 121).
10. ✔ **CountScale.Factor for the 13 presets** — Independent hand calculation of K = Σ over 52 cards of (tag − mean)², factor = √(K/40), vs program output. Hi-Lo K=40→1. Omega II K=128→1.7889. Uston SS K=187.7→2.1662. KO 1.0451. Red Seven 1.0238. Hi-Opt I 0.8944. Halves 1.0488. APC 2.5298. All match.
11. ✔ **MetricFor and HiLoEquivalent are inverses** — Executable: 13 counts × 8 deck counts × Hi-Lo −10..+10 in 0.5 steps. Maximum round-trip error 3.6e-15.
12. ✔ **RampRangeFor (Hi-Lo −3..+8) and IndexRangeFor (−10..+10), all counts and deck counts** — Executable. Hi-Lo −3..8 at every deck count. KO 6D ramp −23..21, index −51..29. Uston SS 8D −37..83, exactly 121 rows (the cap is not exceeded); index clamped to −100..100. Red Seven 1D −4..4.
13. ✔ **DefaultRoundsPerHour table** — Compared with the published Kilby/Wizard hands-per-hour table (from memory). 209/139/105/84/70/60/52 for 1–7 spots, as published.
14. ✔ **ApplyPreset for every count (6D)** — Executable. The top bet of 12 starts at a Hi-Lo equivalent of 6.0–6.7 for all 13 counts. KO 1–12: first raise at RC −7 (Hi-Lo equivalent 1.02), top at RC +13. Back-count preset: KO plays from RC −7, Uston SS from +6.
15. ✔ **RampForRules with a preset ramp** — Executable: KO, Red Seven, Uston SS, Hi-Lo; 6D to 1..8D. Equal to ApplyPreset under the target rules for every deck count. Example KO 6D→2D: [−5..10] 1,1,1,1,1,1,2,4,6,6,8,10,12,12,12,12.
16. ✔ **RampForRules with a custom ramp** — Executable: identity check and independent nearest-neighbour check. 6D→6D returns the identical ramp. 6D→2D: 0 rows differ from an independent nearest-Hi-Lo-equivalent mapping, for 4 counts.
17. ✘ **Game page deck change with a custom ramp (RC or unbalanced count)** — Executable: the same call sequence as GamePage.Save, plus the real SweepPage. A KO custom 1–8 ramp with wong-out becomes the 1–12 preset with no wong-out (RampPreset=3). On the same 2D simulation the sweep point shows SCORE 176.4 / ROR 0.13%; after the Game page change it is SCORE 145.3 / ROR 0.52%. See finding 1.
18. ⚠ **Count page SaveConversion resets the ramp for RC/unbalanced counts** — Code reading: CountPage.cs:296-300. Any IRC edit on an RC count runs ApplyPreset(RampPreset), so a custom ramp becomes 1–12, the same mechanism as finding 1. Resetting on a metric or count change is reasonable.
19. ✔ **SimKeyOf contents** — Code reading: Rules.Key, CountSystem.Key, StrategyConfig.Key. Covers every rule (including pen to 4 decimals and burn card), all 20 card-code tags, IRC, metric, deck estimation, rounding, index mode and range, overrides, and spots. Unit, ramp and RPH are correctly left out because they don't affect the simulation.
20. ⚠ **AppState.Result cache key** — Code reading. The key is Rounds|Key|ramp|unit|rph|bankroll. A different SimData with the same Key and Rounds (for example a sweep run of equal size stored by Remember) would reuse the old AnalysisResult. The difference is simulation noise only. Listed as an open question.
21. ✔ **Snapshot → CompareEntry fields** — Executable: Compare grid vs Results tiles for S1/S2/S3/S6/S7. S1: $36.87, $868, $35.11, +0.711%, $49.38, 1–12/100%, 17.18, 4.15, 58,202/554, 8.66%, $30,612, $30.58, $21.80. A never-bet ramp gives "—" and "— / —".

23. ✔ **Raw AnalysisResult values vs independent recomputation from the tallies (6 scenarios)** — Python verify_pages.py: rebuilds bins (1/20 units, two-seat pooling, E[XY]), rows, EV, E[W²], variance, SE. EV, Var, M2, SeEV, AvgBet, ROR, Kelly, CE and Spread agree to 1e-9 relative. S1: EV 0.0140457 units/round, Var 11.48218, M2 11.48238, SeEV 0.000444.
24. ✔ **Results tiles: win/h, per 100, sim error, SD/h, SD/100** — Python recomputation; tolerance is half the last displayed digit. S1: 0.0140457×105×25=$36.87; ×100×25=$35.11; SE $1.17; √(11.482×105)×25=$868; √(1148.2)×25=$847. S4 heads-up at 209 RPH: $175.48 and $1,849.

26. ✔ **SCORE and DI** — Python, plus the definition: Kelly unit B·EV/E[W²] gives 1e6·EV²/E[W²] per 100 rounds. S1 SCORE 17.18=1e6·0.0140457²/11.4822, DI 4.15. The code's SCORE equals DI² exactly; the strict Kelly definition differs by a factor 1+1/N0 (1.000017).
27. ✔ **N0 and N0 in hours** — Python. S1 11.4822/0.0140457²=58,202 rounds, /105=554 h. S2: 22,638 rounds, 216 h.
28. ✔ **Lifetime ROR and bankroll for 5%, 13.5% and 1% ROR** — Python (exp(−2·EV·B/Var) and its inverse). S1 ROR 8.66%; $30,612, $20,463, $47,058. S2 1.66% and $18,274.

30. ✔ **Certainty equivalent per hour** — Python ((EV − Var/(2B/unit))·rph·unit). S1 $21.80/h, S3 $50.17/h, S6 −$13.50/h.
31. ✔ **Spread, share of rounds played, hours to double, basic-strategy edge tile** — Python. S1 1–12, 100.0%, 25000/36.87=678 h, −0.530%. S2 1–6, 27.7%. S3 1–24. Spread with hands at the minimum is finding 3.
32. ✔ **Ramp grid and 'Where the money comes from' grid** — Python, every row of every scenario. Frequency, EV/hand, bet, wager, win per 100 rounds, share of win and share of variance all match. Example S1 +4 row: 2.50%, +2.03%, $200, $10.15, 28.9%, 18.1%.
33. ✔ **'Share of variance' uses second moments rather than variance** — Python. Sum of row second moments divided by variance = 1.000017 (S1) to 1.000044 (S2), so the shares are unaffected at the displayed precision.

63. ✔ **Published I18 + Fab 4 rows (S17) vs published values and the engine** — Code reading plus engine indices (H17Idx.exe, exact decks). The rows match the published S17 list. The engine's S17 indices are within 1 of every row (for example 16vT +1, 12v6 −1, 13v3 −2).
64. ⚠ **Published rows under H17** — Engine indices S17 vs H17 (H17Idx.exe). The app changes only 11vA (−1), 10vA (+3) and 15vA (−1) for H17. The engine moves 12v6 from −1 (S17) to −3 (exact rounding) or −4 (floored) under H17; the app keeps −1. See open question 1.
65. ✘ **Published Hi-Lo rows when another count is selected** — Executable: the real LoadPublished, 30M rounds per run. KO SCORE 14.60→12.18 and win/h −$2.7. Omega II 18.89→15.38 and −$3.0. KO's 12v5 row '−2' equals Hi-Lo TC +2.3. Finding 2.
66. ✔ **Override semantics** — Code reading: StrategyBuilder.ApplyOverride. ≥/≤ with a ±1e-9 tolerance, applied to all bins −100..100 as the page text says. A 'No…' play inverts the condition; insurance needs Rules.Insurance.
67. ✔ **Basic-strategy chart cells (6D H17 DAS LS)** — Executable: real StrategyChart.Render, compared with the standard published chart. All 34 rows match: 11 doubles vs A; 15 surrenders vs T and A; 16 vs 9/T/A; 17 'Rs' vs A; A8 'Ds' vs 6; 8,8 'Rp' vs A; 9,9 stands vs 7/T/A. Index marks match DescribeIndices.
68. ✘ **Strategy chart tooltip EVs** — Executable plus code reading (CAEngine.Evaluate). 16 v T shows surrender −0.539 and stand −0.576. These include dealer-blackjack deals (pBJ=24/309); the conditional values are −0.500 and −0.540. Finding 5.
69. ✔ **StrategyChart.ShortIndex rounding** — Code reading. DescribeIndices thresholds are always whole bin values, so the banker's rounding never bites.
70. ✔ **Guide, tooltip and dialog definitions vs code** — Code reading plus numbers. SCORE=DI² (exact in code); DI=1000·EV/SD; N0=(SD/EV)² and 'ahead 84%' (Φ(1)=0.841); ROR=exp(−2·EV·B/Var); Kelly=B·EV/E[W²]; CE=EV−Var/(2B); edge = per initial amount wagered; rates per observed round. The Optimise dialog says 'EV÷variance' while the code uses EV÷E[x²] (factor ≤1.0003).
71. ✘ **Spread definition with multiple hands** — Executable: Spread.exe. Two hands at every count, 1..12 units: MaxBet 24 / MinPositiveUnits 1 shows '1–24'; the real table spread is 2..24 = 1–12. Finding 3.
72. ⚠ **ROR precision across views** — Code reading plus dumps. Header uses 3 decimals below 0.1%, else 1; tiles 4 below 0.1%, else 2; Report and Compare 2; Sweep 1. A 0.04% ROR shows '0.0%' in the sweep grid. Nit, finding 10.
73. ✔ **Simulation page text claims (1/20 units, speed)** — Code reading plus timing. Tallies are divided by 20 and 400. Measured 85.8M rounds/s heads-up and 33–35M/s with 3 spots on 8 threads (shared CPU), consistent with 'order of 100 million'.
74. ⚠ **Web/Rust parity of the findings** — Code reading: web/app/src, engine-wasm, engine/src/analysis.rs. The web has the same behaviour for findings 1 (decksChanged→defaultRamp→apply_preset), 2 (addPublished), 5 (StrategyChart.svelte 'EV per unit'), 7 (RiskPage.svelte 1.2816) and 9 (CountPage.svelte 'ed'). Rust analysis.rs:298 shares finding 3. Penetration: the web clamps to 0–1, C# does not.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- H17 published rows: under H17 'Add published Hi-Lo I18 + Fab 4' changes only 11vA (−1), 10vA (+3) and 15vA (−1). The engine moves 12 v 6 from −1 (S17) to −3 (exact rounding) or −4 (floored, MC-confirmed in the 2026-10-28 audit), and 13 v 3 by 1. I could not verify from a source whether the published H17 table lists 12v6 at −1 or around −3. If it is −3, that row is off by 2 under H17.
- With the engine's index generation, the published Hi-Lo rows scored +$2.05/h ± 1.9 better than the generated I18+Fab4 on default 6D H17 (30M rounds each). Not significant, but the index reviewers may want a larger run.
- StrategyBuilder.MidDepthDecks and BinMoments use the nominal penetration, not the capped cut card. For 1–2 decks (1D with 3 spots: 75% nominal, 67.3% actual) this moves index generation and the RC ramp mapping slightly. This is engine scope; I did not quantify it beyond dMid 0.625 vs 0.663 decks.
- AppState.Result caches on Rounds|Key|ramp|unit|rph|bankroll. If a different SimData with the same key and round count replaces Sim (for example a sweep run of equal size stored by Remember), pages can show the old AnalysisResult while the Simulation page shows the new data. The difference is noise only; I did not reproduce it end to end.
- Small label points I did not report as findings: the Compare 'Average bet' is the bet when playing; SCORE shows 0.00 next to a negative DI although the guide says SCORE = DI²; the Charts legend says 'Bet (units)' while the axis says units × hands.

### 9. Windows app: numbers shown to users — review 2 of 2 (independent)

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: app-csharp_

I checked every number that the C# app computes or shows: the Results, Risk, Charts, Compare, Sim, Game, Count and Sweep pages, the header chips, the HTML report, the tooltips, the Guide and the dialog texts, plus the AppState helpers (presets, CountScale, ramp and index ranges, RampForRules). I compiled a harness with the real app sources, ran a 100M-round default simulation and read the values the pages actually display. A separate Python script recomputed them from the raw tallies. Every tile, grid row and chart point matched to the displayed digit. The only exception is a half-cent rounding artifact. ROR, bankroll-for-ROR, trip ruin (confirmed by Monte Carlo), probability of loss, percentiles, SCORE=DI^2, N0, Kelly unit B*EV/E[W^2], CE and the edge formulas are all correct. I found no critical or major errors in the formulas. The findings are about state handling and labels. On the Game and Count pages a custom ramp is replaced by the 1-12 preset for running-count and unbalanced counts, while the decks sweep carries it over: sweep 2D shows $228/h, the Game page then gives $142/h. The cached Result can belong to a simulation that has since been replaced. Stale results are labelled with the new settings. There are also a few display and parsing nits, including C#/TS differences.

1. ✔ **Theme.Pct / SignedPct / F: negative zero and rounding** — executable: AppCheck.exe fmt (scratch harness compiled with src/App + src/Engine). Pct(-1e-5,2)='0.00%', Pct(-1e-5,3)='-0.001%', SignedPct(1e-5,2)='+0.00%', F(-1e-4,'0.00')='0.00'; .NET Framework 4.8 never prints '-0.00'; Pct(0.004999,2)='0.50%', F(0.125,'0.00')='0.13'
2. ✔ **Theme.Money / Money0: cents first, half away from zero, no '-$0'** — executable: AppCheck fmt. Money(-0.004)='$0.00', Money(-0.005)='-$0.01', Money(999.994)='$999.99', Money(999.995)='$1,000', Money0(-0.4)='$0', Money0(-0.5)='-$1', Money(-1234.5)='-$1,235'
3. ✔ **Theme.Count unit thresholds (K/M/B)** — executable: AppCheck fmt. 999949->'999.9K', 999950->'1M', 1949999->'1.9M', 999950000->'1B', 1234567890->'1.23B', 1049->'1K', 1050->'1.1K'
4. ✘ **Theme.TryParse (ramp Units/Hands cells, count tags, override index) vs web parseNumber** — executable: AppCheck fmt + reading web/app/src/lib/format.ts:104. C#: '25,000'->25, '1,000'->1, '1,5'->1.5, '1,000.5'->1000.5; web parseNumber('25,000')=25000. Reported as a finding.
5. ✔ **Results page: all 12 tiles vs an independent re-implementation** — executable: AppCheck pages 100000000 reads Tile.value/sub via reflection; recheck_pages.py recomputes Bins/Rows/Evaluate from raw tallies with exact fractions. Both give: $38.27/h ($36.45/100, ±$0.74), SD $867/h ($846/100), edge +0.739% (avg bet $49.35), SCORE 18.56 / DI 4.31, N0 53,891 rds / 513 h, ROR 7.84%, B(5%) $29,424, B(13.5%) $19,668, B(1%) $45,232, Kelly $31.82 (0.79x), CE $23.24/h, spread 1-12 / 100%, 653 h to double, basic edge -0.530%

Freq / EV per hand / wager / win per 100 / share of win / share of variance agree on all rows. Only the +6 row win/100 differs: app $8.02, exact $8.025 (the double is 8.0249999999999986, a half-cent artifact).

11. ✔ **Trip-ruin formula (reflection principle with drift) in Analysis.TripRuin** — Monte Carlo of a Gaussian walk with the Broadie-Glasserman continuity correction (tripruin_mc.py, 30k paths) + closed form. Formula 0.03988; MC 0.0411 ± 0.0012. The discrete-round (Siegmund) correction would give 0.0390, which is negligible.

Hi-Lo sim + KO RC ramp: -$12.41/h, SCORE 0.00, ROR 100.00%, avg bet $111.05, while the banner says 'results are for the previous settings' (Hi-Lo: $38.43/h, SCORE 18.80). Reported as a finding.
18. ✔ **Report.cs numbers** — executable: Report.Build dump vs Results tiles. Same values ($38.27, $867, +0.739%, 18.56, 4.31, 53,891, 7.84%, $29,424, $31.82, $23.24, ±$0.74, 1-12, 100.0%); the by-count table equals the Results grid
19. ✔ **Header chips (MainForm.UpdateHeader)** — code reading. Same AnalysisResult fields; ROR shown to 1 decimal, or 3 decimals when < 0.1%; status line marks stale results
20. ✔ **Sim page tiles and bins table** — executable: same Evaluate path as SimPage (flat ramp [0,0]) + code reading. Flat EV -0.445% ± 0.010%, SD 1.150, 21.7 rounds per shoe; per-bin SE = sqrt(MeanVar); 2-hand corr = (Cov-Mean^2)/Var
21. ✔ **Guide 'covariance ≈ 0.48 units² per pair', GamePage 'ρ ≈ 0.37', Analysis.AssumedCorrelation** — executable: cov.py on the 3-spot tallies. Measured neighbouring-seat covariance 0.492, correlation 0.372 (within-bin pooled and overall agree)
22. ✔ **Definitions in tooltips, Guide and dialogs: SCORE=DI^2, DI=1000 EV/SD, N0=(SD/EV)^2 and 84% ahead, ROR formula, Kelly=B*EV/E[W^2], CE=EV-Var/(2B), edge=win/avg initial bet** — derivation + numeric check. 4.3076^2 = 18.556 = SCORE; Phi(1)=0.8413; Kelly B*EV/m2 = 25000*0.01458/11.4569 = $31.82; Kelly win/100 at $10k = 1e6 EV^2/(Var+EV^2), which differs from SCORE by a factor of 1/(1+1/N0) = 1-1.9e-5
23. ✔ **Guide claim 'house edge 6D S17 DAS 0.41%, H17 0.62%'** — executable: EngineTest.exe ca. Engine 0.4057% / 0.6180%. EngineTest itself uses 0.40% as the published S17 figure; the Guide's 0.41% is the engine's own rounded value.
24. ✔ **Guide claim 'with floored TC, 16 v T stands from +1'** — executable: EngineTest.exe idx (6D S17 LS) + report index list for the default 6D H17 LS. '16 v T Stand at +1 or more' in both
25. · **Guide claim that the off-the-top EV agrees with simulation within 0.005%** — none (offtop suite reserved for the lead). —
26. ✔ **RampPresets.UnitsAt/ApplyPreset shapes vs preset names, for all 13 preset counts** — executable: AppCheck ramps (max bet, min positive bet and ramp text for every preset and count). Hi-Lo: flat 1; 1-4; 1-8; 1-12 (+1:2 ... +6:12); 1-16; 1-24; wong preset sits out at TC<=-1 and bets 1 at 0; back-count bets 0 at TC<=0 and 2-12 from +1. Every count's max and min match its label.
27. ✔ **CountScale.Factor values** — hand computation vs AppCheck ramps. Hi-Lo 1, Hi-Opt I sqrt(32/40)=0.8944, Hi-Opt II sqrt(112/40)=1.6733, Omega II/Zen sqrt(128/40)=1.7889, KO 1.0451 (K=43.69)

29. ✔ **RampForRules nearest-HL-equivalent mapping (KO custom ramp 6D -> 2D)** — executable + hand calculation of HL equivalents. 6D RC+1 = HL 3.06 maps to 2D RC+3 (3.06) -> 2 units; RC+4 (3.83) -> 8; RC+5 (4.59) -> 14; RC+6 (5.36) -> 20. The mapping itself is correct.

Sweep 2D point: $228.22/h, +2.82%, SCORE 154.1, ROR 2.9%; after picking 2 decks on the Game page (same cached sim): ramp replaced by the 1-12 preset, $141.76/h, +2.05%, SCORE 139.0, ROR 0.6%. Reported as a finding.

Base -0.530%; S17 +0.197, 1D +0.517, 2D +0.201, 4D +0.050, 8D -0.025, no DAS -0.144, no surrender -0.088, ES +0.625, D9-11 -0.106, D10-11 -0.201, SP2 -0.054, SP3 -0.008, RSA +0.068, HSA +0.177, 6:5 -1.360, 7:5 -0.453, ENHC -0.106, OBO 0.000 (exact: OBO equals peek)

App on the default 6D H17 LS shows Hi-Lo 0.963 and KO 0.955 (rule-dependent EORs; see open questions). The page's reuse of one CAEngine gives the same values as fresh engines.

Wong Halves is shown as 'level 1.5' (max |tag|; usually called level 3), which is definitional and not reported.

42. ✘ **StrategyChart tooltip when indices are off (basic only)** — executable: AppCheck misc (tips array). Basic-only '16 vs T ... at TC +5' shows off-the-top EVs (stand -0.576, hit -0.571); with real per-count CA at TC +5 the values are stand -0.603, hit -0.632. Reported as a nit.
43. ✘ **StrategyChart index marks** — executable: AppCheck marks (6D S17 LS full indices). '17 v A Surrender from -8 to -6' is drawn as 'R≥-8'. Reported as a nit.
44. ✔ **Optimise and Linear dialogs: texts and formulas** — code reading. Kelly lambda = (Bankroll/Unit) x fraction; bet = lambda*mean/(M2+(h-1)Cov), described as 'EV ÷ variance' (M2≈Var). GenerateDialog: u = b below start, else min(max, b+step*(v-s0+1)); sit-out rule as labelled.
45. ✔ **Units and conventions across pages: $ vs units, per round / per 100 / per hour, percent vs fraction** — code reading of every Set/Rows.Add call + executable dumps. Every SignedPct(EdgePct/100), Pct(ROR) and ROR*100 on chart axes is consistent; Risk 'Units' = $/unit; max bets include hands; Sim SD is in units per round
46. ✔ **Rust app.rs ports of the AppState helpers** — line-by-line reading of web/engine-wasm/src/app.rs. factor, metric_for, reset_ramp_range (121-row cap), index_range, preset_units, apply_preset (custom->3), ramp_for_rules and count_info weights are identical to C#
47. ⚠ **Web Risk page and sweep vs C#** — code reading (RiskPage.svelte, sweep.svelte.ts). Risk formulas are identical. The sweep clamps penetration to [0,1] in web but not in C# (SweepPage.cs:148). Reported as a nit.
48. ⚠ **Displayed ROR precision across pages** — computation for a $100,000 bankroll (ROR = exp(-10.18) = 3.8e-5). Results/Risk '0.0038%', chip '0.004%', Compare/Report '0.00%', Sweep '0.0%'. Reported as a nit.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- Betting correlations shown for the default 6D H17 DAS LS game are noticeably lower for 7-counting systems than published (KO 0.955 vs 0.98, Red Seven 0.957). The engine's EORs for that game (7: +0.220, 5: +0.879, 9: -0.283, A: -0.502 per card x decks) differ from its own 6D S17 no-surrender EORs (7: +0.269, 5: +0.751, 9: -0.201, A: -0.568). The 1D EORs match Griffin's. I could not settle whether this much H17/LS sensitivity is right; the CA reviewer may want to confirm against a published H17/LS EOR table.
- Is replacing custom ramps on a deck or conversion change intended? Both apps do it, but RampForRules' doc comment and the sweep's 'As on the Game page' comment suggest carry-over was meant.
- Wong Halves is shown as 'level 1.5' (max |tag|), whereas the literature usually calls it a level-3 count. This is definitional, so not reported.
- The sweep silently caps requests at 60 points (e.g. 60-90 step 0.1 stops at 65.9); the point labels show it, but there is no warning.
- The Guide's claim that the off-the-top EV agrees with shuffle-every-round simulation within about 0.005% was not checked, because the offtop suite is reserved for the lead.

### 10. Web/desktop/Android app and WebAssembly ops: numbers shown to users

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: app-web_

I checked the web, desktop and phone app's math layer end to end: every WASM op in lib.rs and app.rs, the shipped calcbj.wasm, the worker tallies (simulation.svelte.ts, sim-worker, sim-data), state, sweep, scenario, format and NumberInput, and all nine pages against their C# counterparts. Most checks ran code. A C# harness compiled with the app's own sources (AppState, CountScale, RampPresets, CountPage.Correlations via reflection, Theme, SweepPage logic) agrees exactly with the WASM ops on 11,760 cases. The shipped calcbj.wasm agrees with the native build on 12,782 requests, apart from last-ulp exp/ln noise. The real .svelte.ts modules, compiled with Svelte and run in Node against the shipped WASM, give tallies equal to the native per-worker runs bit for bit through run, add-rounds, stop, failure, hung-worker and phone pause/resume. .bjev files survive both directions between the web and Windows apps, and every Compare number on the Windows sample equals what today's C# app computes. Bottom line: no wrong number in normal use. There is 1 minor defect: after opening a scenario while the Count page is shown, it keeps the old custom index plays and an edit writes them back over the file's. There are 4 nits: speed accounting after a phone goes to the background, format.ts rounding parity in edge cases, double rounding of typed tags, and the "truncateed" typo.

1. ✔ **app.rs index_range vs C# AppState.IndexRangeFor** — independent re-implementation: C# harness AppCheck.exe built with all src/ files, requests run through native calcbj_wasm (wasmcli) — compare_cases.py. 480 cases (13 presets + 7 custom counts incl. thirds, red tags, fractional IRC, unbalanced TC, balanced RC × decks 1-8 × pen 0.5/0.75/0.9): all identical
2. ✔ **app.rs reset_ramp_range + apply_preset (defaultRamp op) vs C# ResetRampRange + ApplyPreset, presets 0-9 (8 = custom and 9 = invalid both fall back to preset 3)** — C# harness vs native WASM op. 4,800 cases: ramp min/max/units/hands and returned preset identical; MAX_RAMP_ROWS 121 fallback included (Uston SS 8D)
3. ✔ **applyPreset op on a shifted custom ramp vs C# ApplyPreset** — C# harness vs native WASM op. 480 cases with random presets 0-7: identical
4. ✔ **rampForRules op (custom ramp carried by nearest Hi-Lo equivalent; presets re-applied) vs C# AppState.RampForRules** — C# harness vs native WASM op. 5,040 cases (decks → 1/2/6/8, presets 3, 6, 8): identical
5. ✔ **countInfo op: factor, betting and insurance correlation (20 card codes), irc, imbalance, balanced, level, tagText, key, scale vs C# CountScale.Factor and CountPage.Correlations** — C# harness (private Correlations called via reflection on a CountPage instance) vs native WASM op. 160 cases: max abs diff 0 for factor, both correlations, irc, imbalance and level; strings and scale identical
6. ✔ **simKey op vs C# AppState.SimKeyOf (results cache and .bjev 'matches settings')** — C# harness, 400 random configurations. identical keys incl. pen 0.66666/0.12345678/0/1, payouts 1.25 and 4/3, override indices 1e-7, 1/3, 0.30000000000000004, 123456.789, disabled overrides
7. ✔ **rulesInfo op (describe, key, totalCards, cutCard, midDepthDecks) vs C# Rules.Describe/Key/CutCard and MidDepthDecks** — C# harness, 400 random rule sets × random spots. all identical, midDepthDecks diff 0
8. ✔ **Shipped web/app/src/lib/engine/calcbj.wasm vs native build of the same sources** — Node runs the .wasm with the wasm.ts protocol (wasm_runner.mjs); compare_x.py compares 12,782 replies value by value. identical: ramps (271,200+270,816+31,920 numbers), keys, house edges (225), strategy indices (6,686 numbers), chart action codes, sim tallies (24,180 numbers), bins (7,035), optimize (462), phiInv (505), countInfo (1,120). Last-ulp differences from libm exp/ln: phi 18/500 (max rel 2.3e-16), tripRuin 21/300 (2.8e-16), probLoss 8/300 (2.4e-16), evaluate ror/bankrollFor 15 values (2.7e-16). Per-count cellEv: 17,694 values differ by up to 3.6e-12 rel (exp in strategy.rs:350); decisions identical in all 130 strategy/chart builds
9. ✔ **sim_run tally layout vs TS constants and simulation.build() column order (n, sa, qa, sb, qb, ab, rounds, shoes, emergency)** — code reading + exact cross-check in sim_test.mjs. Rust TALLY_LEN = 6×201+3 = 1209 = wasm.ts TALLY_LEN; BIN_MIN -100, NUM_BINS 201 match strategy.rs; all 1209 merged values equal native in every run below
10. ✔ **Double precision of i64 tallies carried in Float64Array and JSON (2^53 limit; lib.rs sim() conversion)** — measured tally growth from native 2M-round sims (Hi-Lo 6D, Hi-Lo 1D, KO 1D, 2 spots) + code reading of lib.rs sim(). largest tally (qa/qb of the busiest bin) grows 138–178 per round played → a 10B-round run peaks at 1.8e12; 2^53 is first reached after 5.1e13–6.5e13 rounds held for one setting (about 5,000 maximum runs). JSON.stringify writes integers below 1e21 without an exponent, and serde reads them into i64 exactly
11. ✔ **Plain run: 2,345,678 rounds (not a multiple of the 500k chunk), 4 workers** — compiled simulation.svelte.ts (TypeScript → Svelte compileModule) in Node; mock Web Workers running the shipped calcbj.wasm; fixed seed; cross-check against native wasmcli per worker (sim_test.mjs T1). rounds 2,345,678 = sum(n) = done = target; worker split 845,678+3×500,000; tallies whole numbers; 5 checkpoints monotonic and ≤ final; all 1209 values = Σ native per-worker runs
12. ✔ **Run again on top of held results (mergeSim base + run)** — sim_test.mjs T2. 3,345,678 = 2,345,678 + 1,000,000; seconds added; all values = base + native
13. ✔ **Stop mid-run: chunks in progress drained, none lost or counted twice** — sim_test.mjs T3. stopped at 2,500,000 (whole chunks) = done = Σ worker rounds; tallies = native
14. ✔ **Worker failure: run ends with the chunks that came back** — sim_test.mjs T5 (injected error on a worker's 2nd chunk). error text shown; 4,500,000 kept = done; tallies = native
15. ✔ **Hung worker: drain timeout, hung chunk excluded, time stops at the last tally (lastTallyMs)** — sim_test.mjs T6 (DRAIN_MS set to 300 ms in the compiled copy). 'A simulation worker stopped answering…'; 2,500,000 kept; seconds 1.460 = last tally at 1.460 s (finished at 1.591 s); tallies = native
16. ✔ **Phone app: pause in background, checkpoint, resume; no double counting; paused time excluded** — mobile build of simulation.svelte.ts (__MOBILE__=true), visibilitychange events (sim_test.mjs T4). 0 chunks handed out while hidden; checkpoints 500k,1M,1M,2M,2.5M,3M,3.5M,4M; final 4,000,000; seconds 4.580 of 5.193 s wall with 0.613 s hidden; tallies = native
17. ⚠ **Phone app hidden after the last chunk was handed out (run ends in the background)** — sim_test.mjs T4b. all 3,000,000 rounds kept and equal to native, but seconds 1.090 s while workers were busy for 0.319 s more after hiding (1.409 s wall), so the speed is overstated → nit finding
18. ✔ **Rounds/s, ETA and progress (App.svelte) vs C# MainForm.UpdateHeader** — code reading. rate = done/seconds, ETA = (target−done)/rate, progress = done/target; same formulas and texts as C#; seconds only advance with tallies, so rate stays consistent
19. ✔ **AppState defaults vs C# AppState (ramp, rph table, unit, bankroll, trip, presets, rules, strategy, round options)** — compiled state.svelte.ts in Node (state_test.mjs S0) + code reading. ramp −3..+8 units [1,1,1,1,2,4,6,8,10,12,12,12]; rph 105 for 3 spots; RPH_TABLE {0,209,139,105,84,70,60,52}; ROUND_OPTIONS 10M..10B; RAMP_PRESETS and defaultRules identical to C#

21. ✔ **Settings change: stale banner logic, results per key, showCached** — state_test.mjs S5. spots 3→4: stale, auto rph 84, old results stay until a run; back to 3 spots shows the cached results
22. ✔ **Compare snapshot fields and NaN/Infinity through save/restore (restoreEntry)** — state_test.mjs S6 (fresh AppState restored from mock localStorage). snapshot = evaluation (score, winPerHour, avgBet = avgBetPlayed×unit, playedPct, bankroll5 = bankrollFor[0]); houseEdge = −houseEdge op = −sim.basicEv; a losing flat bet keeps n0/hoursN0/bankroll5 = ∞; a never-betting ramp keeps NaN after restore

The fixture's stored entry predates the 10-28/29 fixes (spread 8 vs 16, house edge 1.5540% vs 1.5672%); today's C# gives the web's values
24. ✔ **.bjev web save → Windows load (sample file)** — writeScenario output read by AppCheck.exe (C# XmlSerializer path). all settings, ramp, sim rounds and the Compare entry identical to the original file
25. ✔ **.bjev with awkward values: Windows → web → Windows** — bjev_roundtrip.mjs with AppCheck writeodd (thirds, 1.05/−0.95 tags, red tags, IRC −1.3/+0.7, pen 0.723, 7:5, ENHC_OBO, ES, D9-11, RSA/HSA, 6 spots auto rph, unit 12.5, bankroll 7777.77, custom ramp with 0 units and 1-3 hands, Compare entries with INF/NaN and rounds 123,456,789,012). web key = Windows key; every value exact (1/3, 2/3 bit-equal); the Windows app reads the web's save identically (15 field groups, Compare entries)
26. ✔ **scenario.ts number mapping (fractions vs percents, ints vs doubles, INF/-INF/NaN, long arrays, xsd:dateTime offsets)** — code reading + the two round trips above. penetration stays a fraction both ways; int fields written as integers; .NET 'INF'/'NaN' read and written; dateTime keeps its offset sign (e.g. −05:30)
27. ✔ **Sweep 'max bet, optimal ramp' points vs C# SweepPage logic** — compiled sweep.svelte.ts on the sample vs AppCheck sweep4 (Analysis.Optimize + Evaluate). 12 points (1–2..1–24): labels, score, win/h, edge, ROR, sd/h, N0 hours equal (max rel diff 0)

35. ✔ **chart.ts niceStep/ticks/compact** — Node run. niceStep(37,4)=10, (123456,6)=20000, NaN→1; ticks(−0.3,0.7,0.1) clean; compact 999,500 → '1M', 1,234 → '1.2K', −1500 → '-1.5K' (999.995 → '1000' is cosmetic only)
36. ✔ **Results page tiles, ramp table and 'Where the money comes from' vs C# ResultsPage.cs** — line-by-line reading + S6/S7 numbers. win/h, ±SE, SD/h, edge = EdgePct/100, SCORE '0.00', DI, N0 '#,0'/never, ROR 4 or 2 decimals, bankroll 5/13.5/1%, Kelly and unit/Kelly, CE/h, spread '0.#', hours to double = bankroll/win, basic EV from the sim; variance share freq·u²·(h·m2+h(h−1)·cov); optimiser and linear dialogs map the same settings
37. ✔ **Risk page vs C# RiskPage.cs and first principles** — reading + derivation. bankroll(r) = −ln r·var/(2·EV)·unit inverts ROR = exp(−2·EV·B/var); trip μ·n, σ√n with n = hours×rph; P(loss) = Φ(−μn/σ√n); drifted Brownian first passage Φ(a) + e^(−2μB/σ²)Φ(b); percentiles = μn + z·σ√n; ±1.2816 SD; max bets use units×hands; ROR curve marker uses the same formula; horizon min(5000, 1.2·N0 h)
38. ✔ **Charts page vs C# ChartsPage.cs** — reading. frequency = rowFreq×100; advantage only where rowN>0; bet = units×hands; contribution = rowContribution×100×unit; normal density over ±3.5σ; chance of losing Φ(−μ/σ)
39. ✔ **Compare page vs C# ComparePage.cs** — reading + S6. 21 metrics with the same formats; best-value logic skips NaN and uses −houseEdge, −N0, −ROR, −bankroll5; 'never (losing game)' for ∞
40. ✔ **Game page vs C# GamePage.cs** — reading + houseEdge op. house edge = −houseEdge op; same 'effect of one rule' rows and labels; cut-card text cut/52 '0.##'; decks change resets ranges only for RC or unbalanced counts (same as C#); default 6D H17 DAS LS −0.5297%
41. ✔ **Sim page vs C# SimPage.cs** — reading. flat-bet EV from ramp 0..0×1 at unit 1 / 100 rph / bankroll 1000; ±SE; SD; rounds per shoe; table shows counts with freq ≥0.005%, SE = √meanVar, SD = √(m2−mean²), corr = (cov−mean²)/var
42. ✔ **Count page info line and published I18 + Fab 4 rows vs C#** — reading; published values from memory. BC/IC 3 decimals, level and factor '0.##', imbalance, IRC note same as C#; 22 published rows (Ins +3, 16vT 0, 15vT +4, TTv5 +5, TTv6 +4, 10vT +4, 12v3 +2, 12v2 +3, 11vA +1, 9v2 +1, 10vA +4, 9v7 +3, 16v9 +5, 13v2 −1, 12v4 0, 12v5 −2, 12v6 −1, 13v3 −2; Fab 4 14vT +3, 15vT 0, 15v9 +2, 15vA +1) with H17 changes 11vA −1, 10vA +3, 15vA surrender −1, same as C#
