IC Hi-Lo 0.760, Hi-Opt II 0.909, Zen 0.850, Wong Halves 0.725, Silver Fox 0.694, KO 0.783 (published 0.76/0.91/0.85/0.72/0.69/0.78). BC is computed from the current rules' EORs: KO 0.955 (6D H17 LS) to 0.969 (S17 NS) vs 0.98 published; with Griffin EORs my re-computation gives 0.977, so the difference comes from the rules, as in C#
44. ✔ **Guide/tooltip definitions (SCORE = DI², N0 = (SD/EV)² with ≈84%, ROR, Kelly B·EV/E[W²], CE = EV − var/2B, SD×√h)** — first principles. 1e6·EV²/var = (1000·EV/SD)²; P(ahead after N0) = Φ(1) = 0.8413; all match analysis.rs/Analysis.cs
45. ✔ **Guide: covariance between neighbouring seats ≈0.48 units², assumed ρ = 0.37** — native simulation, 8M rounds per configuration. cov 0.4868/0.4896/0.4868/0.4781, corr 0.3714/0.3675/0.3716/0.3665 (6D H17 LS 2 spots; 6D S17 NS; 7 spots; 2D S17 3 spots)
46. ✔ **Guide: '6D S17 DAS 0.41%, H17 0.62%' and '16 v T stands from +1'** — houseEdge and strategy ops. 6D S17 DAS no surrender 0.406%, H17 0.618% (the published ~0.40%/0.62% are quoted from memory); with floored TC 16 v T 'Stand at +1 or more' (also with truncate and with round/exact)
47. ✔ **Guide/Sim page speed claim (~100M heads-up rounds/s on 8 cores)** — timed WASM in Node and native. WASM 6.43M heads-up rounds/s per thread (2.89M with 3 spots); native 7.92M. 8 to 16 threads ≈ 50–100M/s, so the claim's order of magnitude holds
48. ✔ **StrategyChart cell EV layout, pair fallback and index marks vs C# StrategyChart.cs** — reading + strategy op. EVs [stand, hit, double, surrender, split] per cell (ca.rs:530-574); unsplit pairs show the 2t / A,A soft-12 code; thresholds are whole numbers (a 4.5 override shows as 'at +5 or more'), so the rounding in shortIndex is harmless
49. ✔ **Header chips (win/h, SD/h, SCORE '0.0', ROR 3 or 1 decimals) vs C# UpdateHeader** — reading. same formulas and formats
50. ✘ **Count page custom index plays table stays in sync with app.strategy** — reading CountPage.svelte, App.svelte, files.ts, state.svelte.ts vs C# CountPage.cs Reloaded handler. rows are copied once at mount (line 155) and nothing refreshes them; opening a .bjev from any page leaves the Count page mounted → minor finding
51. ✘ **True-count note text** — reading. `rounding.toLowerCase() + 'ed'` gives 'truncateed' in both apps → nit

Open questions the reviewer left:
- The Compare entry in web/app/tests/fixtures/windows-sample.bjev was written by the 2026-10-27 build: spread 8 (today 16, max bet = units×hands) and house edge/BasicEV 1.5540% (today 1.5672%). The app is fine (today's C# and web agree exactly), but any test that checks against those stored numbers needs the fixture regenerated with tools/BjevTool.cs.
- The browser build is not bit-identical to the native/C# engine for exp/ln (ROR, trip ruin, phi: ≤4e-16 rel) or for per-count cell EVs (≤3.6e-12 rel; Gaussian depth weights use exp, strategy.rs:350). All 130 strategy/chart builds I compared gave identical decisions. In principle, a play whose two EVs differ by less than about 1e-12 could flip between the browser and Windows. Is that acceptable, or should the README/Guide claim of 'identical results' be qualified?
- Outside my area, by reading only: C# GamePage.Save (src/App/Pages/GamePage.cs:116-151) rewrites every rule from the controls on any change. A .bjev whose payout is not one of the five options (e.g. 1.25), or whose penetration is outside 20–95%, is silently changed to 3:2 or the clamped value as soon as any Game-page control is touched. The web app doesn't do this.
- C# Theme.TryParse (ramp units, tags, override index) reads '25,000' as 25 and '1,000' as 1 (comma as decimal mark when there is no '.'), while the web reads them as thousands. Is that intended for the Windows ramp-units cells?
- The sweep's penetration points are clamped to [0, 1] in the web (sweep.svelte.ts:129) but not in C# (SweepPage.cs:148). The two only differ for points above 100% or below 0%, where C# changes mid-shoe depth, indices and the key.

### 11. Rule handling: analysis vs simulator vs casino rules

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: Rule handling consistency: analysis vs simulator vs real casino rules (area key: rules)_

I traced every Rules option through the C# CA (Evaluate, ExactOffTop/SplitExact), the C# simulator, and their Rust/WASM ports. Then I tested them against code written separately from the engine and re-ran everything for this attempt (v3). That code was a casino-rules simulator over 20 rule sets that EngineTest offtop does not cover (max |z| 1.56) and an exact no-split recursion over 216 rule sets (max diff 4e-16). The engine simulator matched ExactOffTop on the same 20 sets (max |z| 2.66, which fell to 0.24 on a 2e9-round rerun), and also with 3 spots at both seats. C#, Rust and WASM gave bit-identical results on 16 mixed rule sets. Hole-card, surrender, DAS, RSA, HSA, payout, insurance and penetration semantics are consistent with each other and with real casino rules. Rule effects and ENHC/ES strategy cells match published values within tolerance. Two defects remain, both reachable only through a scenario file: the simulator rounds the blackjack payout to 0.05 units while the CA uses the exact value (+0.0756% EV gap at 4:3), and the CA caps split hands at 4 while the simulator allows up to 8.

1. ✔ **Decks: shoe composition 4d per rank 1-9 and 16d tens in CA and simulator** — code reading (Combinatorial.cs FullShoe; Simulator.cs:354-361 baseShoe; sim.rs:350-358) plus sims at 1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8 decks. baseShoe adds 2+2 codes per rank per deck (8+8 for tens), so 4d/16d. The 20 rule sets cover 1D (A,G,I,M,O,R), 2D (B,H,P,S), 3D (J,T), 4D (N), 5D (K), 6D (C,E,F,Q), 7D (L) and 8D (D). Every engine and independent sim agrees with exact (see the simulation items).
2. ✔ **H17 / S17: dealer hits soft 17 only when an ace counts as 11 with hard 7** — code reading of Simulator.cs:665, sim.rs:776 and the independent sim; exact recursion. Break condition: best>17 || (best==17 && !(h17 && dace && dh==7)), the same in C#, Rust and the independent sim. H17 effect on 6D S17 DAS SP4 is -0.2122% (published about -0.21 to -0.22%, quoted from memory).
3. ✔ **US peek: hole card dealt before play; BJ checked under A and T; CA conditions on no BJ and charges pBJ once** — code reading of Simulator.cs:601-634 and Combinatorial.cs:289,344,359-363,723; independent exact recursion (ExactNoSplit, which settles BJ at the end of each path). Peek rows of the 216-game grid: engine ExactOffTop equals the independent recursion to <= 4e-16.
4. ✔ **ENHC (lose all): dealer BJ takes doubles and split bets; CA puts dBJ into stand, double and split values** — code reading (Simulator.cs:682-687 takesAll; CA FillStand(dBJ) when !peekCond); engine sim and independent sim vs exact. ENHC sets D, H, I, N, O, R. Engine 4e8: z -0.03, -2.66, 0.47, -2.10, 0.21, 1.68. H and N rerun at 2e9 with a new seed: z +0.24 and -0.74. Independent: z -0.28, 0.49, -1.44, -0.56, -1.10, 1.12. ENHC exact-grid rows: diff <= 4e-16.
5. ✔ **ENHC_OBO: dealer BJ takes the original bet only; CA treats it like peek** — code reading (sim res=-20 when !takesAll; CA peekCond = !DealerBJTakesAll); rule-effects table; sims. OBO vs US peek effect is exactly +0.0000% (6D S17 DAS SP4), as theory says. OBO sets B, K, M, S, T: engine z -0.18, 0.35, 1.25, 0.51, -0.23; independent z -0.74, 0.40, 0.64, 0.11, 0.13.
6. ✔ **Early surrender in peek games: before the peek, half the bet kept against a dealer BJ (ES vs A and T)** — code reading (Simulator.cs:609-613 esPeek, :627; CA evR=-0.5 when SurrenderBeforePeek); sims. Sets A, L, P: engine z 0.19, -0.78, -0.34; independent z 0.67, 0.19, 0.71. ES effect on 6D S17 DAS is +0.6273% (published about +0.62-0.63% vs A and T, from memory).
7. ✔ **Early surrender in ENHC/OBO: -0.5 always, decided at the player's turn** — code reading (surrAtTurn; res=-10 unless lateSurr); sims. Sets D, K, N, O: engine z -0.03, 0.35, -2.10 (rerun -0.74), 0.21; independent z -0.28, 0.40, -0.56, -1.10. The exact grid includes ES x ENHC and ES x OBO rows (72 ES rows), all diff <= 4e-16.
8. ✔ **Late surrender: after the peek in US games; in ENHC/OBO the whole bet is lost if the dealer then shows BJ** — code reading (Simulator.cs:678; sim.rs:791; CA evR = -pBJ - 0.5(1-pBJ) at Combinatorial.cs:364,712; Types.cs:56-63 doc comment); sims; exact grid. Sets B, H, I, J, Q, S, T: engine z -0.18, -2.66 (rerun +0.24), 0.47, -1.82, -0.12, 0.51, -0.23; independent z -0.74, 0.49, -1.44, -0.16, -0.38, 0.11, 0.13. The named exact checks '2D S17 ENHC LS D9-11' and '4D H17 ENHC LS' agree to 1.7e-16 and 1.0e-16.
9. ✔ **No surrender after split, after double, or on multi-card hands** — code reading (WantsSurrender called only on p1/p2 before PlaySpot; CA evR only on two-card combos). Simulator.cs:640 and sim.rs:747 run before play_spot; Combinatorial.cs:364 and :712 apply only at the deal.
10. ✔ **Pair vs total surrender precedence (pair surrender flag, then split flag, then total flag)** — code reading of Simulator.cs:504-516, sim.rs:563-577, Combinatorial.cs:435-437 and :714-716. Same order in sim, Evaluate pass 3 and ExactOffTop. No-split LS game J (8,8 surrendered as hard 16): engine z -1.82 and independent z -0.16 at 3e8/4e8; exact named check diff 1.75e-16.
11. ✔ **Double on 9-11 / 10-11: hard totals only, no soft doubling (real-world reading of D9-11)** — code reading (Types.cs:46-54 CanDoubleTotal; types.rs:103-109; Simulator.cs:496-502; sim.rs:553-561); exact grid; sims. Soft hands are excluded everywhere. D9-11 sets C, H, P and D10-11 sets G, L, N, T: all |z| <= 2.66 (engine) and <= 1.36 (independent). 144 D9/D10 rows of the exact grid: diff <= 4e-16. Effects: D9-11 -0.0961%, D10-11 -0.1921% (published about -0.09/-0.10% and -0.18/-0.20%, from memory).
12. ✔ **DAS with restricted doubling: a split hand doubles only with DAS, never on split aces, and only on allowed totals** — code reading (sim PlayHand: (!split || (das && a!=1)) && CanDouble; CA SplitHandValue line 169; ExactOffTop SplitHand line 580; Rust identical); sims. C (noDAS D9-11): engine -0.65, independent 1.22. H (DAS D9-11 ENHC): -2.66 (rerun 0.24), 0.49. L (DAS D10-11): -0.78, 0.19. P (noDAS D9-11): -0.34, 0.71. noDAS effect -0.1418% (published about -0.14%).
13. ✔ **MaxSplitHands 1-4 incl. no splitting; resplits count toward the maximum** — code reading (sim nh < maxHands; CA ResplitDP and SplitDP with maxHands); sims. SP2 (G, N, R), SP3 (C, K, O, T), SP4 (the rest) and no-split (J) all within |z| <= 2.66. Effects: SP2 -0.0542%, SP3 -0.0076%, no splitting -0.5687% (published about -0.05%, -0.01%, -0.57%).

RSA sets B, I, L, M, P: engine z -0.18, 0.47, -0.78, 1.25, -0.34; independent z -0.74, -1.44, 0.19, 0.64, 0.71. RSA effect +0.0689% (published about +0.07-0.08%).
15. ✔ **Hit split aces: hitting allowed, doubling split aces never; without HSA, A+A stands as 12 and A+T counts 21** — code reading (Simulator.cs:569; sim.rs:646-647; CA SplitHandValue line 167; SplitHand line 577); sims. HSA sets D, I, M, N, P, T: engine z -0.03, 0.47, 1.25, -2.10 (rerun -0.74), -0.34, -0.23; independent z -0.28, -1.44, 0.64, -0.56, 0.71, 0.13. HSA effect +0.1805% (published about +0.18-0.19%).
16. ✔ **Two-card 21 after a split is not a natural (paid 1:1)** — code reading (sim PlayHand returns 21 then compares with the dealer; CA sev[21]; ExactOffTop Terminal(21)). Simulator.cs:524 and sim.rs:584; Combinatorial.cs:578. Covered by every split sim above.
17. ✔ **Dealer BJ vs player's 21 after a split under ENHC / OBO** — code reading and sims. ENHC: res = -20 * sum(bets), so split 21s lose (Simulator.cs:682-687). OBO: res = -20 net. Agreement with exact in ENHC sets D, I, N, O and OBO sets B, K, M, T (see above).

Exact 1:1 effect -2.26615% (engine -2.2661%); exact 6:5 effect -1.35969% (engine -1.3597%). Sims E (2:1) z 1.28/-1.56, F (1:1) 1.62/-1.38, G (6:5) 0.90/1.36, K (7:5) 0.35/0.40, S (6:5) 0.51/0.11, T (6:5) -0.23/0.13 (engine/independent).

Same-seed 1.25 control diff +0.0087%. Net +0.0756%; predicted (1.35 - 4/3) * 4.5323% = +0.0755%. See the findings.
20. ✔ **Insurance and even money in every hole-card rule (offered in ENHC/OBO too); insured hands win 2:1 on half a bet** — engine and independent sims with insurance always taken vs exact + (4/52)(1.5 pT - 0.5). Q (peek LS), R (ENHC), S (OBO 6:5). Engine 1 spot z -0.12, 1.68, 0.51; engine 3 spots z -0.80, 0.57, -0.47 (seat B 0.60, 1.43, -0.48); independent z -0.38, 1.12, 0.11. CA Insure = Insurance && pT > 1/3 (Combinatorial.cs:305); sim insures only when insOn (Simulator.cs:614).
21. ✔ **Insurance offered = false** — C#/Rust/WASM parity case 14 (2D H17 LS, pen 0, Insurance off); code reading. Identical tallies in all three (sim hash 39C9B8EBE53764E5). The insurance on/off effect off the top is +0.0000%, as theory says (pT = 16d/(52d-1) < 1/3).
22. ✔ **Insurance combined with early surrender (CA adds insurance EV to hands that early-surrender; the sim does not insure surrendered hands)** — code reading (Combinatorial.cs:442 vs Simulator.cs:614-615). Differs only when Insure is true (pT > 1/3), which never happens off the top. Per-count CA PlayerEV is NaN with a count model, so no displayed number is affected.
23. ✔ **Penetration to cut card: cut = round(N*pen), capped at N - min(N/3, 8+6*spots), floor 10; Penetration=0 means reshuffle every round** — code reading (Types.cs:70-79, types.rs:127-141: C# banker's Math.Round = Rust round_ties_even); table printed by v3/Edge.exe; parity case 14. Cut table, e.g. 1D 75-95%: 38 (1 spot), 35 (2-7 spots); 2D 90%: 90/84/78/72/70/70/70; 6D 95%: 296/292/286/280/274/268/262; 8D 75%: 312 for all. Pen 0 gives cut 1: parity case 14 has shoes = 1,000,000 for 1,000,000 rounds. The cut check runs at round start, so the last round finishes past the cut (Simulator.cs:590).
24. ✔ **Burn card counts toward the cut position; the sim only burns at a shuffle** — code reading (Shuffle -> DrawHidden when burn; pos includes the burned card). Simulator.cs:419-426, sim.rs equivalent. Parity cases 3 and 13 (burn off) are identical in C#, Rust and WASM.

SimPage.cs:107 and SimPage.svelte:70 show 'cut card at X of Y'. Describe() still shows the nominal 'pen 80%', which is accurate as the setting.

Discards are reshuffled like a dealer would, so the EV is unaffected.

LS +0.0727, ES +0.6273, RSA +0.0689, HSA +0.1805, SP2 -0.0542, no split -0.5687, D9-11 -0.0961, D10-11 -0.1921, ENHC -0.1117, OBO 0, 6:5 -1.3597, 1 deck +0.5489, 2 decks +0.2120, 8 decks -0.0266. The Svelte select behaviour with an unlisted value was not verified.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- Index generation (BinMoments: last = round(N*Penetration), Strategy.cs:152 / strategy.rs:340) and MidDepthDecks (also used by AppState.MetricFor for preset ramps) use the nominal penetration. The simulator uses CutCard(spots), which caps penetration at about 67-73% in 1-deck games and at 67-87% in 2-deck games with several spots, e.g. 1D 80% 3 spots: nominal 42 vs cut 35. The last round deals past the cut, so the true decision depth lies between the two. The effect on indices and ramps was not re-measured in this attempt; an earlier attempt's 16 v T data (not re-run) suggested the nominal value gives the better index there.
- The web GamePage <select> bound to an unlisted payout or split count read from a file was not checked in a browser; it probably shows a blank or the first option while the engine uses the file value.
- Rules have no 'early surrender vs 10 only' or 'double after split aces' variants; these are modelling scope choices, not defects.

### 12. Parity between C#, Rust, WebAssembly and TypeScript; test coverage

_Scope as the reviewer stated it: parity_

I re-ran the parity checks from the earlier attempts' harnesses (rebuilt from cd7ff40) and added new ones. The parity grid has 276 configs: 23 counts × 12 rule sets, 1 to 8 decks, peek/ENHC/OBO. C#, native Rust and the shipped WASM agree on every decision, index list, key and chart code. They also agree, bit for bit, on all app.rs helpers (factor, correlations, index ranges, presets, RampForRules) and on 10 extra seeded simulations. Floating-point values differ only in the last 1-3 ulps, from exp/ln/pow. The smallest decision margin is 1.1e-7, so these differences cannot change a play. I found no math parity defect with money impact. The findings are small: (1) IRC values typed with more decimals than the field shows are used differently by the two apps, and the C# app shows a rounded value it does not use; (2) a reversed index range gives an empty index list in both apps while the strategy uses 49 indices (the fixture hides this); (3) the test suites cannot detect wrong decisions or EV errors of 0.01-0.03%, which is how the confirmed 1-2 deck problem got through; (4) num.rs rounds exact 16th-digit ties half-to-even, unlike .NET (no realistic input reaches it).

1. ✔ **Map what the Oracle fixtures cover** — Code reading of tools/Oracle.cs (RuleCases, CountCases, StrategyCases, SimCases, AnalysisFixture). ca.json: about 17 rule sets (8/6/5/4/2/1 decks, S17/H17, DAS/noDAS, none/LS/ES, SP1-4, RSA, HSA, ENHC, ENHC_OBO, 6:5, 7:5, 5:4, 2:1, D9-11, D10-11, pen 0/0.625/0.83, no burn, no insurance) plus an Omega II count-model eval. counts.json: all presets plus 3 variants × 3 rule sets (scale, imbalance, level, IRC, tag text, key, MetricValue for every DeckEstimation). strategy.json: 17 cases (Full/I18/I18Fab4/Basic; Hi-Lo, KO, Red Seven, Omega II exact/round, Halves quarter/truncate, custom red tags, RC fractional IRC, ENHC, CSM, overrides, reversed range). sim.json: 16 seeded runs (1-7 spots, CSM, OBO ES no burn, 7:5 no insurance, no splits, seed near 2^64). analysis.json: Evaluate/rows/BankrollFor/Optimize (Wong, Kelly), Phi, PhiInv, TripRuin, ProbLoss. Not covered: engine-wasm app.rs (count-quality numbers, presets, RampForRules, ranges), most counts in Full mode, 3/4/7-deck sims, the WASM target itself, and DescribeIndices with the raw (reversed) range.
2. ✔ **Rust fixture-test tolerances** — Code reading of web/engine/tests/*.rs and common/mod.rs. CA/analysis use TOL=1e-12 (absolute, or relative for values above 1: rel(want)=1e-12*max(|want|,1)). Basic/bin EV 1e-14, bin_tc_eq and mid depth exact (0.0), strategy flags compared by FNV hash (exact), sim tallies exact (c.eq). That is far tighter than any meaningful error.
3. ✔ **Shipped calcbj.wasm is built from the current sources** — sha256 of web/app/src/lib/engine/calcbj.wasm against a fresh cargo build --release --target wasm32-unknown-unknown of web/engine-wasm. Both c1fc46dc750c5034af40457e6e105bd8d60c1a09bfdfb3eab9d6d24fd10d5427. web/app/src-tauri has no calcbj-engine dependency (grep), so the desktop and Android apps run only the WASM engine; native Rust is used only by cargo test.
4. ✔ **All C# fixtures replayed through the shipped WASM (cargo test only covers native)** — wasm_fixtures.mjs re-run (JSON ops of calcbj.wasm against web/engine/tests/fixtures). ca 48,834/48,834 bit-identical; counts 432/432; strategy 2,162 compared, 2,127 bit-identical, 35 within 2.27e-13 abs (4.8e-16 rel) on per-bin cellEV sums; sim 128/128 bit-identical (500k-round chunks, worker 0); analysis 2,037 compared, 2,024 bit-identical, 13 within 4.2e-16 rel. 0 failures.
5. ✔ **Strategy grid: C# vs native Rust** — ParityGrid.exe (C# engine) vs parity-rs native.exe over 276 FullIndices configs (23 counts incl. all presets plus Exact/Round, Quarter/Truncate, FullDeck/Floor, Zen Exact/Floor, custom thirds, RC fractional IRC, KO as TC, Halves red 9; 1-8 decks; peek 230/ENHC 23/OBO 23; LS 92/ES 46/none 138; BJ 3:2, 6:5, 7:5); compare_grid.py. key, basicEV, basic flags, insure, binTcEq, table flags, index lists equal in 276/276; 9,798 bins with equal flags/EV/insure. Unseen moments 55,186/55,476 bit-identical (453 at 1 ulp, 110 at 2, 9 at 3; 5 near-zero values ~1e-20). cellEV max |diff| 1.33e-15.
6. ✔ **Strategy grid: C# vs WASM (parity build), and native vs WASM** — wasm_grid.mjs strategy (parity_rs.wasm, same engine sources) and compare_grid.py. Decisions, keys, index lists and basic EV equal in 276/276; unseen 50,280/55,476 bit-identical (up to 10 ulp, 27 near-zero); cellEV max |diff| 2.44e-15. Native vs WASM: 0 of 276 configs identical in every float field (libm last bits), but every decision is identical.
7. ✔ **Shipped WASM over the whole grid** — shipped_grid.mjs (strategy and chart ops of calcbj.wasm against the C# per-bin flags). Index lists identical 276/276, basic EV bit-identical 276/276, chart codes identical at 9,798/9,798 counts.
8. ✔ **Decision robustness against last-bit differences** — margins.py: smallest |EV(best) - EV(second)| per bin over the C# grid. Smallest margin 1.089e-7 (Uston SS 6D, count -25); 0 bins below 1e-8, 11 below 1e-6. The largest cross-implementation cellEV difference is 2.4e-15, so the margin is about 4e7 times larger.
9. ⚠ **libm last-bit differences (exp/ln/pow) between .NET, native Rust and WASM** — gen_libm_args.py (185,752 arguments incl. 115,576 real BinMoments exp arguments), LibmNet.exe, native.exe libm, wasm libm, cmp_libm.py. .NET vs native: exp 524/115,576 BinMoments args, 205/40,000 exp, 26/25,014 ln, 15/5,162 pow differ. .NET vs WASM: 10,316/115,576, 3,654/40,000, 1,480/25,014, 473/5,162. All are at most 1 ulp. So 'bit-identical to C#' is only true for code paths without libm calls; analysis.rs documents this. No decision is affected (see margins).
10. ✔ **BinMoments 'never reached' threshold (w <= 1e-12) cannot flip on a 1-ulp exp difference** — WThreshold.exe (BinMoments weight sum, same arithmetic, over the grid). 27,226 non-empty bins (248 with w < 1e-9). The closest is w = 2.47e-10 (Uston APC 6D, bin 72), |ln(w/1e-12)| = 5.5, a factor of about 247 from the threshold.
11. ✔ **engine-wasm app.rs vs AppState.cs/CountPage.cs helpers** — ParityGrid.exe app mode vs native (calcbj_wasm rlib) vs shipped calcbj.wasm; 1,380 cases (23 counts × 1-8 decks × rule variants); compare_app.py and resized_check.py. Factor, betting correlation and insurance correlation bit-identical 1,380/1,380 (C#=native=WASM), display strings equal; index ranges 1,380 equal; preset ramps 13,800 equal; RampForRules 22,080 equal; a custom ramp with preset 8 turns into preset 3 in all three (1,380/1,380, the same as C# ResetRampRange+ApplyPreset(8)).
12. ✔ **Count-quality formulas from first principles** — Hand computation and code reading of app.rs factor/corr/count_info against CountScale.Factor. Per-code weights 8:2 (tens vs others) = 4:1 in corr (2.0/0.5). Hi-Lo K = 20+20 = 40, so Factor = 1. Insurance EOR -9/4 for tens and +1 for others balances: 16*(-9/4) + 36*1 = 0. EORs come from removing one card from the full shoe, weighted correlation of tag and EOR (Griffin BC/IC definition).
13. ✔ **Count page display of correlations and factor** — corr_display.py: .NET '0.000' (15 digits, then half away from zero) vs JS toFixed(3); '0.##' vs format.ts trim(v,2). 2,760 correlations: 0 display differently; 1,380 factors: 0 differ.
14. ✔ **num.rs dmax/dmin vs .NET Framework Math.Max/Min** — NetNum.exe vs num.exe (same bodies as num.rs) on 64 special pairs (±0, NaN, ±inf, ±1, 1e-300). Identical after treating every NaN as NaN (15 raw differences are only NaN payloads fff8… vs 7ff8…). Ordered ±0 results are equal bit for bit (Max(+0,-0) = -0 in both).
15. ⚠ **round_even_i32 vs (int)Math.Round** — NetNum.exe vs num.exe on 88 values (halves -20..20, 0.49999999999999994, 0.5000000000000001, 2147483646.5, -2147483647.5, ...); call-site reading (sim.rs:220,225,348,370,371). 1 differs: 0.5000000000000001 gives 0 in .NET Framework (its floor(x+0.5) tie test is wrong) and 1 in Rust (correct). Unreachable: the arguments are tag*scale, IRC*scale and BJ*20, which CountSystem.Scale()'s Whole() keeps within 1e-9 of an integer. The only exception is the scale-60 fallback, which needs non-decimal tags. Also by documented semantics: for NaN or |x| >= 2^31, Rust 'as i32' saturates (NaN gives 0) while C# gives int.MinValue. Only reachable with NaN or huge IRC/tags, which neither UI produces.
16. ⚠ **TcRounding::Round / EstimateDecks: f64::round vs Math.Round(AwayFromZero)** — Rust native/WASM vs .NET on edge values (run2/round libm table); RoundReach.exe over 800,740,080 (estimation, scale 1..60, cards 0..416, rc ±4000) combinations. Native x86_64-pc-windows-gnu f64::round(±0.49999999999999994) returns ±1 (mingw libm); .NET and WASM return ±0. In metric_value the true count equals ±0.49999999999999994 in only 24 of 800M combinations, all at scales 13/26/39/52 with Exact estimation. Scales derived from tags (thirds, 0.05) divide 60, so these are unreachable unless an IRC like 0.0769230769 is typed. 8,140,900 exact halves round away from zero in all three. The shipped WASM is unaffected; only cargo test runs native.
17. ✘ **dotnet_general / dotnet_fixed vs .NET formatting** — 285,368 values (gen_values.py: specials, decimal ties at 1-5 digits, k/3, k/7, k/52, random magnitudes 1e-10..1e20, neighbours of ties) through NetNum.exe vs num.exe; num_diff_analysis.py; extra small-value tie file (run3tie). G 285,044/285,368 identical; '0.#' 285,052; '0.##'/'0.###'/'0.####' 285,044. All 1,612 differences are exact ties at the 16th significant digit: Rust rounds them half-to-even, .NET half away from zero (e.g. 3777081272337.625 gives …337.62 vs …337.63). Small-value repro: 0.5000152587890625 gives 0.500015258789062 (Rust) vs 0.500015258789063 (.NET). 1e-5 → 1E-05, 0.1+0.2 → 0.3, -0.0 → 0 and 1.2345678901234568e17 → 1.23456789012346E+17 all match. Reported as a nit.
18. ✔ **Keys and texts on real inputs** — sample_key.mjs (shipped WASM vs the C# Windows sample .bjev); the key fields of the grid and fixtures. simKey equal for the sample (2D 6:5 RC Halves-like count, override 'Pair 10 6 Split >=4.5'); RulesText equal; 276 strategy keys and 432 count texts/keys equal.
19. ✔ **BasicEV parity outside the fixtures (sample rules 2D S17 DAS 6:5)** — SampleEv.exe (current C#) vs shipped WASM. C# HouseEdgeOffTop -0.015672060379474488 = WASM -0.015672060379474488 (bit-identical). The .bjev stores -0.015539859595270064, the old CA total-dependent value; that staleness falls under the accepted 'no engine version in the results key'.
20. ✔ **format.ts trim/fixed/pct/money vs .NET display formats** — ts_format2.mjs (format.ts, identical to the copy used) on the same 285,368 values vs .NET 0.#, 0.##, F2, F3, Theme.Money. For |v| < 1e12 every difference is a near-tie (within 1e-9 relative of x.5 at the shown digit): trim1 4,838/245,443, trim2 5,091, fixed2 5,091, fixed3 5,271, money cents 728/191,762. 0 non-tie differences. Differences at |v| >= 1e12 (.NET pads after 15 digits) are not reachable for displayed amounts.
21. ✔ **grouped() vs .NET '#,0'** — grouped_check.mjs plus grep of every grouped() call site. 32,865 differences, all '-0' (Intl) vs '0' (.NET) for negatives that round to 0. All call sites (n0, hoursN0, bankroll/winPerHour when > 0, bankroll/unit, b/maxBet, tripHours, shoes, bin n, hours) pass non-negative values.
22. ✔ **RiskPage TS formulas vs C# AnalysisResult** — ts_risk.mjs: bankrollFor(ror) and the ROR curve in JS on shipped-WASM evaluate results vs C# BankrollForRor/RorForBankroll in analysis.json. 105 values, 102 bit-identical, max relative difference 1.67e-16. Trip loss, ruin and percentiles call engine probLoss/tripRuin/phiInv, which the fixtures cover.
23. ✔ **Other TS vs C# page formulas** — Code reading: SimPage per-bin sd/corr, linear ramp builder, tag snapping, ShortIndex, sweep values, ramp resize, ramp/index reset triggers. SimPage: TS v = max(0, m2 - mean^2), corr = (cov - mean^2)/v, the same as C# (Var <= 0 gives 0 in both). Linear ramp ResultsPage.svelte:121 = Dialogs.cs:115. snapTag = CountSystem.Snap (thirds within 0.005, else 0.05; TS roundEven ties-to-even checked for ±0.5, ±1.5, ±2.5). ShortIndex: C# ToEven vs TS half-up, but thresholds are always integer bins (strategy.rs:534-587), so no difference. Sweep: same accumulation; Math.Round(v,4) vs Math.round(v*1e4)/1e4 differ only on 5th-decimal ties, which the inputs don't have. resizeRamp = BetRamp.Resize. Reset conditions on a deck change (RC or unbalanced) and on a count change are the same in both apps.
24. ✔ **Extra seeded simulations outside the fixtures** — SimParity.exe (C#, single thread, seeded) vs shipped WASM simPrepare+sim_run in 150,000-round chunks (wasm_sim_extra.mjs). 10/10 identical in every tally (400k rounds each): Hi-Opt II 3D, Omega II 7D I18F4 5 spots, Zen 4D ENHC-OBO ES RSA HSA, Red Seven 1D, Omega II quarter/truncate 5D 7:5 7 spots, Canfield Expert 1D (13 fallbacks), custom thirds 6D ENHC, KO as TC 2D 6:5 D10-11, Uston APC 8D I18, Halves red 9 2D.
25. ⚠ **EngineTest 'Published' tolerance 0.03%** — Code reading and arithmetic. References are rounded to 0.01% (-0.43, -0.40, -0.62), so a true deviation of up to about 0.035% passes, which covers part of the 'major' band (0.01-0.05%). Only 6D/8D are checked, where the CA approximation is smallest; there are no 1-2 deck references.
26. ⚠ **EngineTest offtop |z| <= 3.5 (2e9 rounds, SE about 0.0026%)** — Power calculation. A 0.01% bias gives z = 3.85, detected with about 64% probability per game; a 0.005% bias about 6%. The family-wise false alarm over 11 games is about 0.5%. Adequate for EV given the decisions, but it compares the simulator with the CA under the CA's own play table, so it cannot detect suboptimal decisions.
27. ✔ **EngineTest regress <= 4 SE** — Analytic SE estimate (Hi-Lo 6D, n = m/2 = 154 unseen, 400k samples). Ten slope b = -0.400 per unit of tag sum; residual var about 6.96, Var(x) about 59.6, SE(b) = 5.4e-4, about 0.14% of the slope. 4 SE is about 0.5% of the slope, roughly 0.05 count at an index of +10. The max over 10 ranks gives a false alarm of about 6e-4. Tight enough.
28. ⚠ **Rust threaded_simulation_agrees_with_analysis (40M rounds, |z| < 4)** — Power calculation. Per-round SD about 1.15 units, so SE = 0.018% and 4 SE = 0.073%. It detects only biases of 0.07% or more (critical size). Rust sim correctness rests on the bit-identical seeded fixtures and the 10 extra configs above, not on this test.
29. ✘ **DescribeIndices with a reversed index range (fixture normalizes, apps do not)** — wasm_fixtures.mjs note re-run, plus reading Strategy.cs:245-246, strategy.rs:203-204, CountPage.cs:540/563, lib.rs:227, CountPage.svelte:220/381-397. For 6D H17 LS Hi-Lo Full with range 4..-3, Build swaps to -3..4 and the normalized C# DescribeIndices lists 49 index plays; the WASM strategy op called with the raw range lists 0, and the C# CountPage passes the raw range too. Reported as minor finding 2.
30. ✘ **IRC entry: C# NumericUpDown vs web NumberInput** — NudCheck.exe (real WinForms NumericUpDown, DecimalPlaces=1) plus reading NumberInput.fit and CountPage.svelte decimals={1}. C#: typed 0.25 keeps Value 0.25 but shows '0.3'; -2.75 keeps -2.75, shows '-2.8'; 1.04 keeps 1.04, shows '1.0'. Web: fit() = Math.round(v*10)/10, so 0.25 becomes 0.3 and -2.75 becomes -2.7. Reported as minor finding 1.
31. · **Every other C# NumericUpDown field (unit, bankroll, rph, penetration, ramp cells) against web rounding** — not run. Budget. Probably the same pattern as IRC (C# keeps typed decimals, web rounds); listed under open questions.

Open questions the reviewer left:
- The other C# NumericUpDown fields with DecimalPlaces (unit, bankroll, rounds per hour, penetration, sweep from/to/step, override index cells) probably keep typed extra decimals while showing a rounded value, whereas the web rounds them half-up. Each field was not checked; only IRC was verified with a real control.
- When a C# .bjev whose IRC has extra decimals (e.g. 0.25) is opened in the web app, is the value used as is and displayed rounded (the same mismatch in the other direction)? Not checked.
- The Windows sample .bjev in web/app/tests/fixtures stores BasicEV -1.5540% from an older engine; the current engines give -1.5672%. This falls under the accepted 'no engine version in the results key', but the test fixture itself could be regenerated.
- The engine README says 'bit-identical'. In fact C#, native and WASM differ by 1-10 ulp on libm-dependent values; all decisions, keys, indices and sim tallies are identical. The wording might be adjusted.

## Verification of the findings

Each of these re-checked a finding and was told to try to refute it.

### m11 (app-csharp#1, code lens) → confirmed (minor)

Corrected claim: The finding is correct in both C# and the web/WASM app. Refined numbers at 200M rounds for KO going from 6D to 2D with the custom 0-then-1..8 ramp: | Ramp | SCORE | ROR | Win/h | Spread | Played | |---|---|---|---|---|---| | Game page (1–12 preset) | 149.9 | 0.48% | $147 | 1–12 | 100% | | Decks sweep (mapped custom ramp) | 179.2 | 0.125% | $141 | 1–8 | 31% | Same simulation key. Preset ramps (0-7) agree on both paths, and balanced true counts are unaffected, so only custom ramps are hit. Two more triggers on the Count page not named in the finding: - the IRC per-deck field; - for an unbalanced count played by true count, the rounding and deck-estimation controls. These replace a custom ramp even though the ramp rows do not change.

- I tried to refute the finding by tracing the code and by running the real C# pages and the WASM ops. It holds in both implementations, and nothing elsewhere makes up for it. Checklist of what I did: 1. Code trace, C# (reading). GamePage.Save (lines 143-148) runs ResetRampRange, then ResetIndexRange, then ApplyPreset(State.RampPreset), for RC or unbalanced counts whenever the deck count changes. AppState.ApplyPreset line 368 is `if (preset < 0 || preset >= RampPresets.Custom) preset = 3;`. It then sets RampPreset = 3 and overwrites every row. Custom ramps are reachable this way: ResultsPage.MarkCustom sets RampPreset = Custom on a cell edit, Optimise or Linear. SweepPage line 159 instead calls AppState.RampForRules(cs, rules, r2, ramp, preset). RampForRules' own doc comment says a custom ramp is carried over row by row, and the README describes the same for the sweep. So the sweep comment at line 155, 'As on the Game page', is wrong for custom ramps. No compensation exists. MainForm.OnConfigChanged only swaps in cached results. ResultsPage.LoadInputs only shows the new preset ('1–12 spread') while `loading` is set. The Guide and the Game page give no warning. 2. Code trace, web (reading). GamePage.svelte setDecks calls app.decksChanged(), which calls resetRanges(), which calls engine.defaultRamp(rules, count, ramp, rampPreset). That is the WASM op at lib.rs:252-257: reset_ramp_range, then app::apply_preset. At app.rs:87 the preset falls back to 3 unless it is 0..7, and the returned preset 3 is stored. ResultsPage.svelte sets rampPreset = CUSTOM_PRESET on edits (lines 71, 76, 112, 127). sweep.svelte.ts:139 uses rampForRules. CountPage.svelte setConversion calls resetRanges() for RC or unbalanced counts. 3. Executed: the real GamePage, driven through its private decks ComboBox (the real Save handler runs). KO, 6D, custom ramp with units(v)=min(8,max(0,v+6)), switched to 2D. The ramp became preset 3, matching the finding exactly. The sweep's call gives the mapped ramp. Index range (-15..13) and sim key are identical, so it is the same configuration. 4. Executed: parity for preset ramps. For KO, Red Seven and Uston SS with presets 0-7 (24 cases), going from 6D to 2D, the Game page and RampForRules give identical ramps. Only custom ramps differ. 5. Executed: Hi-Lo (balanced, true count) with a custom ramp, 6D to 2D. The ramp is unchanged and stays preset 8, as intended. 6. Executed: custom ramps with sit-outs and 2-hand rows for Red Seven, Uston SS and KO, 6D to

### m11 (app-csharp#1, domain lens) → confirmed (minor)

Corrected claim: Confirmed as stated, with these refinements: (1) At 25M rounds the gap is SCORE 150.7 vs 180.0, ROR 0.47% vs 0.12%, spread 1–12 vs 1–8, rounds played 100% vs 31%. The 10M figures in the finding are within sampling noise of these. (2) The reset is "silent" only in the sense that nothing warns the user. The Results page's preset box changes to "1–12 spread", and the custom ramp cannot be recovered (switching back to 6D gives the 1–12 preset). (3) The scope is a bit wider than stated: - all running counts (KO, Red Seven, Uston SS) and unbalanced true counts; - any deck change (1, 4 and 8 decks too); - on the Count page, IRC and metric changes, plus deck-estimation and rounding changes for unbalanced true counts; - both the C# app and the web app. (4) The proposed fix is domain-sound: - For an IRC change with a running count, shifting the ramp by the IRC change is exact. For an unbalanced true count the shift depends on depth, so a Hi-Lo-equivalent mapping is needed there instead. - For a deck change, RampForRules is the app's documented approximation (it keeps KO's +4 pivot fixed). It does not preserve the advantage exactly: in 2D it starts betting about one count step later than the 6D ramp, measured by EV.

- I tried to refute the finding and could not. Every factual part holds in both the C# app and the web app (the WASM path). What happens today: 1. On the Game page, a deck change with a running count or an unbalanced count runs ResetRampRange, ResetIndexRange and then ApplyPreset(RampPreset). 2. ApplyPreset turns preset 8 (custom) into 3 and overwrites the units and hands of every row. 3. Custom ramps are common in normal use. ResultsPage.MarkCustom sets preset 8 after any cell edit, after Optimise and after Linear. 4. The decks sweep instead uses AppState.RampForRules / ramp_for_rules, which carries a custom ramp over row by row. The README promises this for the sweep ("your ramp mapped by Hi-Lo equivalent"), and SweepPage claims "As on the Game page". 5. Git history explains the mismatch. The Game page's ApplyPreset dates from the first commit (74d37a4). RampForRules was added in the audit fix (22cfb54), but only to the sweep. UiTest has no check that a custom ramp survives. The same simulation shows two different results. After a decks sweep, switching the Game page to 2D gives the same simulation key the sweep stored ("...#I18Fab4|-15|13#spots=3"). MainForm.OnConfigChanged then loads that cached simulation, so one simulation is shown with SCORE about 151 on the Results page and about 180 in the sweep grid. Count page: changing the IRC (and also the metric, or deck estimation / rounding for an unbalanced true-count system) resets a custom ramp to 1–12 in both apps. Is the finding's idea of "correct" actually correct? (domain lens) - IRC edit: changing the IRC by d changes every running count by exactly d for the same cards. So shifting the custom running-count ramp by d is the exact carry-over, and the reset throws that information away for no reason. I checked this identity with exact fractions. - Deck change: mapping by Hi-Lo equivalent at mid-depth fits KO theory. With the standard IRC, the true-count equivalent at RC +4 (the KO pivot) is exactly 4 for 1, 2, 6 and 8 decks, so the pivot maps onto itself. The key counts quoted from memory (6D −4, 8D −6) map to 2D RC 1.33 and 1.50, against the published 2D key of +1. - But the mapping is an approximation. It does not preserve the advantage exactly. In 25M-round simulations, the 6D ramp starts betting at RC −5, where the per-hand EV is −0.05%. The mapped 2D ramp starts at RC +1 (+0.61%) and sits out RC 0 (+0.10%). So the mapped ramp is about one 2D count step more conservative. This does not weaken the fin

### m13 (app-web#1, code lens) → confirmed (minor)

Corrected claim: Confirmed as described, with two corrections. What is true: CountPage.svelte copies app.strategy.overrides into `rows` once when it mounts, and has no effect that copies them again. Opening a .bjev while the Count page is shown leaves CountPage mounted, because loadScenario never changes app.page and the {#if} branch key stays the same. This applies to the sidebar, Ctrl+O, the More sheet and the apps' dialog alike. The table then keeps the previous rows, while the chart, "Index plays in use", the "+N custom" status and the sim key show the file's plays. The next Add row, Add published, cell edit or ✕ writes the stale rows into app.strategy.overrides. That replaces the file's custom plays or brings back the previous scenario's. Leaving the page and returning fixes the table. The Windows app reloads its grid on open (Reloaded → LoadFromState). Correction 1, Clear: Clear only writes [], which is its intended effect, so it does not corrupt anything. Its one fault is that it is wrongly disabled when the stale table is empty. Correction 2, the SCENARIO: with windows-sample.bjev, the dropped T,T v 6 split ≥4.5 is also dead in the Windows app's 23 rows. The published T,T v 6 split ≥4 row comes after it, and overrides apply in list order, last wins. So both apps simulate the same decisions; only the overrides list, the sim key (Windows keeps '1Pair106Split>=4.5') and the '+22' versus '+23 custom' label differ. Two cases show a real change in strategy: - A file play that is not in the published list, e.g. T,T v 7 split ≥4.5: after Add published the web app plays the computed +11 index and the Windows app plays +5, with charts differing at RC +5 to +10. - Any cell edit after opening, e.g. the 22 rows of the previous scenario replacing the file's single play.

### m8 (counts#2, numeric lens) → confirmed (minor)

Corrected claim: Confirmed as stated, with these refinements: (a) In C# this is not a process crash. The "Unexpected error" dialog appears (Application.ThreadException). The count's IRC is already stored, but the ramp and index range are not updated. (b) C# can also be left with an unusable ramp. When the clamped range ends exactly one row past, BetRamp.Create silently makes a 0-row ramp: at 6D RC that is IRC 112.3–113.2 and −131.9 to −131.0, which the ±1 spin arrows always pass through. After that, every reset, ramp-row change and evaluation throws IndexOutOfRangeException until a file is opened or the app restarts. The empty-old-ramp guard is therefore needed in both C# and Rust, alongside clamp-first. (c) For a balanced TC count, neither app resets the ramp on an IRC change. The +200 TC 1D case fails only on a following metric or tag change. Unbalanced TC counts fail immediately (KO TC 1D, IRC +70). (d) Web: the held empty ramp makes every later resetRanges trap, even on replacement instances. Besides a restart, opening a scenario repairs it, and so does Results-page rows plus the linear ramp. A restart does not repair the index range left at 112..100: it is autosaved and changes 14 of 540 chart cells at RC 0. (e) The native abort exit code is 3, not 127.

- Read the counts#2 section and checked every cited line: AppState.cs 297-306 (fix-up at 304, clamp at 305) and 351-356; Analysis.cs 31-39; app.rs 52-67 (fix-up at 59, clamp at 62); lib.rs 252-258 (the filter units.len()==rows() lets a 0-row ramp through); analysis.rs 44/57/65. All match.
- Independent Python model (explicit 52-card deck, SD-ratio factor, dMid = D(2-pen)/2): the 6 claimed scenarios give 138..100 (-37 rows), -100..-120 (-19), 108..100 (-7), -100..-120 (-19), 103..100 (-2), 317..100 (-216). Controls: +112 6D gives 100..100 (1 row); 13/deck 8D gives 89..100. Clamp-first fix gives 99..100 / -100..-99. File: C:\Users\bene\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\dc31a4ef150e96e7\f8128c32-48f7-4dc1-9bfc-570a66aede8c\scratchpad\mathcheck\agents\verify2\counts#2-numeric\ramp_range_indep.py
- Threshold scan over all 13 presets, RC/TC, 1-8 decks. Hi-Lo RC fails from IRC +102.9 (1D) to +116 (8D) and from -106 to -141, all reachable with the base field alone. Hi-Lo TC: +65/-68.2 at 1D, +130 at 2D, +195 at 3D, +260 at 4D (base + per-deck needed); not reachable from 5D.
- Built CalcBJ.exe from the 25 repo sources into scratch\bin with csc. AppState via reflection: all 6 scenarios throw OverflowException (BetRamp.Create <- Resize <- ResetRampRange). Ramp and index range stay unchanged, and IRC back to 0 recovers. KO played as TC, 1D, IRC +70 also throws.
- C# UI limits, read from CountPage.cs and checked live on the NumericUpDowns: ircBase -200..200, ircPerDeck -20..20, 1 decimal, increment 1. Typing 999 is clamped to 200.
- C# CountPage handlers via reflection, IRC typed 0 -> 150 directly: OverflowException out of SaveConversion. Count IRC is now 150, but the ramp stays -12..30 and the index range stays -38..38 (inconsistent state). Back to 0 works. Per-deck +20 at 6D also throws OverflowException.
- C# message-loop test with the app's own exception setup (CatchException + ThreadException): the exception reached the handler once and the loop kept running. So it is the 'Unexpected error' dialog, not a process termination.
- Additional C# finding: stepping the IRC 108 -> 116 at 6D RC, IRC 113 silently gives a 0-row ramp 101..100 (lo = hi+1, so new double[0]); 114 and above throw OverflowException. Back to 0, a metric change, Results-page Resize(-3,8) and Analysis.Evaluate then all throw IndexOutOfRangeException, and the index range is stuck at 75..100.
- C#, balanced Hi-Lo TC at 1D with IRC +200: no reset on the IRC change itself. A following tag edit (7 -> +0.5) or metric change throws OverflowException.
- Rust native scratch crate through calcbj_wasm::call: defaultRamp returns {min:138,max:100,units:[],hands:[]} and the other 5 scenarios also give empty ramps (+113 gives 101..100 empty; +112 gives 100..100 with 1 row). indexRange gives 112..100. applyPreset and evaluate both return 'the bet ramp rows do not match its count range'. rampForRules returns an empty 135..100.
- Rust native: defaultRamp with IRC 0 and the held ramp panics at web/engine/src/analysis.rs:58:19 with 'index out of bounds: the len is 0 but the index is 0'; the process aborts with exit code 3, not 127. Choosing KO, or the boundary ramp 101..100, panics the same way.
- WASM: built a release wasm32 module (panic=abort) from an identical copy of the sources. In Node, using the app's own EngineInstance (wasm.ts) plus the engine.ts replacement logic and the setConversion/resetRanges flow: IRC 150 is accepted; applyRampPreset throws an Error; IRC 0 gives 'RuntimeError: unreachable' with broken=true; the held ramp is unchanged; choosing Zen traps again (3 instances in total). Same result with the repo's prebuilt calcbj.wasm.
- Repair paths in the web app: a restart and loadScenario both build the ramp from null and give a valid ramp. Results-page rows alone leave null units (applyPreset fails with 'invalid type: null'), but adding the linear-ramp tool gives a valid ramp. So 'only after a restart' is overstated.
- Side effect, web: the index range 112..100 left behind when resetRanges traps is autosaved and kept on restart. With IRC 0 it changes 14 of 540 chart cells at RC 0 and 11 at RC +6 (stale_index.mjs).
- Web UI limits in CountPage.svelte: NumberInput min -200 max 200 decimals 1, and min -20 max 20 decimals 1; NumberInput.fit clamps to these. The engine's count() accepts |ircBase| <= 10000 and |ircPerDeck| <= 1000, so the extreme values pass.
- Re-ran the earlier reviewer's EdgeIrc.exe: identical ranges and OverflowExceptions.
- Repo untouched: git status clean, and no files under web/engine or web/engine-wasm newer than my scratch work.

### m3 (dealer#1, numeric lens) → partly (minor)

Corrected claim: **What is right.** DealerDag.Evaluate and dealer.rs evaluate skip negative remaining counts (comp_r − m_r < 0) as draws but keep them in the n − k denominator. On the fractional compositions of the per-count (CountModel/Regress) analysis, the dealer outcome probabilities therefore sum to more than 1. C# and Rust are bit-identical. Whole-number shoes, which are used for basic strategy and the exact off-the-top EV, are exact. The finding's examples reproduce exactly: the 0.5-deuce shoe sums to 1.000449, and 1D H17 Hi-Lo +10, up 2 v 2,2 sums to 1.0017293. **Worst single-deal sum within default index ranges:** | Decks | 75% penetration | 95% penetration | |---|---|---| | 1 | 1.0020 (KO) | 1.0052 (KO); Red Seven 1.0025 | | 2 | 1.00004 | 1.0003 | | 6 | 1 + 5e-11 | 1 + 2.3e-6 | The quoted Red Seven 1.0037 is bin +8, outside that count's default range of −7..+5. **Effect of the positive-part renormalisation on per-count cell EVs at default ranges:** - 1 deck: up to 0.0012 for Hi-Lo at 75% (3,3 v 3 split +0.17805 → +0.17687), 0.0018 for KO at 75%, 0.0064 at 95%. - 2 decks: up to 3.3e-5 at 75% and 3.8e-4 at 95%. **Index texts.** - Nothing changes in 2-deck games or in the finding's 36 combinations. - In 1-deck games with the default rules, near-tie index texts do change at default ranges: 8 of 26 tables at 75% penetration, 41 of 130 at 50–95%. Examples: - Uston APC: 10 v A double +9 → +10. - Canfield Expert: 9,9 v 5 no split −9 → −8. - Revere RPC: 16 v 2 hit −16 → −18. - Hi-Lo at 95% only: 8,8 v T surrender +3 → +2. - Every flip has an action-EV gap of about 1e-4 or less. **The fix.** It changes nothing for whole numbers. It is not more accurate than the current engine: against the exact average over whole-number unseen compositions, both versions miss stand EVs by about 0.0015 on average, so the expected-composition approximation dominates.

- 1. Independent recursion on hand-made shoes, compared with C# DealerDag and Rust dealer_outcomes. My Python recursion is written from the dealer rules (top-down memo, not the DAG). Upcard 2, S17, shoe minus the upcard with 0.5 deuces: engine-rule sum 1.0004486 in all three implementations, per-outcome |diff| <= 1e-15, and C# and Rust give the same digits. H17: 1.0004493. Upcard A, 0.5 aces: 1.0002735. Upcard 6, four ranks at 0.5: 1.0011275. Whole-number control (3 deuces): sum 1 within 4e-16 in every mode.
- 2. Renormalised (positive-part) rule: my Python and my patched C# DealerDag agree to about 1e-15 and sum to 1 within 2e-16 in all 5 cases. A mixture over whole-number compositions also sums to 1 and differs from the renormalised values by 4e-5 (single fractional rank) to 1.4e-3 (four fractional ranks).
- 3. Single-state arithmetic for the 0.5-deuce shoe: after a deuce hole card, the outgoing probabilities sum to 48 / 47.5 = 1.0105, which confirms the mass-creation mechanism.
- 4. Finding's count-model scenario, 1D H17 75% Hi-Lo bin +10, up 2 v 2,2. Engine BinMoments gives Unseen 26.0059 and UnseenTags -5.4990. My regression, re-implemented from the documented formula, matches the engine's Regress exactly (max diff 0). Dealer sum: 1.0017293347 from my code vs 1.0017293346947 from the engine. Renormalised: 1.0000000000. Shifts: bust +8.8e-4; stand EV +4e-5 on 16, +4.9e-4 on 18, +8.4e-4 on 19, +1.18e-3 on 20.
- 5. Sweep of max(sum-1) over all deals, default index ranges (copy of AppState.IndexRangeFor), all 13 presets x H17/S17 x penetration {0.50, 0.65, 0.75, 0.85, 0.95} x {1, 2, 6, 8} decks; about 17 s on 6 threads. 1D: 1.09e-3 at 50%, 1.68e-3 at 65%, 2.02e-3 at 75% (KO), 2.99e-3 at 85%, 5.195e-3 at 95% (KO bin +8, up 2 v 3,4); Red Seven at 95% 2.544e-3. 2D: 4.31e-5 at 75% (Revere RPC), 3.18e-4 at 95% (Uston APC). 6D: 4.8e-11 at 75%, 2.3e-6 at 95%. 8D at 95%: 8.9e-7. The six worst compositions were re-evaluated in Python and the excesses match exactly.
- 6. Source of the quoted 1.0037: the earlier reviewer's FracSum gives 3.71e-3 for Red Seven S17 at 95% only at bin +8, which is outside the default range of -7..+5. Inside that range the worst is 2.54e-3, and the peek-conditioned excess is larger still (for example 8.3e-3 for KO 1D 95% at bin 8).
- 7. Original vs my patched engine through StrategyBuilder.Build (full indices, default range) in 260 tables: 1-2 decks x H17/S17 x 5 penetrations x 13 presets, default rules (DAS, SP4, LS); about 19 s. Max |delta cellEV| in 1D: 0.0008 (50%), 0.0012 (65%), 0.0018 (75%, KO 2,2 v 3 split), 0.0029 (85%), 0.0064 (95%, KO T,T v 2 split +0.9901 -> +0.9836). In 2D: 3.3e-5 at 75%, 3.8e-4 at 95%. The finding's example reproduces exactly: 1D S17 75% Hi-Lo +10, 3,3 v 3 split +0.17805 -> +0.17687.
- 8. Index texts in the same 260 tables: no change in any 2D table. 1D index texts change in 41 of 130 tables, 8 of 26 at 75% penetration. Examples: Uston APC 10 v A double +9 -> +10; Canfield 9,9 v 5 no split -9 -> -8; Omega II A,4 v 3 double +6 -> +7; Revere RPC 16 v 2 hit -16 -> -18; Uston SS 7,7 v A 'split at +8 or more' -> 'from +8 to +13'; a new Wong Halves 17 v 9 surrender at +11; Hi-Lo only at 95% (8,8 v T surrender +3 -> +2). In all 60 flipped flags the competing actions are within about 1e-4.
- 9. The finding's own 36-combination set (1D 65% with no DAS, SP2, no surrender; 2D/6D 75%; six counts): 0 index changes; max |delta EV| 0.00091 in 1D, 3.3e-5 in 2D, 0 in 6D. The claim holds within its tested scope.
- 10. Widened-range jobs as the finding ran them (SP2, no DAS, no surrender): its 4 near-tie text changes reproduce. 1D H17 Hi-Lo 3,3 v 5 changes from 'No split at -8 or less' to '-6 or less'; at -6, split -0.10570 vs hit -0.10572 in the original and split -0.10573 after the patch. Red Seven 1D 65% bins +12..+18: max delta EV 0.0096.
- 11. Whole-number invariance of the fix in 20 games (1/2/4/6/8 decks x H17/S17 x {default, ENHC + SP2 + no DAS + no surrender}): flags, CA PlayerEV, exact off-the-top EV and the cellEV checksum are bit-identical between original and patched.
- 12. C# vs Rust on per-bin analysis: Rust strategy::build cell EVs for 1D S17 75% Hi-Lo bins +8..+10 (9 values) are bit-identical to C#, for example the 3,3 v 3 split 0.1780453221514045. Both apps carry the defect.
- 13. Exact reference (Truth.cs): the dealer distribution averaged exactly over all whole-number unseen compositions with hypergeometric weights, conditioned on unseen size and Hi-Lo tag sum (1D H17, n=26, about 45,000 compositions). Compared with dealer(regression composition) under both rules. For 2,2 v 2 at s=-5: truth bust 0.39179; engine +0.00113, renormalised +0.00026; stand-on-20 error -0.0016 (engine) vs -0.0028 (renormalised).
- 14. Batch of 48 one-deck deals (upcards 2, 4, 6, 9, T, A x hands T6, T8, 23, 88 x s in {-5, +3}), 240 peek-conditioned stand EVs on 16-20: mean |error| 0.00148 (engine) vs 0.00149 (renormalised); max 0.0051 vs 0.0051; renormalised closer in 99 of 240. The expected-composition (Jensen) error dominates, and the fix does not improve accuracy.
- 15. Scope check (grep): DealerDag is fed fractional counts only through CountModel/Regress in the app. EoR, basic strategy and off-the-top use whole numbers; ShiftedShoe fractional shoes appear only in Oracle fixtures. The repository git status stayed clean.

### m4 (counts#4, domain lens) → confirmed (minor)

Corrected claim: Both apps compute the betting correlation from effects of removal taken from CAEngine.Evaluate: the strategy is re-optimised for each depleted shoe and the CA uses fixed draw odds. The textbook BC (Griffin, and the EoR definition in Ethier & Lee) uses the full-shoe basic strategy held fixed, with exact probabilities; those EoRs sum to exactly 0. The displayed BC is off by 0.001–0.009: - In 4–8 decks the error comes almost entirely from the approximate CA. Hi-Lo, KO and Red Seven are about −0.0025, Hi-Opt II about +0.003. Exact re-optimised EoRs give the fixed-strategy BC to within 0.0004. - In 1–2 decks, re-optimisation adds to the error. The largest gap found is −0.0090 for KO in 1D S17 LS (shown 0.966 vs 0.975). - The finding's 0.0062 and +1.69%/deck come from no-split games only; with splits the EoR sums reach +1.99%/deck. - The third decimal differs for 10–13 of the 13 presets in every rule set tested, and the second decimal for 1–9. Published two-decimal BCs agree with both methods, except Hi-Opt I. Fix: compute the EoRs with ExactOffTop on depleted shoes using the full-shoe flags; its dealer cache must be keyed by, or cleared for, each shoe. A "re-optimised play" label alone would be wrong for shoe games.

- Read CountPage.cs:472-521 and app.rs:122-191. EoR = Evaluate(shoe-k) - Evaluate(shoe). Evaluate re-optimises the flags with fixed draw odds (Combinatorial.cs:243-448). BC is a centred weighted Pearson over 20 colour codes, shown with 3 decimals in both apps (CountPage.cs:448 {2:0.000}; CountPage.svelte:305 toFixed(3)). It is not used anywhere else.
- Ran the web engine's own entry point (calcbj_wasm::call, op countInfo, native build, CARGO_TARGET_DIR in scratch). 6D H17 DAS LS SP4 gives Hi-Lo 0.962545, KO 0.954625, Zen 0.964075. The finding's scenarios give 1D H17 DOA LS no-split Hi-Lo 0.967 / KO 0.965, and 6D H17 DOA LS no-split 0.964 / 0.958. These match my C# replica of CountPage.Correlations to 4 decimals.
- Exact fixed basic-strategy EoRs from a scratch copy of ExactOffTop patched to deal from a given shoe, with the dealer cache cleared each call. This reproduces the finding: 1D H17 LS no-split EoR(5) +0.6922 vs app +0.7996, Hi-Lo 0.9706, KO 0.9709. 6D H17 LS no-split: Hi-Lo 0.9668, KO 0.9600. App EoR sum +1.693.
- Independent exact CA of my own (MyCA.cs, no engine code; one split computed exactly via exchangeability) on 5 rule sets including SP2+DAS+LS. Max |EoR difference| against the patched ExactOffTop is 5.4e-17. Full-shoe EVs match, e.g. 6D H17 DAS LS SP2 -0.5832860689%. Hi-Lo/KO/Zen BCs are identical.
- Burn-card identity sum_k n_k*EoR_k = 0: the exact fixed EoRs give 1e-17 to 2e-14 (no split / SP2) and <=1e-7 (SP4 resplit approximation). App EoRs sum to +0.136..+1.987%/deck across 32 rule sets. Exact re-optimised EoRs sum to +0.005 (6D) up to +1.72 (1D S17 DAS LS).
- Requested rule set 6D H17 DAS LS SP4, app / fixed / re-optimised: Hi-Lo 0.9625/0.9650/0.9651, KO 0.9546/0.9568/0.9568, Zen 0.9641/0.9652/0.9651, shown as 0.963 vs 0.965, 0.955 vs 0.957, 0.964 vs 0.965. The re-optimisation share is <=0.0001, so the gap is the approximate CA.
- Requested rule set 1D S17 DOA noDAS no surrender SP4: Hi-Lo 0.9661/0.9697/0.9690, KO 0.9752/0.9797/0.9773, Zen 0.9607/0.9643/0.9605. For 1D S17 DAS LS SP4: Hi-Lo 0.9643/0.9685, KO 0.9642/0.9732 (gap 0.0090, of which -0.0080 is re-optimisation), Zen 0.9614/0.9653.
- Scan of 32 rule sets (1/2/6/8 decks x S17/H17 x DAS/noDAS x LS/none, SP4). The third decimal differs for 10-13 of 13 presets in every set; the second decimal for 1-9 of 13. Largest gap 0.0090 (KO, 1D S17 LS). In 6-8 decks the gap is 0.0024-0.0035 and the re-optimisation share is <=0.0004. In 1-2 decks the re-optimisation share is up to 0.0103.
- Literature on the definition. Ethier & Lee 2019 (arXiv 1906.01220, text extracted) define EoR with basic strategy 'optimized for the 39-deck shoe', i.e. the full shoe, held fixed. They cite Griffin Ch. 4 for the tag/EoR correlation as a stand-in for betting efficiency, and Schlesinger for the single-deck convention.
- Griffin's published 1D EoRs (A -.61 ... T -.51 as commonly reproduced; A and T confirmed by a secondary source) sum to -0.12% over 52 cards, which is 0 within +-0.26 rounding. Re-optimised EoRs for that game sum to +0.61 (exact) or +0.99 (app), outside that band. Griffin's values are closer to exact fixed (max deviation 0.022, rms 0.013) than to the app's (0.048, 0.026). Griffin's T -0.51 matches fixed -0.509, not re-optimised -0.483.
- Published 2-decimal BCs (Hi-Lo .97, KO .98, Zen .96, Hi-Opt I .88, Hi-Opt II .91, Omega II .92, Halves .99). Griffin's EoRs give .968/.977/.962/.875/.909/.917/.992. Exact fixed for 1D S17 noDAS gives .970/.980/.964/.876/.912/.918/.991, all 7 matching after rounding. The app gives .966/.975/.961/.874/.911/.916/.992; only Hi-Opt I misses (.87 vs .88). Published precision cannot tell the two apart apart from that one case.
- Counter-reference: the Wizard of Odds 6D EoR table states 'strategy adjustments for the changed deck composition' (bjstrat CDCA). It cannot be reproduced under either convention: EoR(5) .141 vs .1173 exact fixed, .1175 exact re-optimised, .1234 app (6D S17 DAS SP2); shoe sum +0.384% vs +0.005%. In shoe games the convention changes BC by <=0.0004.
- Sensitivity to which basic strategy is held fixed. 1D S17 noDAS played with the 2-deck or 6-deck basic strategy (7 or 9 cells differ): Hi-Lo -0.0015, KO up to -0.0016, Zen up to -0.0041, Hi-Opt I up to -0.0066. 6D default with the 8-deck strategy: identical. With the 2-deck strategy: <=+0.0025. So in 1D the third decimal also depends on the details of the basic strategy.
- Repository untouched: git status is clean. All builds and target directories are in the scratch folder.

### M2 (counts#1, numeric lens) → confirmed (major)

Corrected claim: The finding stands as written. Both apps build every preset row from the row's lower edge, floor(v/F). With the default floored true count this puts Wong Halves rows +1..+6 at 1/2/4/6/8/10 units instead of 2/4/6/8/10/12, and Silver Fox rows +1..+5 at 1/2/4/6/8 instead of 2/4/6/8/10. Zen, Omega II, Hi-Opt I, Hi-Opt II and APC lag by one step on 2–4 rows. Hi-Lo, Canfield and RPC are unchanged. In the 6D H17 DAS LS, 3-spot, $25 scenario this costs: - Halves: about $13.7/h (about $29.9 instead of about $43.7) and 5.6 SCORE (about 17.2 instead of about 22.9). - Silver Fox: about $11/h. - Zen: about $3.1/h. Both apps give identical ramps, and the ramp is re-applied whenever the count changes. Two corrections: 1. Measured against a Hi-Lo baseline of about $39.2/h, the app's Halves comes out about 24% below Hi-Lo, and the corrected ramp about 11% above. The finding's "19% below / 19% above" figures came from a Hi-Lo run that was about 2 SE low. 2. The proposed Representative-based fix covers Floor and Truncate rounding but not Round. There Representative(v) = v, so Halves, Silver Fox and Zen stay one row late (Halves +1..+6 still 1/2/4/6/8/10, while Hi-Lo bets 2..12). Mapping to the nearest Hi-Lo row under the count's own rounding would cover all three settings.

### M1 (ca-core#1, numeric lens) → partly (major)

Corrected claim: Confirmed as written, except for two sentences. Corrected claim: - **Confirmed:** the CA's fixed draw odds and frozen dealer distribution (and, in peek games, ignoring the no-blackjack information) give wrong basic-strategy flags in 1-2 deck games and 8D S17. The cases are 2D S17 11vA, 1D S17 A2v4 and A8v6, 1D S17 LS 16vA, 2D H17 A7v2 and 8D S17 A2v5, plus A,A as soft 12 v 5 (2D) and v 6 (8D) when not splitting. All EVs are exactly as stated. - **Confirmed:** per-cell errors reach 0.011/0.020 (1D), 0.010 (2D) and 0.003 (6D). Hi-Lo indices are off by one bin in 11/24 (1D S17), 10/24 (2D S17) and 3/24 (6D H17) plays. Correcting the flags is worth at most about +0.0027% off the top (Game page +0.159% vs +0.161%). - **Reword the EOR sentence:** the fixed draw odds move the Count-page EORs by at most about 0.011% (1D, the ten; the 5 only 0.0002%) and the Hi-Lo betting correlation by about -0.001. The 0.053% / 0.003 gap is measured against EVs where the player follows the flags after a hit. It comes mainly from the CA assuming the best play after each hit, which the proposed fix keeps. - **Reword the fix note:** conditional draw odds remove about 45-65% of the error for an ace up, but only 10-20% for a ten up.
