### M1 (ca-core#1, domain lens) → confirmed (major)

Corrected claim: The finding stands as stated, in both C# and Rust: - Evaluate freezes the player's draw odds and the dealer distribution after the first three cards and ignores the peek information. - Against the exact total-dependent strategy, which is also the Wizard of Odds published chart, this gives wrong plays in 1D S17 (A,2 v 4; A,8 v 6; 16 v A with LS), 2D S17 (11 v A) and 2D H17 (A,7 v 2). - In 8D S17, A,2 v 5 is also wrong, but it is a near-tie worth 0.00003 per hand. - 1D and 2D indices move by one count in about half the common plays. - The money effect is at most about 0.003% per round. The finding understates the scope. SplitEV uses the same fixed odds, so with DAS the engine also fails to split: - 6,6 v 7 and 7,7 v 8 in 2D (S17 and H17); - 9,9 v A in 1D H17. Splitting is better in all three by the published values and by the engine's own ExactOffTop. The original scan missed these because it reused the engine's split EVs as its reference. A fix should cover pair cells as well as hit/double/surrender. The user-facing docs ("optimal", "perfect basic strategy") overstate the current method.

- Read finding ca-core#1 and the code: Combinatorial.cs:285-363 sets p[r]=sp[r]/Np after upcard and two cards; FillDP/Dbl reuse one frozen dealer distribution; no conditioning of draw odds on 'no dealer blackjack'. ca.rs mirrors it. The approximation is exactly as described.
- Published charts (Wizard of Odds chart images read in the browser): 1D S17 A,2 v 4 Dh, A,8 v 6 Ds, 16 v A Rh; 1D H17 same plus 15/16 v A Rh, 17 v A Rs; 2D S17 11 v A Dh; 2D H17 A,7 v 2 Ds; 4-8D S17 A,2 v 5 Dh.
- Engine charts from my C# harness (EngChart.exe, compiled from src/Engine, DAS, SP4, LS, peek): engine shows H/S/H at 1D S17 A2v4/A8v6/16vA, H at 2D S17 11vA, S at 2D H17 A7v2, H at 8D S17 A2v5. No hard/soft deviations in 1D H17, 6D S17 or 6D H17.
- Rust engine (my own crate rustflags against web/engine): flags, cell EVs and exact_off_top identical to C# for every named cell (e.g. 2D S17 11vA H -0.211227, D -0.215494; ExactOffTop -0.145238%).
- Independent exact CA (ExactCA.cs, explicit hole-card posterior, exact removal, best play after each hit). Named cells: 2D S17 11vA H -0.209957, D -0.207763 (double by 0.00219); 1D S17 A2v4 H 0.110213, D 0.115097; A8v6 S 0.482354, D 0.482626; 16vA H -0.659386 vs R -0.655672 (no pairs: -0.659352 vs -0.655102); 2D H17 A7v2 S 0.118882, D 0.119481; 8D S17 A2v5 H 0.136331, D 0.136360. All match the finding to 5 decimals.
- Published per-hand EVs (Wizard Appendix 9, conditional on no dealer BJ) reproduced by my code to 6 decimals: 2D S17 A v 9,2 / 8,3 / 7,4 / 6,5 and A v 7,A (-0.100502 stand, -0.100359 hit); 1D S17 4 v 2,A, 6 v 8,A, A v 10,6, A v 9,7, A v 8,8; 2D H17 2 v 7,A; 8D S17 5 v 2,A. The engine's converted 2D S17 A,7 v A hit is -0.097297, 0.0031 off the published value.
- Wizard Appendix 3B (2D S17 composition-dependent exceptions) lists '11 (9+2) vs Ace = Hit' and '11 (8+3) vs Ace = Hit', which implies a total-dependent double, as my per-hand values show.
- Full exact charts (hard 5-17, soft 13-20, all upcards, LS) for 1D S17, 1D H17, 2D S17, 2D H17 and 8D S17 match the published Wizard charts in all 210 cells each. Engine vs exact differs only in the named cells. Excluding pairs from the hard-total weights changes nothing.
- Largest per-cell error, engine minus exact: hit/double 1D S17 0.010948/0.020260; 1D H17 0.010868/0.017588; 2D S17 0.005152/0.009643; 2D H17 0.005092/0.008303; 6D S17 0.001656/0.003115; 6D H17 0.001634/0.002666; 8D S17 0.001237/0.002327. Stand errors are 0. This confirms 0.011/0.020 in 1D, 0.010 in 2D and 0.003 in 6D.
- Money effect via the engine's own ExactOffTop with one cell changed: 1D S17 LS A2v4 +0.000559%, A8v6 +0.000042%, 16vA R +0.002051%, all three together +0.002653% (0.158690% to 0.161343%; the Game page shows +0.159% as 'perfect basic strategy'); 2D S17 11vA +0.000831%; 2D H17 A7v2 +0.000145%; 8D S17 A2v5 +0.000025%.
- Pair cells outside the finding (SplitEV uses the same fixed odds): 2D S17/H17 DAS 6,6 v 7 split +0.000342% and 7,7 v 8 split +0.000102%; 1D H17 DAS 9,9 v A split +0.000077%. Published Appendix 9 agrees (6,6v7 split -0.229024 vs hit -0.237102; 7,7v8 -0.387476 vs -0.389533; 9,9vA -0.182862 vs stand -0.186131) and the charts show Ph/Ps. The engine shows no split. Exception: 2D H17 8,8 v A, where Wizard shows Rp but the engine's ExactOffTop prefers splitting (surrendering costs -0.000190%), so it is not counted as an engine error.
- Index replication (IdxCheck.cs: engine BinMoments and engine Regress via reflection, my exact method on fractional compositions). 1D S17 Hi-Lo LS, engine/exact first bin: 10vA +3/+2, 11vA 0/-1, 12v4 +1/+2, 12v5 -1/0, 16vT S +3/+2, 14vT R +4/+3, 15vA R +1/0, A7v2 +2/+1; 9v2 and 15vT R unchanged. 2D S17: 10vA +4/+3, 11vA +1/0, 16vT +2/+1, 14vT +4/+3. 6D H17: only 11vA 0/-1 of the 10 plays checked. All agree with the earlier idx logs.
- Size of the index shift: exact margins at the misplayed bin in 1D S17 are 11vA bin -1 +0.0078, 10vA bin +2 +0.0128, 12v4 bin +1 -0.0022, 16vT bin +2 +0.0017, 15vA R bin 0 +0.0024 units per hand affected. My rough estimate (not computed) is a few thousandths of a percent per round in total.
- Betting correlation: Hi-Lo BC recomputed from the earlier reviewer's 1D EORs is 0.9659 (engine) vs 0.9691 (exact), a difference of -0.0032. The Count page shows it to 3 decimals. I did not recompute the EORs themselves.
- Display and documentation: StrategyChart shows pt.Basic flags and tooltip 'EV per unit' from CellEV to 3 decimals. The Game page says 'perfect basic-strategy player' (3-decimal %). README and Guide promise 'optimal hit/stand/double/split' and 'optimal hitting, doubling, splitting'. Only code comments mention the fixed draw odds (as a 0.04% PlayerEV bias); the peek conditioning is undocumented.
- Not verified: the fix's '45-70% of A/T error removed by conditional draw odds' and its '0.3-1.5 s per game' runtime. My unoptimised single-thread exact chart took about 1.8 s (1D) to 7 s (2D/8D). The conditional formula P(T|no BJ) = c_T/(n-1) for an ace up is correct.

### m1 (simulator#1) → partly (minor)

Corrected claim: [minor] Analysis.Bins (C# and Rust) uses the average of seat A (the last seat, which acts last) and seat B (the seat beside it, which acts just before A) for every ramp row. The app, however, says the player sits in the last seat and uses the seat beside it only for 2-3 hands. Seat A's index decisions also see the cards B drew. One-hand rows are therefore understated by (A-B)/2 per hand: | Game (2 spots, Hi-Lo) | A-B per hand | SCORE understated, 1/2/3/4 ramp at TC <=0/1/2/>=3 | |---|---|---| | 1D H17 noDAS, full indices | +0.027% ± 0.002% | 1.6% ± 0.14% (131.8 vs 133.9); 0.9% for a 1-12 ramp | | 1D H17 noDAS, I18+Fab4 | +0.013% ± 0.002% | 1.0% | | 2D H17 DAS LS, I18+Fab4 | +0.004% ± 0.0015% | 0.5% | | 6D S17 DAS LS, I18+Fab4 | no significant effect (+0.001% ± 0.0014%) | — | - Basic-strategy play shows no seat difference, so the gap comes only from the information A has. - Two-hand rows (A+B) are exact. - Three-hand rows (3·avg(A,B)) probably overstate by a similar small amount, but this is unmeasured: no third seat is tallied, and with 2 spots there is no third seat at all. - The bias is conservative, about one standard error of a default 200M-round run, and limited to 1-2 deck index play. Fix: use seat A's own mean and M2 for one-hand rows and A+B for two-hand rows, or change the UI text to say that one-hand results are the average of the last two seats.

- Read the '### simulator#1' section of C:\Users\bene\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\dc31a4ef150e96e7\f8128c32-48f7-4dc1-9bfc-570a66aede8c\scratchpad\mathcheck\lead\findings-round3.md and the reviewer's seatse code and output (agents/simulator/mine/src/checks.rs:1292, r3_seatse.txt).
- Simulator.cs:366-367 and sim.rs:372-373: spotA = spots-1 (last seat), spotB = spots-2. Record() (Simulator.cs:577-586, sim.rs:658-669) tallies result[spotA] into SA/QA and result[spotB] into SB/QB/AB under the bet bin taken before the deal (betBin), which is the same for both seats.
- Play order: the seat loop runs s = 0..S-1 (PlaySpot at Simulator.cs:641, sim.rs:750), so A acts last (third base), right after B.
- Count at decision time: Draw() adds each face-up card's tag to rc at once (first two cards of every seat, the up card, every hit/double/split card). Fl()/Bin() read the live rc and the cards remaining. The peek hole card is drawn hidden and counted only after play. A therefore sees B's drawn cards and B never sees A's. Insurance and early surrender happen before any draw (same information for both seats). At-turn late surrender uses the count at the seat's turn.
- Analysis.Bins (Analysis.cs:151-158, analysis.rs:189-196): with Spots >= 2, Mean = (SA+SB)/(40n) and M2 = (QA+QB)/(800n) for every bin. Evaluate: e = h*u*Mean and q = u^2(h*M2 + h(h-1)*Cov), so 1 hand gives avg(A,B), 2 hands give A+B exactly, 3 hands give 3*avg(A,B) with no third seat tallied.
- UI text: GamePage.cs:163 and GamePage.svelte:160 say 'You play the last seat (third base); the seat beside it is used when your ramp calls for two or three hands.' The Guide and SimPage (C# and Svelte) say 'your seat's results are stored' and 'covariance to the neighbouring seat'. Only the developer comment in web/app/src/lib/engine/types.ts:91 mentions 'the last two seats'. The averaging is never disclosed to the user.
- Own Rust crate C:\Users\bene\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\dc31a4ef150e96e7\f8128c32-48f7-4dc1-9bfc-570a66aede8c\scratchpad\verify_sim1\src\main.rs on calcbj-engine. Seeds differ from the reviewer's (bases 0x5EEDA11C..., 0x5EEDB22D..., ...). 64 independent batches x 1e8 rounds, 8 threads. Seat A only is evaluated with spots=1, seat B only with SA:=SB, QA:=QB, spots=1. Paired SE comes from the spread between batches and was cross-checked with the iid SE from QA+QB-2AB (they agreed: 0.0017% vs 0.0016%). Outputs: run1.txt and run2.txt in the same folder.
- 1D H17 noDAS noSurr pen .75, 2 spots, Hi-Lo full indices, 6.4e9 rounds: A 0.2517%, B 0.2247%, A-B = +0.0269% ± 0.0017% per hand (z 15.7). The shipped analysis understates EV per hand by 0.0135% ± 0.0009% (the reviewer had 0.0103% ± 0.0031%; consistent at 1 sigma).
- Same game, ramp 1/2/3/4 at TC <=0/1/2/>=3: SCORE shipped 131.82 vs seat A alone 133.93, a gap of +2.12 ± 0.18 (+1.61% ± 0.14%; the reviewer had +1.55% ± 0.47%). EV per round 0.02900 vs 0.02922 (+0.76% ± 0.07%). SCORE A alone minus B alone: +4.21 ± 0.36. Ramp 1-2-4-6-8-12: SCORE +0.90% ± 0.10%.
- 6D S17 DAS LS pen .75, 2 spots, Hi-Lo I18+Fab4, 6.4e9 rounds: A -0.2526%, B -0.2538%, A-B = +0.0012% ± 0.0014% (z 0.8). Ramp 1-4: SCORE 9.23 vs 9.28 (+0.50% ± 0.37%). Ramp 1-12: 29.78 vs 29.84 (+0.19% ± 0.21%). No significant effect.
- Control, 1D H17 noDAS with basic strategy only, 3.2e9 rounds: A-B = -0.0011% ± 0.0024% (z -0.5); ramp 1-4 SCORE -0.32% ± 0.33%. There is no positional artifact; the gap comes only from index play using B's cards.
- Extra, 1D H17 noDAS 2 spots I18+Fab4, 6.4e9 rounds: A-B = +0.0134% ± 0.0018% (z 7.6); ramp 1-4 SCORE 101.42 vs 102.43 (+1.00% ± 0.14%). This contradicts the finding's 'no significant effect in 1D with I18+Fab4' (the reviewer's -0.0003% ± 0.0033% is about 2 sigma low).
- Extra, 2D H17 DAS LS 2 spots I18+Fab4, 6.4e9 rounds: A-B = +0.0044% ± 0.0015% (z 2.9); A-avg = +0.0022% ± 0.0008%, against the finding's +0.0073% ± 0.0018%. The finding's SE is too small: the iid SE for 4e8 rounds is about 0.0032%. SCORE ramp 1-4: +0.52% ± 0.17%; ramp 1-12: +0.36% ± 0.11%.
- Severity context: the SCORE SD per 1e8-round batch is 1.44% (1D, 1-4 ramp), so about 1.0% at the default AppState.SimRounds = 200,000,000. The 1.6% bias is about 1.6 standard errors of a default run. It is systematic, conservative (understates), zero in the default 6-deck game, and 0.005-0.014% EV per hand, comparable to items the same file rates minor. Two-hand rows are exact.
- Three-hand claim: not measurable with the engine, because only two seats are tallied. It is plausible for spots >= 3 by the same mechanism, with a size of about half of A-B per hand. With 2 spots a third hand has no seat at all. Marked as unverified.

### M3 (offtop#1) → confirmed (major)

Corrected claim: The claim holds as stated, with three refinements. 1) Size of the gain. Changing 16 v T from surrender/else-hit to surrender/else-stand raises the exact off-the-top EV by: - 1D: +0.016007% (LS peek, S17 or H17, also with RSA), +0.020103% (LS ENHC), +0.015091% (ES) - 2D: +0.006724% - 4D: +0.002783% - 6D: +0.001512% (LS), +0.002058% (ENHC), +0.001506% (ES) - 8D: +0.000884% 2) Which hands use the bit. Almost all of the gain comes from non-split 3-card and 4-card hard 16s (1D: 3-card +0.0082%, 4-card +0.0061%, 5-card +0.0015%). Split hands add only about +0.0001% in 1D and about 0 in 6D. Judged per multiset of player cards, which is what the player knows, standing is better for 80-90% of the reach in 1D and 65-83% in 6D. Hands holding 6s through 9s (2-6-8, A-6-9, 4-6-6, 3-6-7) are the exceptions and still prefer hitting. 3) Only 16 v T is affected. For every other surrender cell, the current fallback is already the better one; flipping it costs EV: - 15 v T: -0.054% - 15 v A: -0.024% - 16 v 9: -0.014% - 16 v A: -0.015% - 17 v A (H17): -0.022% - ES cells 12-17 v A and 14 v T: also negative No other hard 12-18 or soft 13-20 cell gains from a flip in 1D S17 LS, 6D H17 LS or 1D S17 NS. Without surrender, hitting 16 v T stays right (flipping costs 0.034% in 1D and 0.007% in 6D). User impact: - Headline for 1D H17 DAS LS (US peek and OBO): "house edge 0.012%" becomes "+0.004%" player edge. The sign changes. - Default 6D H17 DAS LS page: headline 0.530% becomes 0.528%. - Its '1 deck' row: -0.012% becomes +0.004% (effect +0.517% becomes +0.532%). - Its '2 decks' effect: +0.201% becomes +0.206%. - Its 'No surrender' effect: -0.088% becomes -0.090%. - 1D S17 DAS NS page: 'Late surrender' effect +0.016% becomes +0.032%; 'Early surrender' +0.614% becomes +0.629%. - Chart: 'R' should read 'Rs' for 16 v T in every LS/ES game. The proposed fix is correct, and in practice it changes only 16 v T.

- Code read: Combinatorial.cs:366-401 sets Flag.Stand from two-card S >= H even when Flag.Surrender wins. Play() (526-542), SplitHand() (571-583) and ExactOffTop() (666-731) use that bit. ca.rs:519-527 has the same logic. Simulator.cs:536's hit loop reads the stand bit. StrategyChart.cs:191-192 shows 'R' = 'Surrender, otherwise hit' vs 'Rs' = 'otherwise stand'. HouseEdgeOffTop feeds GamePage.cs:178/219 (3-decimal %).
- Engine C# ExactOffTop with only 16 v T's stand bit XORed. My own FlipScan.cs is compiled with the engine sources and changes CAResult.Flags directly (no reflection needed): 1D S17 DAS LS SP4 0.158690% -> 0.174697% (+0.016007%); 6D H17 DAS LS SP4 -0.529678% -> -0.528166% (+0.001512%). HouseEdgeOffTop equals ExactOffTop in every game. Insure = false.
- Two-card 16 v T CellEV matches the finding: 1D S -0.57414, H -0.54686, R -0.54082; 6D S -0.57557, H -0.57076, R -0.53883.
- Same flip in other games, all matching the finding: 1D H17 DAS LS -0.012188% -> +0.003819% (sign change); OBO identical; 1D LS ENHC +0.020103%; 1D ES +0.015091%; 1D RSA +0.016007%; 2D H17 and S17 +0.006724%; 4D H17 +0.002783%; 6D S17 +0.001512%; 6D ES +0.001506%; 6D ENHC +0.002058%; 8D H17 +0.000884%.
- Rust port, via a scratch crate on calcbj-engine (CARGO_TARGET_DIR in scratch): 16 v T flags = 4 (surrender, else hit). 6D H17 LS -0.529678% -> -0.528166%; 1D H17 LS -0.012188% -> 0.003819%; 1D S17 LS 0.158690% -> 0.174697%, identical to C#. The web/Tauri Game page is affected the same way.
- Independent exact recursion (my own Indep.cs, no engine code): hole card dealt first, conditioned on no dealer blackjack, full removal, non-split hands only. 1D S17 LS non-split gain +0.015933% (engine total +0.016007%); 6D H17 LS +0.001505% (engine +0.001512%). 1D H17 gives the same +0.015933%.
- Independent paired shuffle-every-round simulation (own game code, same card order for both strategies, real removal including splits), 8e9 rounds each. 1D S17 DAS LS: EV(A) 0.15898% +- 0.00129% (exact 0.158690%), B-A +0.016171% +- 0.000186% (87 SE), split rounds +0.000108%. 6D H17 DAS LS: EV(A) -0.53146% +- 0.00128% (exact -0.529678%), B-A +0.001448% +- 0.000177% (8.2 SE).
- Which hands use the bit, 1D S17 LS, reach per round: 3-card 1.318%, 4-card 0.389%, 5-card 0.055%, 6+ cards 0.005%; split rounds 0.011%. Gain by size: 3-card +0.00817%, 4-card +0.00612%, 5-card +0.00147%, 6+ +0.00017%. 6D: reach 1.141/0.381/0.062%, gain +0.00049/+0.00076/+0.00023%.
- Stand vs hit per player-card multiset (averaged over hole cards): stand better for 79.9/90.5/86.5% of 3/4/5-card reach in 1D and 67.4/65.0/82.9% in 6D. Per-hand values, 1D: 2-4-T +0.0043, A-5-T +0.0136, 4-5-7 +0.0467, 3-5-8 +0.0292; hit better for 2-6-8 -0.0211, A-6-9 -0.0257, 4-6-6 -0.0229, 3-6-7 -0.0082. A first per-hole-card tally (41%) used the wrong granularity and was redone per multiset.
- Other surrender cells, engine flips across 16 games: all negative except 16 v T. 6D H17 LS: 15vA -0.023865, 15vT -0.054112, 16vA -0.014868, 16v9 -0.013888, 17vA -0.022474. 1D H17 LS: 15vA -0.016234, 16vA -0.004804, 17vA -0.034172. ES cells 12-17 v A and 14 v T: negative or 0.
- Other surrender cells, independent recursion agrees: 6D 15vT -0.052684 (non-split), 15vA -0.023865, 16v9 -0.013441, 16vA -0.014868, 17vA -0.022474; 1D H17 15vA -0.015751, 16vA -0.004759, 17vA -0.033581; 6D ES 14vT -0.089741, 12vA -0.016764.
- Full scan flipping every hard 12-18 and soft 13-20 cell, all upcards, including double cells, in 1D S17 LS, 6D H17 LS and 1D S17 NS: no positive flip other than 16 v T. Without surrender, hitting 16 v T is right: flip -0.033904% (1D S17 NS), -0.007333% (6D H17 NS).
- Game page impact, from the GamePage.cs formulas and the numbers above: 1D H17 DAS LS headline 'house edge 0.012%' becomes '+0.004%' player edge. Default 6D H17 DAS LS headline 0.530% -> 0.528%; '1 deck' row -0.012% -> +0.004% (effect +0.517% -> +0.532%); '2 decks' effect +0.201% -> +0.206%; 'No surrender' -0.088% -> -0.090%; S17 and ES rows unchanged. 1D S17 DAS NS: 'Late surrender' +0.016% -> +0.032%, 'Early surrender' +0.614% -> +0.629%.
- Not verified numerically: the per-count tables (Strategy.cs:291 eng.Evaluate per bin) and their interaction with the 16 v T index. They use the same pass 1, so the issue is plausible there too.

## Follow-up test: depth-money-test → mixed (minor)

The critic found a real gap, but switching lists is worth little money, and whether it helps depends on the game. BinMoments assumes decisions spread evenly over k = 3..round(N*pen). That ignores seat A's position, the capped cut card and the round finished past the cut. G1 (1D H17 DOA noDAS SP2, 3 spots, pen 75%, cut 35): the reweighted list is right on 48 of the 60 hard/soft stand/double/surrender lines it changes. On the lines in the task it lands exactly on the Monte-Carlo crossing for two-card hands, which is what the engine's CA models: 16vT +2, 16vA +2, 16v9 +6, 15v9 +6, 17vA hit at -6. The current list is right on 12v3, 8v6, 9v7, 9v8, 11vA (doubles) and a few soft doubles. G2 (1D S17 DAS SP4 LS, heads-up, pen 80%, cut 38): the current list is right on 19 of 31 lines. Most of its money comes from 16vT (and 16vA). There, two-card 16s are surrendered, so the stand bit is consulted only by multi-card 16s. For those hands the true crossing is about 0, so both lists (+2 and +3) are too high and the current one is simply nearer. On the lines decided by two-card hands (15v9 and 13vT surrender, 15v9 stand), the reweighted list is right again. Money is small in both games. Seat A's own decisions give +0.027 per 100 hands in G1 and -0.0035 in G2. The engine's own simulator, end to end, gives +0.046 and -0.011, which is +7% and -1.5% of what all the index plays together are worth. So this is not a material error, and the critic's reweighting is not better in every game. It is worth fixing for multi-spot games, preferably with a depth model taken from the real deal. A bigger structural problem showed up as a side finding. CAEngine.Evaluate pass 1 builds each hard/soft cell's flags from two-card hands only, but the simulator uses those flags for multi-card and split hands too. Counting every hand seat A really plays, the true 16vT crossing is about 0 in both games, against tables at +2/+3, and the 16v9 crossing in G1 is +4, against +6/+7. The insurance index is the same in both lists in both games, so it was not tested. Outputs: g1b.txt (G1, 60M rounds), g2.txt (G2, 120M rounds) and list.txt, all in the same folder.

Money is per 100 hands at a 1-unit flat bet. 'Right' means right at the bins where the two lists disagree.

It loses at the common even bins 6 (-0.0120+-0.0005), 8 (-0.0080+-0.0007) and 10 (-0.0045+-0.0011) | at the disputed bin +5 CURRENT right (z 6), but both lists are wrong at 6..10 | -0.000003/100
- G2 15v9 stand | current +6 | reweighted +7 | MC +7: bin 6 -0.0078+-0.0004, bin 8 +0.0086+-0.0005 | REWEIGHTED right (z 18) | +0.0002/100. G2 13vT surrender | current +8 and +10 up (not +9) | reweighted +10 | MC +9 | REWEIGHTED right at +8 (z 67) | +0.0013/100. G2 13v6 stand | current -3 | reweighted -4 | MC -3 | CURRENT right (z 17) | -0.0005/100
- G2 all 31 changed lines: current right on 19 (2 insignificant), reweighted right on 9, 3 never decided. Total -0.0035+-0.0001/100. Leaving out the three stand lines consulted only by multi-card hands (16vT, 16vA, 15vT), the reweighted list gains +0.0014/100.

Money: Value of switching to the reweighted list, at a 1-unit flat bet, per 100 hands. Seat A's own decisions (sum over lines of frequency x mean exact EV difference at the disputed bins): G1 +0.0266+-0.0002 (60 hard/soft lines); G2 -0.0035+-0.0001 (31 lines). In G1, 16vT alone is +0.0083; in G2, 16vT alone is -0.0049. End to end with the engine's own Simulator, where every seat switches and split and pair-surrender lines are included (2.4e9 rounds per table in G1, 3.6e9 in G2): - G1: reweighted minus current = +0.046+-0.003. Hard/soft cells alone +0.039+-0.002; pair cells alone +0.007+-0.003. - G2: -0.0105+-0.0023. Hard/soft cells alone -0.0089+-0.0019; pair cells alone -0.006+-0.002. Both methods give the same sign in both games. The engine's figures are 1.5-2.5x larger, and part of that gap is not explained. Likely causes: the other seats switch too, changed plays consume a different number of cards (round count and depth effects), and the probe assumes the best continuation after a hit rather than the table's. Scale: all index plays together are worth +0.677 (G1) and +0.701 (G2) per 100 hands over basic strategy. Switching therefore changes that value by about +7% in G1 and -1.5% in G2. Flat-bet EV moves from 0.321 to 0.366 in G1 and from 0.725 to 0.714 in G2. Side finding: a crossing taken over all consults, multi-card hands included, would be worth about +0.0045 for 16vT alone in G2 (stand at 0 instead of +2) and +0.002 in G1.

Checks:
- Reproduced the critic's tables with its own Depth.cs code (DepthDist on 1M rounds, same seed, same order of the two games). G1: 118 vs 137 plays, 60 differing index lines. G2: 151 vs 142 plays, 43 differing. All 10 example lines in the task reproduced exactly. The insurance index is identical in both lists in both games.
- Checked the exact EV module (dealer terminal multisets with removal, peek conditioning that also covers the player's own draws) against brute-force outcome Monte Carlo: 7 cases (full and depleted 1D compositions, H17/S17, upcards T, A and 9), 16M deals each. Stand/hit differences all within |z| <= 1.7; doubles agree to about 3e-4.
- Replicated SimWorker.PlayRound: burn card, Rules.CutCard (35 in G1, 38 in G2), last round finished past the cut, seats 0..S-1 with seat A last, US peek, late surrender at the player's turn, insurance on Bin() before the peek, split/DAS/HSA rules. The bin comes from Simulator.MetricValue with cards remaining = N - pos. The replica deals 3.60 rounds per shoe in G1 and 7.23 in G2.
- Probe: every seat-A consult of a cell whose Stand/Double/Surrender bit differs (two-card, multi-card and split hands), within 5 bins either side of the disagreement. The unseen set is burn + hole + undealt cards; ties scored by exact EV. G1: 60M rounds, 6.9M exact evaluations. G2: 120M rounds, 8.2M evaluations. SEs from 96 batches. Guarded the 0/0 case in a nearly empty shoe; 0 non-finite results after the fix.
- Ran the engine's own Simulator end to end with the current, reweighted, basic-only and two hybrid tables (hard/soft cells swapped, pair cells swapped). Current and the hard/soft hybrid were repeated with fresh seeds. The signs agree with the probe in both games.
- Source check: CAEngine.Evaluate pass 1 (Combinatorial.cs:310-401) sets the hard/soft cell flags from two-card hands only, and SimWorker.PlayHand applies them to multi-card and split hands as well. This is why the all-consults crossings for 16vT (G1 and G2) and 16v9 (G1) sit below both lists. In LS games, 16vT/15vT/16vA stand bits are consulted only by multi-card hands.
- 1D half-deck estimation side effect: odd bins occur only in the first round (40 or more cards left), even bins deep in the shoe. Some cells therefore end up with non-monotone flag patterns, and some bins mix very different depths (example: 14vA surrender in G2).

## Completeness critic

The 16 area reviews computed nearly every formula in the engine, both apps and the WASM ops. I found no formula that was never computed apart from short web page formulas. The largest remaining gap is a model assumption in index generation. BinMoments spreads decisions evenly over 3..round(N·pen) seen cards. The simulator caps the cut card (CutCard reserve), deals the last round past the cut, and with several spots seat A decides only after 2·spots+2 cards. This had been verified only for 6D heads-up. My check reweighted the engine's own bin moments with the seat-A decision depths of the simulator's way of dealing; with uniform weights it reproduces the engine exactly. In 1D it changes 43-60 of about 120-150 Hi-Lo index lines, including 16vT, 16vA, 15vT and 14vA surrender. In 2D it changes 23 lines and in 6D 3. This is as large as the confirmed CA shortcuts in 1-2 decks. A second, concrete defect class: the Windows app's numeric boxes truncate typed fractions with (int) while showing them rounded (spots '2.6' shows 3, simulates 2), and read a decimal comma as a thousands separator (penetration '72,5' becomes 95%, Kelly '0,5' becomes 2.00); only the IRC field had been checked. I closed the 'other surrender/double stand bits' item: only 16 v T gains, in 6 games. Still unchecked: pair-split indices under the count model, the heads-up 2-3 hands model, per-count 16vT fallback bins, emergency-reshuffle IRC handling, and the risk formulas against real outcome distributions (my analytic estimate: negligible). Scratch harnesses are in C:\Users\bene\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\dc31a4ef150e96e7\f8128c32-48f7-4dc1-9bfc-570a66aede8c\scratchpad\mathcheck\critic (Crit.cs, Depth.cs, NudTyped.cs). No repository files were changed.

- [high] **Index generation assumes decisions are spread evenly over 3..round(N·pen) seen cards, which does not match the depths at which the simulator really deals decisions** (src/Engine/Strategy.cs:104-108 (MidDepthDecks), :133-181 (BinMoments, last = round(N·pen) at :152), unreached-bin fallback :283-290; web/engine/src/strategy.rs:119, :323-380 (last at :340). Compare src/Engine/Types.cs:70-79 (CutCard reserve min(N/3, 8+6·spots)) and Simulator.cs:330. MidDepthDecks also feeds src/App/AppState.cs:95-106 (MetricFor: ramp and index ranges, RC presets, RampForRules) and app.rs.): The depth distribution decides which unseen composition each count bin gets, so it drives every per-count play and insurance decision. It was only verified for 6D S17 heads-up (indices review). Rules listed 'nominal penetration vs reserved cut card' as not-checked, and indices had 'Depth range k=3..round(N·pen), pen 0' as a code-reading concern only. In 1-2 decks the cut card is capped: 1D with 2+ spots cuts at 35/52 for any pen ≥ 67.3%. The last round is also dealt past the cut, and with several spots seat A never decides before 2·spots+2 cards. Strategy/Rules keys have no spots, although the depths depend on spots. Check: RAN two checks (sources in C:\Users\bene\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\dc31a4ef150e96e7\f8128c32-48f7-4dc1-9bfc-570a66aede8c\scratchpad\mathcheck\critic\Crit.cs and Depth.cs, compiled with src/Engine). (a) Hi-Lo full indices (-10..+10) rebuilt with pen set to the capped cut card: 25 of 118 index lines change in 1D H17 noDAS with 2-3 spots (last 39→35). 52 of 151 change in 1D S17 LS heads-up at pen 80% (42→38: 16vT stand +2→+3, 16vA +6→+7, 15v9 R +2→+3). 2D pen 85% with 3 spots: 30/133. 6D pen 90% with 7 spots: 14/129 (15vA stand +5→+6, 14vA R +5→+4). KO 1D: 67/201. (b) Dealt 1M rounds the way the simulator does (burn, CutCard, last round finished, seat A last, simple hit/stand play) and recorded the depth of seat A's first decision. Then reweighted the engine's own BinMoments with that distribution. - [low] **Risk and Kelly formulas never tested against the real (skewed, discrete) per-round outcome distribution** (src/Engine/Analysis.cs (RorForBankroll/BankrollForRor, TripRuin, KellyUnit, certainty equivalent); web/engine/src/analysis.rs; RiskPage.cs / RiskPage.svelte): Every ROR, trip-ruin and Kelly check used Gaussian random walks or derivations. None used blackjack outcomes (naturals, doubles, splits, overshoot at ruin). The analysis review lists 'exact log-utility Kelly/CE vs second-order' as not-checked. Check: Analytic estimate only. Adjustment coefficient R ≈ 2μ/E[W²]·(1 + R·E[W³]/(3E[W²])). For a 1-12 ramp (E[W²] ≈ 21, E[W³] ≈ 24 units³ with per-hand E[x³] ≈ +0.17) the correction is ≈ 0.1% of the exponent. The overshoot at ruin is a few units against B ≈ 1000, about 0.5%. So a displayed 5% ROR is ≈ 4.9-5.0%: likely negligible. A bankroll-path MC driven by real engine rounds would settle it.
